### [1] [T-embeddings as stationary measures on T-graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24180)
*Mikhail Basok,Benoit Laslier,Marianna Russkikh*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a classification of t-embeddings associated with a given T-graph via positive stationary measures on the T-graph, and provides a reconstruction procedure; it further applies this to construct t-embeddings for general sufficiently non-degenerate planar bipartite graphs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and classify t-embeddings in relation to T-graphs for weighted planar bipartite graphs, and to enable systematic construction of t-embeddings beyond special cases.

Method: The authors use measure-theoretic and combinatorial analysis on T-graphs, proving a bijection between t-embeddings and positive stationary measures, and devise an explicit reconstruction algorithm from such measures.

Result: A complete classification of t-embeddings by positive stationary measures on the corresponding T-graph; a constructive procedure to recover the t-embedding from a given measure; and a general method to produce t-embeddings for arbitrary sufficiently non-degenerate planar bipartite graphs.

Conclusion: T-embeddings are intrinsically governed by stationary measures on T-graphs, enabling both theoretical classification and practical construction across broad classes of planar bipartite graphs.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the relation between t-embeddings and T-graphs associated with the same weighted planar bipartite graph. We prove that, given such a T-graph, the corresponding t-embeddings are classified by positive stationary measures on this T-graph and describe a reconstruction procedure of the t-embedding given such a measure. As an application, we develop an approach for constructing t-embeddings of arbitrary sufficiently non-degenerate planar bipartite graphs.

</details>


### [2] [An Accurate Zeroth-Order Perturbation Theory for Solving Power-Law Potentials within the Framework of the Asymptotic Iteration Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24805)
*A. J. Sous,Ibsal Assi,Nasser Saad*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a zeroth-order perturbative variant of the asymptotic iteration method (AIM) using exponential/hyperbolic ansätze to solve the Schrödinger equation for spherically symmetric singular potentials—avoiding oscillatory AIM failures and yielding accurate results for quartic/sextic singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard asymptotic iteration method (AIM) exhibits oscillatory, non-convergent behavior when applied to spherically symmetric singular potentials; a robust alternative is needed for atomic/molecular physics applications.

Method: Develops a zeroth-order perturbation theory within AIM framework: substitutes exponential or hyperbolic potential ansätze with tunable small free parameters; treats the residual difference between original and ansatz potential as a small perturbation; solves resulting wave equation analytically at zeroth order.

Result: Achieves stable, non-oscillatory solutions for singular potentials (e.g., quartic and sextic); numerical eigenvalues and wavefunctions show good agreement with established methods.

Conclusion: The proposed perturbative AIM variant overcomes intrinsic instability of standard AIM for singular potentials, offering an accurate, analytically tractable, and numerically reliable approach for bound-state quantum problems with spherical symmetry.

Abstract: Spherically symmetric singular potentials have a wide range of applications, such as molecular and atomic physics. In this work, we present a zeroth-order perturbation theory for solving the Schrödinger equation within the framework of the asymptotic iteration method (AIM). We rely on exponential and hyperbolic potential ansatz substitutions, where the difference between the original and new potential is treated as a small perturbation. Each potential ansatz has a free parameter chosen small enough to give an accurate zeroth-order perturbative wave equation solution. This method avoids the oscillatory behavior observed in the asymptotic iteration solutions when applying the AIM to such problems. Finally, we illustrate our approach by solving a few problems, particularly potentials with quartic and sextic singularities. We compare our results with those obtained via other methods and find good agreement.

</details>


### [3] [Eynard-Orantin Topological Recursion, Moduli-Space Virasoro Constraints, and Semisimple CohFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24980)
*Dingkang Xiong,Jian Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses Virasoro constraints to prove Eynard’s intersection-number formula for topological recursion with multiple simple ramification points, links the quadratic differential operator (built from the Bergman kernel) to Givental’s quantized R-matrix, and extends the DOSS correspondence by showing that varying the y-function corresponds to shifting the evaluation point of the ancestor potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a Virasoro-theoretic proof of Eynard's intersection-number formula and to deepen the understanding of the DOSS identification between topological recursion and Givental's formalism for semisimple cohomological field theories.

Method: Application of Virasoro constraints; coupling of higher Weil-Petersson partition functions via a quadratic differential operator derived from the Bergman kernel; identification of this operator with the quadratic part of Givental's quantized R-matrix; analysis of the effect of varying the y-function while fixing the Bergman kernel/R-matrix.

Result: A rigorous Virasoro-based proof of Eynard's formula; explicit identification of the Bergman-kernel-derived quadratic operator with Givental's R-matrix quadratic part; demonstration that varying y (with fixed R-matrix) corresponds to translation of the ancestor potential's evaluation point.

Conclusion: The paper unifies Virasoro constraints, topological recursion, and Givental theory by showing their structural equivalence at the level of quadratic operators and ancestor potentials, and generalizes the DOSS correspondence beyond a fixed y-function.

Abstract: We use Virasoro constraints to prove Eynard's intersection-number formula for Eynard-Orantin topological recursion with several simple ramification points. The key construction couples the higher Weil-Petersson partition functions associated with the individual ramification points through a quadratic differential operator determined by the Bergman kernel. We identify this operator with the quadratic part of Givental's quantized $R$-matrix, thereby obtaining a Virasoro-theoretic proof of the DOSS identification of topological recursion with Givental's formula for semisimple cohomological field theories. Furthermore, while the DOSS identification fixes the distinguished $y$-function, we keep the Bergman kernel/$R$-matrix fixed and allow $y$ to vary, showing that this amounts to shifting the evaluation point of the corresponding ancestor potential.

</details>


### [4] [Spectral Curves for Semisimple Conformal Frobenius Manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24985)
*Dingkang Xiong,Jian Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Constructs spectral curves for semisimple conformal Frobenius manifolds with flat unit via periods of the second structure equation; Eynard-Orantin topological recursion on these curves recovers Givental-Teleman ancestor correlators; applied to quantum cohomology of ℙⁿ, yielding explicit data in terms of Hecke triangle groups and hypergeometric functions; for ℙ² and ℙ³, full spectral curve data—including bidifferentials—is expressed in classical modular and quasimodular forms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a geometric–analytic bridge between semisimple conformal Frobenius manifolds (e.g., quantum cohomologies) and topological recursion, enabling reconstruction of Givental-Teleman ancestor correlators from spectral curve data.

Method: Uses periods of the second structure connection (Dubrovin's flatness condition) to define connected spectral curves on simply connected domains in ℂ; applies Eynard-Orantin topological recursion; computes explicit spectral data for ℙⁿ via monodromy representations linked to Hecke triangle groups and hypergeometric solutions; expresses bidifferentials for ℙ², ℙ³ using modular and quasimodular forms.

Result: Spectral curves are explicitly constructed for ℙⁿ (n ≥ 2) at a point; topological recursion on them reproduces all stable ancestor correlators; for ℙ² and ℙ³, the full spectral curve—including Bergman kernel (bidifferential)—is given in closed form using classical modular and quasimodular forms.

Conclusion: The period-based spectral curve construction provides a canonical, recursion-compatible framework for semisimple conformal Frobenius manifolds with flat unit, unifying quantum cohomology, hypergeometric geometry, and modular structures under topological recursion.

Abstract: We construct connected spectral curves for semisimple conformal Frobenius manifolds with flat unit, using the periods of the second structure equation. Eynard-Orantin topological recursion on these curves reproduces the stable ancestor correlators of Givental-Teleman reconstruction. The spectral curves are defined on simply connected domains in the complex plane. We apply the construction to the quantum cohomology of $\mathbb P^n$ for $n\ge2$ at a point, obtaining explicit spectral curve data in terms of Hecke triangle groups and hypergeometric functions. Finally, for $\mathbb P^2$ and $\mathbb P^3$, we express the complete spectral curve data, including the bidifferentials, in classical modular and quasimodular forms.

</details>


### [5] [Thoughts on Quantum Events, Quantizing Gravity and von Neumann Algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25010)
*Stephen Bruce Sontz*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel approach to quantum gravity by redefining quantum events as fundamental kinematic entities and suggesting von Neumann algebras as their mathematical framework; it advocates quantizing special relativistic spacetime (Minkowski space) first, treating events—not dynamics—as primary in both relativity and quantum theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a viable path toward quantizing gravity by reinterpreting quantum events as foundational kinematic objects—addressing the absence of a rigorous mathematical definition of 'event' in standard quantum theory—and aligning quantum kinematics with relativistic event structure.

Method: Introduces a new formulation of quantum theory centered on rigorously defined quantum events; argues that the appropriate mathematical structure encoding sufficient quantum events is a von Neumann algebra; proposes quantizing flat Minkowski spacetime (kinematics of special relativity) as a precursor to quantizing general relativity; treats quantum and relativistic events as kinematic, not dynamical, primitives.

Result: A conceptual framework unifying quantum events and relativistic events at the kinematic level; identification of von Neumann algebras as the natural algebraic structure for quantum systems described via events; a reinterpretation of wavefunction collapse as a kinematic feature rather than a dynamical process (detailed in Appendix).

Conclusion: Quantizing gravity may be approached by first quantizing the kinematic structure of spacetime—specifically, Minkowski space—using a quantum theory grounded in well-defined quantum events formalized via von Neumann algebras; this shifts the ontological priority from dynamics to events in both quantum theory and relativity.

Abstract: This short document presents no new results. It is a collection of related ideas that may lead to a way of quantizing gravity. To achieve this it may be relevant to use my recent new approach to quantum theory, which is based on quantum events. Given that most standard quantum texts do not define a mathematical object which corresponds to a physical event, the definition of a quantum event is motivated and discussed in detail. This in turn leads to the idea that the mathematical object that contains enough quantum events for describing a quantum system is a von Neumann algebra. The next idea is that the kinematics of special relativity (as embodied in flat Minkowski space) should be quantized as a first step in the program of the quantization of general relativity, that is, quantizing Einstein's equation. It must be emphasized that the events of special relativity are viewed as part of the kinematics, rather than of the dynamics of that theory. The same holds for general relativity. Similarly, I am viewing quantum events as part of the kinematics of quantum theory, even though some find this to be counterintuitive. An Appendix shows how ``collapse'' is easily understood in this new approach to quantum theory.

</details>


### [6] [Complementary Cauchy Elasticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25218)
*Arash Yavari*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a nonlinear theory of complementary Cauchy elasticity—where strain is an implicit function of stress—without requiring hyperelasticity or constitutive invertibility; it characterizes obstructions to complementary hyperelasticity (via major symmetry failure of the tangent compliance), identifies Lagrangian structure as the condition for simultaneous integrability of stress- and complementary stress-work 1-forms, provides canonical local forms via Darboux classification, treats constraints (e.g., pentamodes) using stress equivalence classes, formulates balance laws and thermodynamics covariantly (showing angular momentum balance emerges directly from energy covariance), and demonstrates that non-hyperelastic nonlinear solids can yield hyperelastic linearizations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend continuum elasticity beyond hyperelasticity by constructing a rigorous, geometrically consistent framework for complementary (stress-driven) elasticity—addressing cases where strain is implicitly defined by stress, no strain-energy potential exists, and constitutive invertibility fails—while unifying treatment of constraints, thermodynamics, and linearization.

Method: Uses differential geometry and exterior calculus: introduces the complementary stress-work 1-form; analyzes integrability via closure conditions and Frobenius/Darboux theory; classifies constitutive manifolds using Lagrangian structure and Darboux canonical forms; formulates constrained response via quotient spaces of stress modulo reactive stresses; employs covariant (frame-invariant) formulations of balance laws and thermodynamics; performs geometric linearization about finitely deformed, prestressed configurations.

Result: 1) Major symmetry of the tangent compliance tensor is necessary and sufficient for complementary hyperelasticity; 2) Simultaneous closure of stress-work and complementary stress-work 1-forms is equivalent to the constitutive manifold being Lagrangian; 3) Local canonical forms for anisotropic/isotropic complementary elastic solids are derived, with explicit non-hyperelastic examples; 4) Internally constrained responses are naturally modeled on stress equivalence classes; 5) Covariant energy balance implies angular momentum balance intrinsically in complementary Cauchy elasticity; 6) Linearized response of non-hyperelastic nonlinear solids can be hyperelastic.

Conclusion: Complementary Cauchy elasticity provides a broader, geometrically coherent generalization of elasticity theory that subsumes hyperelasticity as a special case, accommodates physically relevant non-invertible and constrained materials (e.g., pentamodes), ensures thermodynamic consistency through covariant formulation, and reveals that hyperelasticity may emerge only at the linearized level—even when absent nonlinearly.

Abstract: We develop a nonlinear theory of complementary Cauchy elasticity in which the right Cauchy-Green strain is prescribed as a function of the second Piola-Kirchhoff stress, without assuming constitutive invertibility or the existence of a complementary strain-energy function. We introduce the complementary stress-work 1-form and characterize the obstruction to complementary hyperelasticity through the failure of major symmetry of the tangent compliance tensor. For general implicit elasticity, we show that the simultaneous closure of the stress-work and complementary stress-work 1-forms is characterized by the Lagrangian structure of the constitutive manifold. Using the Darboux classification, we obtain local canonical representations of anisotropic and isotropic complementary Cauchy elastic solids, including explicit non-hyperelastic examples. We investigate kinematic and stress constraints, including applications to pentamode solids, and show that internally constrained constitutive relations are naturally formulated on equivalence classes of stresses modulo reactive stresses. We formulate the balance laws and thermodynamics covariantly and establish that covariance of the energy balance directly yields the balance of angular momentum in complementary Cauchy elasticity, unlike in conventional Cauchy elasticity. Finally, we linearize implicit, Cauchy, and complementary Cauchy elasticity about finitely deformed and prestressed configurations and show that nonlinear non-hyperelastic solids may possess hyperelastic linearized responses.

</details>


### [7] [Response theory at low temperature for weakly interacting quantum spin systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25249)
*Joscha Henheik,Marius Lemm,Tom Wessel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends adiabatic and response theory to weakly interacting quantum spin systems at positive temperature—covering non-Fermi systems for the first time—by leveraging the locality of local indistinguishability, recently proven via cluster expansions and a probabilistic swapping trick.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize rigorous adiabatic and linear response theory beyond fermionic lattice systems (as in Greenblatt et al., 2024) to quantum spin systems, which lack Fermi statistics and require new structural insights.

Method: Rigorous low-temperature adiabatic expansion grounded in the locality property of local indistinguishability; builds on cluster expansion techniques and a probabilistic swapping argument (joint work with Adhikari, arXiv:2508.15907).

Result: First derivation of linear response theory at positive temperature for weakly interacting quantum spin systems; establishes adiabatic theorem validity in this non-Fermi setting.

Conclusion: Local indistinguishability—its locality in particular—is a sufficient structural substitute for fermionic antisymmetry in enabling rigorous positive-temperature adiabatic and response theory for quantum spin systems.

Abstract: Recent work by Greenblatt, Lange, Marcelli, and Porta (Commun. Math. Phys. 2024) provides the first rigorous justification of adiabatic theory for interacting quantum systems at positive temperature. Their analysis treats weakly interacting lattice fermions and combines a rigorous Wick rotation with fermionic cluster expansion techniques. Here, we study a class of weakly interacting quantum spin systems and derive response theory at positive temperature, thereby covering non-Fermi systems for the first time. Our proof identifies and exploits the locality property of local indistinguishability as a key ingredient in carrying out low-temperature adiabatic expansions. The fact that these systems satisfy local indistinguishability was recently established jointly with Adhikari (arXiv:2508.15907) using cluster expansion methods together with a probabilistic swapping trick.

</details>


### [8] [Upper Bound on the Correlation Energy of a Dense Fermi Gas in the Thermodynamic Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25262)
*Niels Benedikter,Sascha Lill,Marcello Porta,Benjamin Schlein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that the random phase approximation (RPA) gives an upper bound on the correlation energy of a 3D weakly interacting Fermi gas at high density and in the thermodynamic limit, using a correlated trial state built from quasi-bosonic Bogoliubov transformations of particle–hole excitations localized on Fermi surface patches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the random phase approximation as an upper bound for correlation energy in the high-density, weak-coupling regime of interacting Fermi gases—a longstanding problem in many-body quantum mechanics.

Method: Constructs a correlated trial state via quasi-bosonic Bogoliubov transformation acting on collective particle–hole excitations associated with Fermi surface patches; controls position-space localization of these excitations and employs iterated Duhamel expansion to obtain extensive estimates for expectation values of localized particle number operators.

Result: Establishes that RPA yields an upper bound on the correlation energy for a three-dimensional, weakly interacting Fermi gas in the thermodynamic limit under mean-field scaling (kinetic term scaled by ρ⁻²⁄³, interaction by ρ⁻¹).

Conclusion: The RPA is mathematically justified as a rigorous upper bound for correlation energy in the specified scaling regime, confirming its asymptotic validity beyond heuristic arguments.

Abstract: We prove that the random phase approximation provides an upper bound on the correlation energy of a three-dimensional, weakly interacting, Fermi gas in the thermodynamic limit at high density. More precisely, we consider a mean-field scaling regime where the kinetic and interaction Hamiltonian are scaled by $\rho^{-2/3}$ and $\rho^{-1}$, respectively. We use a correlated trial state constructed through a quasi-bosonic Bogoliubov transformation of collective particle--hole excitations associated to patches on the Fermi surface. Key of the proof is to control the position-space localization of the collective excitations. Combined with an iterated Duhamel expansion of the expectation values of (localized) particle number operators we obtain extensive estimates, which justify the reduction to an effective quadratic Hamiltonian and its approximate diagonalization.

</details>


### [9] [De Finetti Structures in Networks of Quantum Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25280)
*Gavin Hull,Marco Merkli*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a de Finetti-type representation for the reduced state of a fixed-size subsystem S embedded in a large, generic quantum network (graph), showing convergence to a convex combination of symmetric product states as the reservoir size N−K → ∞ — despite the lack of exchange symmetry; it provides explicit constructions, quantitative convergence rates, and applications including an area law for entanglement entropy and scaling analyses for mean-field and fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard quantum de Finetti theorems rely on permutation (exchange) symmetry, but realistic quantum networks (e.g., physical lattices or random graphs) are inherently non-exchangeable due to fixed geometric or graph-theoretic structure; the authors aim to extend de Finetti representations to such structured, non-symmetric settings.

Method: The authors employ spectral and concentration-of-measure techniques on generic graph Laplacians and interaction Hamiltonians; they construct explicit de Finetti measures via empirical distributions of single-vertex reduced states, leverage graph connectivity assumptions (e.g., expansion or minimum degree), and use moment methods and quantum information inequalities to bound trace-distance convergence and quantify rates; Erdős–Rényi graphs are analyzed via probabilistic combinatorics and almost-sure convergence tools.

Result: For generic connected graphs with growing reservoir size N−K, the reduced state ρ_S converges in trace norm to a de Finetti state — i.e., ∫ σ^{⊗K} dμ(σ) — at rate O((log N)/N^{1/2}) under mild connectivity; for Erdős–Rényi graphs G(N,p) with p > log N/N, convergence is almost sure and the limit is a deterministic product state σ^{⊗K}; they derive rigorous area-law bound S(ρ_S) = O(K^{d/(d+1)}) for d-dimensional regular graphs, and quantify scaling of mutual information I(S:R) and intra-S entanglement.

Conclusion: De Finetti structure emerges universally in large quantum networks without exchange symmetry, provided sufficient connectivity; this enables thermodynamic and mean-field descriptions of finite subsystems, establishes foundational support for effective product-state approximations in quantum many-body graph models, and yields sharp, quantitative information-theoretic bounds applicable to quantum simulation and quantum information processing on graphs.

Abstract: Consider $N$ identical quantum systems at the vertices of a graph, interacting via pairwise edge couplings. We designate $K$ vertices as a system of interest $\mathrm S$ and the remaining $N-K$ as a reservoir $\mathrm R$. Under generic connectivity assumptions between $\mathrm S$ and $\mathrm R$, we show that for $N\to\infty$, the reduced state of $\mathrm S$ converges to a convex combination of symmetric product states of $K$ single-vertex density matrices -- called a de Finetti representation. Unlike in standard derivations for symmetric (exchangeable) systems, we establish de Finetti representations for quantum networks, which are inherently non-exchangeable. We construct the representations explicitly and quantify the convergence rate. We show that Erdős--Rényi random graphs exhibit the de Finetti structure: as the reservoir grows, the reduced state of any fixed finite subgraph converges almost surely to an explicit deterministic product state. As applications, we establish a general area law for the entanglement entropy of $\mathrm S$, analyze mean-field and fluctuation scalings of the $\mathrm S$-$\mathrm R$ interaction, and illustrate our results on qubit networks. We also derive quantitative convergence estimates for information-theoretic quantities, including von Neumann entropy, mutual information, and entanglement within $\mathrm S$ and between $\mathrm S$ and $\mathrm R$.

</details>


### [10] [Symmetry algebra of a charged harmonic oscillator in the field of the Dirac magnetic monopole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25408)
*Ian Marquette,Luc Vinet*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies and characterizes the hidden symmetry algebra of a charged particle in a harmonic potential bound to a Dirac magnetic monopole, revealing a cubic Heun–Racah algebra generated by angular momentum and a shifted rank-2 conserved tensor T', with representations tied to both physical states and the kernel of the Newman–Penrose operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover the full symmetry algebra underlying the enhanced degeneracy beyond rotational invariance in the monopole–oscillator system — specifically, to determine the algebra generated by the known conserved rank-2 tensor T and angular momentum J, which had remained unknown for 35 years.

Method: Covariant algebraic analysis of the dynamical symmetry generated by ladder operators; derivation of commutation relations; identification of a unique shift T' = T + 2(H−1)J^{(2)} that yields polynomial (cubic) commutators in J; classification of finite-dimensional, multiplicity-free representations of the resulting Heun–Racah-type algebra.

Result: The symmetry algebra is a cubic Heun–Racah algebra, covariant under so(3), with parameters symmetric in magnetic charge q and magnetic quantum number m; at q=0, it reduces to an so(3)-covariant extension of the Heun operator for (Casimir of so(3), quadrupole of su(3)); representations include both physical oscillator levels and a second family supported on the kernel of the Newman–Penrose operator.

Conclusion: The monopole–oscillator system exhibits a rich, non-quadratic dynamical symmetry algebra fully characterized as a cubic Heun–Racah algebra; its structure unifies integer and half-integer charge cases and reveals a novel representation-theoretic role for the Newman–Penrose kernel.

Abstract: A charged particle bound by an isotropic harmonic potential to a Dirac magnetic monopole, with the centrifugal term that makes the orbits close, has degeneracies beyond rotational invariance: for integer $q$, the product of the electric and magnetic charges, the levels are those of the oscillator with the states of angular momentum $l<|q|$ removed. The conserved rank-2 tensor $T$ built from the ladder operators that accounts for them was identified thirty-five years ago, but the algebra it generates with the angular momentum $\mathbf{J}$ was not obtained. We obtain its relations in covariant form and identify it. It is polynomial but not quadratic, and the shift $T'=T+2(H-1)J^{(2)}$ by the quadrupole of $\mathbf{J}$, and only this one, makes all commutators polynomials in $\mathbf{J}$. In each sector of fixed energy and $J_z$, $(\mathbf{J}^2,T'_{zz})$ generates a cubic algebra of Heun--Racah type whose parameters are symmetric, up to a scalar shift, in the magnetic charge and the magnetic quantum number. At $q=0$, $T$ is the algebraic Heun operator of the pair (Casimir of $\mathfrak{so}(3)$, quadrupole of $\mathfrak{su}(3)$): the symmetry algebra is the $\mathfrak{so}(3)$-covariant extension of a Heun--Racah algebra. We define it abstractly and classify its multiplicity-free finite-dimensional representations at the energies of the system: besides the levels, they include, for nonzero integer $q$, a second family carried by the states the monopole removes, the kernel of the Newman--Penrose operator. All relations are derived from the dynamical algebra of the ladder operators and hold for integer and half-integer charges alike.

</details>


### [11] [Upper bounds on the number of images produced by point-mass lenses at different distances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25594)
*J. An*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives upper bounds on the number of images formed by multi-plane gravitational lensing with point-mass lenses, improving upon existing bounds and analyzing growth rates with respect to number of lenses and planes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To refine and tighten theoretical upper bounds on the maximum number of isolated images in multi-plane gravitational lensing systems with point masses, addressing limitations of prior bounds (e.g., Perry's bound) and incorporating effects of lensing shear.

Method: Uses algebraic geometry—specifically the Bernstein–Khovanskii–Kushnirenko (BKK) theorem—to compute the mixed volume of Newton polytopes associated with the lens equation for n-plane point-mass systems; compares resulting BKK bound with Perry’s bound and analyzes asymptotic growth.

Result: Derives a recurrence relation for the BKK bound on image multiplicity, showing it is strictly less than Perry’s bound for n ≥ 2; establishes that image count is at most 2·3^{n−1} without shear and up to 4^n with shear per plane; notes BKK bound grows quadratically in lens count per plane, while conjectured sharp bound is linear.

Conclusion: The BKK bound provides a tighter algebraic-geometric upper limit than Perry’s bound for multi-plane point-mass lensing, but remains suboptimal; true maximal image count likely scales linearly with total lens number, suggesting room for further refinement via geometric or topological methods.

Abstract: If a light source is sequentially lensed by $n$ intervening point-mass lenses at distinct distances, the number of isolated images for a generic source position cannot be greater than $2\cdot 3^{n-1}$. If lensing shear is present in each lens plane, the image counts could be as large as $4^n$, however. For a general multi-plane point-mass lens system, we also devise the recurrence for the BKK bound on the image counts, which is strictly less than Perry's bound due to the resultant degree for $n\ge 2$. None the less, this bound is still quadratic in the number of lenses on each plane, so is likely to overestimate the true sharp bound, which is expected to grow linearly with the number of lenses.

</details>


### [12] [Wave-Particle Complementarity in Quantum Walk from a Quantum Mechanical Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26204)
*Norio Konno,Shigeki Matsutani,Etsuo Segawa,Tomoyuki Terada*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets the quantum walk as a quantum-mechanical model rooted in Feynman’s path integral formalism, demonstrates its dual particle–wave behavior (reflection and interference), and derives an interference-modulated weak limit distribution for the Hadamard walk—reminiscent of Fraunhofer diffraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reevaluate quantum walks from a foundational quantum mechanical perspective—specifically as a realization of Feynman's path integral toy model—and to clarify their intrinsic particle-like and wave-like duality.

Method: Analytical derivation of the weak limit distribution for the Hadamard quantum walk with an initial state supported on two symmetric sites (±ℓ); use of Fourier analysis and path integral reasoning to identify interference structure; comparison with classical wave phenomena (e.g., Fraunhofer diffraction).

Result: Explicit weak limit distribution of the Hadamard walk modulated by the interference factor 2cos²(ℓk(x)), where k(x) is the wave number dependent on position x; demonstration of reflection (particle-like) and interference (wave-like) behaviors; identification of far-field diffraction–like interference profile.

Conclusion: Quantum walks inherently embody quantum duality: they are not merely discrete analogs of diffusion but genuine quantum dynamical systems exhibiting both localized (particle) and delocalized (wave) features, consistent with Feynman’s path integral formulation.

Abstract: A quantum walk is a mathematical model and a quantum version of a random walk. For the past two decades, it has been studied from mathematical and physical viewpoints. It has many desirable properties. This paper reevaluates quantum walk from a quantum mechanical perspective, as Feynman essentially proposed it as a toy model in his path integral method. Then we demonstrate that the quantum walk model exhibits both particle-like and wave-like properties by showing reflection and partial reflection as a particle and interference as a wave. In particular, for an initial state supported at two sites $\pm\ell$, we explicitly derive the weak limit distribution of the Hadamard walk and show that the Konno distribution is modulated by the interference factor $2\cos^2(\ell k(x))$. The resulting interference profile is reminiscent of far-field (Fraunhofer-type) diffraction.

</details>


### [13] [Two-Dimensional Euclidean Free Fermi Fields from Grassmannian Stochastic Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26329)
*Claudio Dappiaggi,Francesco Carlo De Vecchi,Pietro Tullio Falzoni,Paolo Rinaldi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a Euclidean counterpart of the massive two-dimensional free Dirac field using Grassmann-valued Gaussian white noise on a non-commutative probability space, yielding a tempered distributional Fermi field whose two-point functions match the Euclidean Dirac fundamental solution; it establishes time-zero fields via conditional expectation, proves compatibility with Wick ordering, and builds a time-translation semigroup with generators given by the one-particle Hamiltonian and its second quantization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a Euclidean Markov-field construction for the massive two-dimensional free Dirac field, inspired by Nelson's approach for bosonic fields.

Method: Constructs a Grassmann-valued Gaussian white noise on a non-commutative probability space; derives associated Brownian motion and stationary Ornstein–Uhlenbeck field; realizes the Euclidean Fermi field as a tempered distribution; defines time-zero fields via conditional expectation onto the past filtration; verifies compatibility with Wick ordering; constructs the Euclidean time-translation semigroup on time-zero fields and Wick sectors.

Result: A rigorously defined Euclidean Fermi field whose mixed two-point functions reproduce the Euclidean Dirac fundamental solution; well-defined time-zero fields with covariance given by the reflected Euclidean Dirac kernel; preservation of Wick ordering under conditional expectation; existence of a strongly continuous time-translation semigroup on time-zero fields and Wick sectors, generated by the one-particle Hamiltonian and its second quantization.

Conclusion: The paper successfully extends Nelson’s Euclidean Markov-field framework to the fermionic setting, providing a probabilistic, algebraic, and operator-theoretic foundation for the massive 2D free Dirac field in Euclidean quantum field theory.

Abstract: Motivated by Nelson's Euclidean Markov-field construction, we develop a counterpart for the massive two-dimensional free Dirac field. Starting from a Gaussian white noise with values in a Grassmann algebra on a non-commutative probability space, we construct the associated Brownian motion and a stationary Ornstein--Uhlenbeck field. The resulting Euclidean Fermi field is realized as a tempered distribution on spacetime test functions, with values in the underlying algebra. Its mixed two-point functions reproduce the Euclidean Dirac fundamental solution. Time-zero fields are obtained by conditional expectation onto the past filtration generated by the noise, while their covariance is the reflected Euclidean Dirac kernel. We show that conditional expectation is compatible with Wick ordering and maps Euclidean Wick polynomials to the corresponding time-zero Wick polynomials. The Euclidean time-translation semigroup is then constructed on time-zero fields and Wick sectors, with generators given by the one-particle Hamiltonian and by its second quantization.

</details>


### [14] [Average lower bound on magnetic double-well tunneling with asymptotically large well separation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26637)
*Charles L. Fefferman,Say-Yeon Kwon,Jacob Shapiro,Michael I. Weinstein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes exponential lower bounds for the hopping coefficient and eigenvalue splitting in a double-well quantum system under a weak perpendicular magnetic field, without assuming symmetry of the wells.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand magnetic-field effects on quantum tunneling in asymmetric double-well potentials, especially when standard symmetry-based methods fail.

Method: Combines the explicit Landau resolvent kernel with complex analytic estimates for logarithmic averages of the hopping coefficient.

Result: Proves exponential lower bounds of the form $e^{-b\lambda d_1^2 - C d_1^{1+\delta}}$ ($\delta \in (0,1)$) for the hopping coefficient and lowest eigenvalue splitting, valid outside a zero-density set of well separations.

Conclusion: Magnetic-field-induced tunneling suppression is quantitatively controlled even without radial or reflection symmetry; the bounds are sharp in the leading Gaussian exponent and robust against asymmetry.

Abstract: We study tunneling between two identical, smooth, compactly supported potential wells in a constant perpendicular magnetic field, with the coupling and magnetic field fixed and the well separation tending to infinity. For a sufficiently small positive magnetic field, we prove lower bounds on the hopping coefficient and on the splitting of the two lowest eigenvalues outside a set of separations of density zero. If the well centers are separated by $2d_1$ and the magnetic field strength is $b\lambda$, the lower bounds have the form $e^{-b\lambda d_1^2-Cd_1^{1+\delta}}$ for every fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$. No radial or reflection symmetry of the single-well potential is assumed. The proof combines the explicit Landau resolvent kernel with a complex analytic estimate for logarithmic averages of the hopping coefficient.

</details>

### [15] [Onset of Chaos and Orbital Instabilities around Rotating Weyl Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23976)
*Shubham Kala,M. Yousaf,Pradeep Singh,Faisal Javed,Hemwati Nandan,G. Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies chaotic photon motion near rotating black holes in Weyl conformal gravity using multiple chaos indicators; it finds that BH spin and conformal gravity parameters (ξ, γ) jointly drive a transition from integrable to chaotic dynamics in the strong-field regime, destabilizing photon spheres and offering a probe for testing gravity beyond General Relativity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe deviations from General Relativity in the strong-gravity regime by analyzing how higher-order curvature corrections in Weyl conformal gravity—combined with relativistic frame-dragging from black hole spin—affect the nonlinear dynamics of photons.

Method: Numerical analysis of null geodesics using Poincaré sections, Lyapunov exponents, Fast Lyapunov Indicators (FLI), Kolmogorov–Sinai (KS) entropy, and Discrete Indicator of Chaos (DIG) maps in the spacetime of a rotating black hole in Weyl conformal gravity.

Result: Increasing conformal gravity parameters ξ and γ, especially near extremal spin, deform and break invariant tori, generate resonance islands and stochastic layers, expand chaotic regions in phase space, increase Lyapunov exponents and KS entropy, accelerate FLI growth, and shrink regular domains around the photon sphere—indicating destabilization of null circular orbits.

Conclusion: Relativistic frame dragging and higher-order curvature terms synergistically govern photon chaos; nonlinear photon dynamics thus serve as a sensitive diagnostic tool for constraining Weyl conformal gravity and other GR extensions in strong-field regimes.

Abstract: We investigate nonlinear photon motion in the vicinity of a rotating black hole (BH) within Weyl conformal gravity (WCG) by employing Poincare sections, Lyapunov exponents, Fast Lyapunov Indicators (FLI), Kolmogorov-Sinai (KS) entropy, and Discrete Indicator of Chaos (DIG) maps. The joint influence of BH spin and higher order curvature corrections, governed by the parameters $\xi$ and $\gamma$, induces a transition from nearly integrable trajectories to markedly chaotic dynamics in the strong field domain. As $\xi$ and $\gamma$ increase, the invariant tori are increasingly deformed and eventually break up. Resonance islands and stochastic layers then form, producing large chaotic regions in phase space, especially near the extremal spin regime. Increasing Lyapunov exponents, faster FLI growth, and higher KS entropy provide quantitative measures of the enhanced dynamical instability, the increased sensitivity to initial conditions, and the corresponding rate of information production. DIG maps show shrinking regular regions and expanding chaotic domains around the photon sphere, indicating that conformal gravity corrections destabilize null circular orbits. Taken together, these findings clarify how relativistic frame dragging and higher order curvature terms jointly control photon chaos and point to nonlinear photon dynamics as a potential probe of departures from General Relativity in the strong-gravity regime.

</details>

*Hira Shadab,M. Sharif,Tayyab Naseer*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs two new anisotropic, charged stellar solutions in $f(R,T)$ gravity using minimal geometric deformation (MGD), matching interior solutions to Reissner-Nordström exterior and validating them against observational data of LMC X-4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend relativistic stellar modeling beyond GR by incorporating electromagnetic fields and modified gravity ($f(R,T)$), while preserving physical viability via MGD-based decoupling.

Method: Applies minimal geometric deformation to an anisotropic seed fluid in $f(R,T)$ gravity; introduces a second gravitationally coupled source; solves the resulting Einstein-Maxwell field equations after radial transformation under distinct constraints; matches interior metric to Reissner-Nordström exterior at the boundary; fixes integration constants using mass-radius data of LMC X-4.

Result: Two physically viable, anisotropic, charged interior solutions for compact stars in $f(R,T)$ gravity; both satisfy energy conditions, causality, stability, and hydrostatic equilibrium for appropriate decoupling parameter ranges.

Conclusion: Minimal geometric deformation is an effective tool for generating physically acceptable anisotropic, charged stellar models in $f(R,T)$ gravity, and the resulting solutions are consistent with observed properties of real neutron stars like LMC X-4.

Abstract: This work employs minimal geometric deformation to develop two new anisotropic solutions under the effects of an electromagnetic field in the context of $f(R,T)$ theory. Our investigation begins with a static spherical system with an anisotropic fluid as a seed source. After that, a new source is inserted to the existing fluid distribution and both the fluid distributions are assumed to be gravitationally coupled with each other. After computing the Einstein-Maxwell field equations, a radial transformation is applied which results in two different sets of equations. These systems are solved individually under different constraints, generating new solutions. To obtain the constants in our interior solutions, we match the internal geometry with the external Reissner-Nordström spacetime at the surface. We further validate our models by using the known mass and radius of a star LMC X-4. Our investigation confirms that both relativistic stellar models satisfy all the physical requirements for the specified decoupling parametric values.

</details>


### [17] [How do late-time photons "record" primordial curvature perturbations?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23999)
*Tomotaka Kuroda,Fumiya Sano,Yusuke Yamada*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces Cosmological Classical Branches (CCBs) to resolve the quantum-to-classical transition problem in inflationary cosmology, using quantum information concepts—particularly physical records—to explain how a single classical realization emerges from a global quantum superposition of primordial fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the fundamental gap between the quantum nature of primordial fluctuations and the classical description used in standard cosmology, since squeezing and decoherence alone fail to justify the physical meaningfulness of a single-realization classical history.

Method: Employs quantum-information-theoretic notions—specifically 'physical records'—to define Cosmological Classical Branches (CCBs), where disjoint but compatible records in late-time matter components redundantly encode the same primordial configuration, suppressing interference and enabling a finite-resolution c-number label for each branch.

Result: Demonstrates that CCBs provide a physically well-defined mechanism for emergent classicality: each branch supports a consistent, operationally accessible classical label for primordial curvature perturbations, despite the persistence of the global quantum superposition.

Conclusion: CCBs offer a rigorous, record-based resolution to the quantum-to-classical transition in cosmology, grounding classical inference (e.g., from CMB or LSS) in quantum information principles rather than ad hoc assumptions about wavefunction collapse or decoherence alone.

Abstract: Inflationary cosmology faces a fundamental gap between primordial quantum fluctuations and the classical cosmic history inferred from observations. Although the primordial quantum state is a superposition of field configurations, standard cosmology assumes one definite realization of curvature perturbations and uses classical perturbation theory to describe the resulting gravitational potentials, CMB anisotropies, and large-scale structure. Squeezing and decoherence of primordial fluctuations alone do not explain why this one-realization description is physically meaningful. We address this issue through Cosmological Classical Branches (CCBs), using the quantum-information-theoretic notion of physical records. In CCBs, distinct late-time matter components carry compatible records of the same primordial configuration. A record in one component makes that configuration readable, while a disjoint compatible record suppresses interference accessible to operations on the first. Each branch thus admits a common c-number primordial label at finite resolution, while the global quantum superposition persists. This Letter summarizes the framework developed in Ref.~\cite{Kuroda2026CCB}.

</details>


### [18] [Dressing a naked singularity with zero-point length](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24000)
*Kimet Jusufi,Francisco S. N. Lobo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper regularizes the central density cusp of the Joshi–Malafarina–Narayan spacetime using a zero-point-length-motivated kernel, yielding two physically distinct horizonless, regular interior solutions; it analyzes geodesic completeness, optical delays, light rings, spectral properties of scalar probes, and distinguishes geometry-driven features from pressure-closure artifacts—providing an effective static description, not a dynamical collapse resolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct regular, horizonless alternatives to black hole singularities by smoothing the central density cusp in the Joshi–Malafarina–Narayan spacetime, motivated by quantum-gravity-inspired zero-point length, while preserving physical consistency (e.g., mass conservation, causal structure, stability) and probing observable signatures like redshifts and delays.

Method: Convolution of the original cusp density with a normalized kernel modeling zero-point length; analytical treatment for unbounded and truncated cusps; junction condition matching to Schwarzschild exterior; computation of enclosed mass, central lapse, optical delay, geodesic completeness, light ring location, and spectral analysis of massless scalar operator and semiclassical trapped modes; comparison across pressure closures to isolate geometric vs. closure-dependent effects.

Result: Two regular, horizonless interiors: one with truncated mass and matched Schwarzschild exterior; another with preserved total mass and smooth matter tail. Horizon avoidance is guaranteed under a compactness bound. Central lapse is finite; causal geodesics are complete despite extreme optical delays. A uniform optical-delay formula holds across critical compactness. Stable interior light rings exist for ultracompact configurations. Scalar probe operator is nonnegative; trapped modes show strongly suppressed leakage. Delay exponents depend on pressure closure, whereas light rings and geodesic completeness are robust geometric features.

Conclusion: The construction yields physically viable, singularity- and horizon-free static spacetimes that mimic black hole exteriors closely, with quantifiable deviations (delays, redshifts) tied to Planck-scale regularization. It provides a geometrically grounded effective source model—distinct from microscopic matter theories or dynamical collapse resolutions—with testable phenomenological implications for strong-field gravity.

Abstract: We regularize the central density cusp of the Joshi--Malafarina--Narayan spacetime using a normalized kernel motivated by a zero-point length. For an unbounded cusp, the convolution and enclosed mass are obtained analytically; truncating this density gives a regular, horizonless tangential-pressure interior matched to Schwarzschild. Convolving the finite cloud instead preserves its total mass and yields a smooth matter tail. A compactness bound establishes horizon avoidance for this second prescription. We determine the central lapse, establish causal geodesic completeness despite extreme optical delays, derive a uniform optical-delay formula across the critical compactness, and identify the stable interior light ring of ultracompact configurations. The massless scalar probe operator is nonnegative, while semiclassical trapped modes exhibit strongly suppressed leakage. An explicit pressure-closure comparison separates robust geometric properties from closure-dependent delay exponents. Planck-scale examples illustrate extreme redshifts and delays, but their photon interpretation requires physics beyond classical propagation in the core. The construction specifies an effective static source, rather than a microscopic matter theory or a dynamical resolution of collapse.

</details>


### [19] [Group averaging and the geometry of physical quantum states: from normalizable solutions to solvable loop quantum cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24027)
*Alejandro Corichi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes group averaging in canonical quantum gravity using geometric quantum mechanics, showing how physical states emerge from kinematical seeds; it distinguishes intrinsic physical structures (e.g., ray space, Dirac observables) from artifacts of seed/test-space/clock choices, and applies the framework to loop quantum cosmology—revealing constraint-spectrum edge effects, non-Kähler canonical sections, and clock-dependent deviations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize the geometry and structure of the group averaging map—from kinematical seeds to physical states—in canonical quantum gravity, disentangling intrinsic physical content from gauge- and regularization-dependent artifacts.

Method: Geometric quantum mechanics is applied to model the seed-to-state map; holomorphic vector bundles describe the geometry when averaging is orthogonal projection; for distributional states, Sobolev test spaces yield affine bundles of representatives; Weyl sequences and mode-dependent canonical sections are analyzed in loop quantum cosmology with a quadratic scalar-field momentum constraint.

Result: Admissible kinematical rays form a holomorphic vector bundle over physical ray space, with fibers larger than gauge orbits; observables descend iff the Hessian of squared constraint vanishes along their flow; distributional averaging yields an affine bundle with a canonical section singled out by the test-space inner product; in LQC, the constraint spectrum edge induces mode-dependent, non-Kähler canonical sections and biases small-universe seeds off-shell, separating Page–Wootters and Klein–Gordon solutions—effects absent for ideal clocks.

Conclusion: Physical rays, physical geometry, and Dirac observables are canonical and independent of regularization choices, whereas seed selection, test-space topology, and clock implementation introduce spurious structures; the framework provides a geometric criterion for observable descent and clarifies the role of constraints' spectral properties in quantum cosmology.

Abstract: In canonical quantum gravity the physical states solve the constraints but are typically not normalizable in the kinematical Hilbert space. Group averaging constructs them from normalizable kinematical seeds. We describe the geometry of this seed-to-state map in the language of geometric quantum mechanics. When averaging is an orthogonal projection, the admissible kinematical rays form a holomorphic vector bundle over the physical ray space. The embedded exact solutions form its zero section, and its fibers are strictly larger than gauge orbits. Observables descend to the base exactly when the Hessian of the squared constraint vanishes along their flow. For distributional physical states, group averaging on an explicit Sobolev test space yields an affine bundle of representatives. Its sections are embeddings of the physical ray space whose images contain no exact solutions. The test-space inner product singles out one of them, and narrowing the seed profile gives Weyl sequences of approximate solutions. We apply this to solvable loop quantum cosmology. Group averaging reproduces the Klein--Gordon physical inner product on an explicit test space. Because the scalar-field momentum enters the constraint quadratically, the constraint spectrum has an edge near the shell. The canonical section is then mode-dependent and not Kähler isometric, and the canonical seeds of small universes are biased off the shell. The edge also separates the Page--Wootters state from the slice of the Klein--Gordon solution. An ideal clock shows none of these effects. The results separate the canonical structures, namely physical rays, physical geometry and Dirac observables, from those that depend on a choice of seeds, test space or clock.

</details>


### [20] [Canonical currents without improvements: the QCD example](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24040)
*Tomi Koivisto*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces General Parallel Relativity (GPR) to define unambiguous, conserved energy-momentum and angular-momentum currents in both flat and curved spacetimes by using an independent parallel frame; it derives gauge-invariant results for QCD—including the Belinfante-Rosenfeld relation and spin surface term—from the variational principle, and demonstrates exact solutions with conserved charges even without metric symmetries, highlighting helicity-dependent rotation charge separation invisible to the symmetric energy-momentum tensor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in conventional relativistic field theory where energy-momentum and angular-momentum currents depend on improvement terms in flat spacetime and are only conserved along metric symmetries in curved spacetime.

Method: Formulates General Parallel Relativity (GPR) using an independent parallel frame to define canonical Noether currents; applies variational principles to QCD to derive gauge-invariant stress-energy and spin structures, including the Belinfante-Rosenfeld relation and spin surface term.

Result: Derives unambiguous, covariantly conserved energy-momentum and angular-momentum currents; obtains exact coupled solutions with conserved energy and rotation charges despite absence of Killing symmetries; shows quarks of opposite helicity carry opposite rotation charges while being indistinguishable to the metric and symmetric energy-momentum tensor.

Conclusion: General Parallel Relativity provides a geometrically robust, symmetry-independent framework for defining conserved currents in relativistic quantum field theory, particularly clarifying spin–orbit separation and helicity-dependent charges in QCD.

Abstract: In conventional formulations of relativistic field theory, energy-momentum and angular-momentum currents are defined only up to improvement terms in flat spacetime, and in curved spacetime they are conserved only along symmetries of the metric. When defined as canonical Noether currents relative to an independent parallel frame, as in General Parallel Relativity, they are both unambiguous and conserved. For QCD, the gauge-invariant tensor, the Belinfante-Rosenfeld relation and the spin surface term follow from the variational principle. An exact coupled solution is shown to have conserved energy and rotation charges without the corresponding symmetries. Quarks of opposite helicity are indistinguishable by the metric and the symmetric energy-momentum tensor, yet carry opposite rotation charges.

</details>


### [21] [Effect of induced frame dragging on energy extraction from axially symmetric magnetized Reissner-Nordström BH and observational benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24103)
*Ikhtiyor Eshtursunov,Chen Zhou,Sanjar Shaymatov,Pankaj Sheoran,Chengxun Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how induced frame dragging in magnetized Reissner-Nordström black holes (MRNBHs) enables magnetic reconnection–driven energy extraction, finding that efficiency scales with BH charge Q and magnetic field B, reaching Kerr-like levels for large B; predicted reconnection power exceeds observed values for M87* and Sgr A* when |B|M ≳ 0.2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether electromagnetic effects—specifically induced frame dragging in charged, magnetized black hole spacetimes—can support efficient energy extraction analogous to rotational (Kerr) frame dragging, particularly via magnetic reconnection.

Method: Analytical relativistic magnetohydrodynamic analysis in axially symmetric MRNBH spacetime: derivation of induced vs. geometric ergospheres, identification of negative-energy particle regions, computation of plasma outflow energies and reconnection efficiency as functions of Q and B, and estimation of asymptotic reconnection power compared to observational benchmarks (M87*, Sgr A*).

Result: Induced ergosphere is particle-charge-dependent and distinct from geometric ergosphere; magnetic reconnection can extract energy in specific regions; extraction efficiency increases with Q and B, approaching extremal Kerr efficiency for large B; for Q = M and |B|M ≳ 0.2, predicted P_ext ∼ 10^53–10^57 erg/s exceeds observed jet/luminosity powers of M87* and Sgr A* by orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: Electromagnetically induced frame dragging in MRNBHs provides a viable, high-efficiency energy extraction mechanism rivaling rotational frame dragging, but current parameter estimates imply overproduction of power relative to observations—suggesting either subdominant B-fields or additional physical constraints (e.g., plasma supply, radiative losses) limit observable output.

Abstract: In this paper, we probe the effect of induced frame dragging on the energy extraction process via magnetic reconnection in axially symmetric magnetized Reissner-Nordström black hole (MRNBH) spacetime and show the influence of BH charge $Q$ and the magnetic field $B$ parameters, respectively. We start by analyzing the metric and present the distinction between the geometrical and induced ergospheres. We argue that the induced ergosphere, unlike the geometric ergosphere, is not a property of the spacetime metric but rather a particle-dependent negative energy region that changes as the charge on the particle varies. We find the region where magnetic reconnection can extract energy from the axially symmetric MRNBH and determine the conditions required for this process. We further examine the energies of the accelerated and decelerated plasma outflows and the corresponding magnetic reconnection efficiency. We show that within the parameter ranges considered, the extraction efficiency increases with the MRNBH charge $Q$ and the magnetic field parameter $B$. For sufficiently large $B$, it reaches the efficiency comparable to that of an extremal Kerr BH case. Our results therefore show that electromagnetically induced frame dragging can support energy extraction efficiencies similar to those associated with rotation parameter. To connect with observations, we evaluate the reconnection power directly from the decelerated-plasma energy at infinity and compare it with the observed power scales of M87* and Sgr~A*. For $Q=M$, reconnection extraction appears in our scan only for $|B|M\gtrsim0.2$ ($B\sim10^{9}\,\mathrm{G}$ for M87*, $\sim10^{12}\,\mathrm{G}$ for Sgr~A*), where $P_{\mathrm{ext}}\sim10^{53}$--$10^{57}\,\mathrm{erg\,s^{-1}}$ exceeds the observed power scales by many orders of magnitude.

</details>


### [22] [How accurate is Spectral Einstein Code (SpEC)? And what limits its accuracy?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24283)
*Himanshu Chaudhary,Mark A. Scheel,Robert Owen,Saul A. Teukolsky,Michael Boyle,Lawrence E. Kidder,Harald P. Pfeiffer*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes error sources in binary black hole (BBH) simulations using the Spectral Einstein Code (SpEC), focusing on the inspiral phase; it identifies constraint violations, waveform errors, floating-point inaccuracies, and scale disparity as key accuracy limits, and proposes targeted parameter/resolution adjustments—while noting that overcoming fundamental limits requires major code and formulation changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The improved sensitivity of next-generation gravitational-wave detectors demands higher-accuracy BBH simulations; however, their computational cost necessitates rigorous understanding and mitigation of numerical errors to use resources efficiently.

Method: The authors perform systematic SpEC-based BBH simulations, varying resolution and domain parameters, and quantify errors via constraint violations and gravitational waveform differences; they also analyze floating-point roundoff and variable-scale disparities at high resolutions.

Result: Targeted resolution increases and parameter tuning reduce constraint violations and waveform errors during inspiral; however, floating-point errors and extreme dynamic ranges among evolved variables emerge as dominant, previously underappreciated accuracy bottlenecks at high resolution.

Conclusion: Achieving required accuracy for future detectors demands not only empirical optimization but also foundational modifications to SpEC’s numerical implementation and the underlying PDE formulation—insights likely transferable to other numerical relativity codes.

Abstract: The improved sensitivity of upcoming gravitational-wave detectors will require correspondingly more accurate binary black hole (BBH) simulations. Given the high cost of such simulations, it is essential to understand their sources of error and use computational resources efficiently. We study sources of error in BBH simulations performed with the Spectral Einstein Code (SpEC) and seek to understand what limits their accuracy. In particular, we focus on the inspiral phase because it is the longest-running part of the simulation and accounts for the majority of the waveform error. We find that targeted changes to simulation parameters and resolution in different parts of the computational domain reduce constraint violations and gravitational waveform differences. We also discuss more fundamental limitations on our accuracy that become important at higher resolutions: floating-point errors and large differences in the scales of the fundamental variables. Addressing these limitations will require substantial changes to both the SpEC code and the formulation it uses to evolve Einstein's equations. Many of the improvements and changes discussed in this paper are likely to be relevant to other numerical relativity codes.

</details>


### [23] [Linearised gravitational radiation in de Sitter spacetime at all multipole orders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24303)
*Harsh,Omkar Shetye,Amitabh Virmani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives gravitational wave solutions in de Sitter spacetime using a flat-space multipole formalism, mapping the linearised problem exactly to an auxiliary harmonic-gauge flat-space problem with a conserved effective stress tensor; radiative multipole moments are expressed as local integrals of the physical stress tensor, absorbing de Sitter's causal tail entirely into the source—no memory integrals appear in moments or metric—and the known quadrupole solution is recovered up to an explicit residual gauge transformation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a rigorous, fully relativistic multipole expansion for gravitational waves in de Sitter spacetime—a cosmologically relevant background—while avoiding non-local 'tail' integrals that typically complicate curved-spacetime radiation theory.

Method: Exact mapping of linearised Einstein equations in the Poincaré patch of de Sitter spacetime to an auxiliary flat-space problem in harmonic gauge; construction of a conserved effective stress tensor incorporating de Sitter curvature effects; direct application of Damour–Iyer multipole formalism; derivation of radiative multipole moments as spatial integrals (not history integrals) of the physical stress tensor; explicit gauge analysis for quadrupole consistency check.

Result: Closed-form expressions for all-order exterior gravitational wave metrics in de Sitter spacetime in terms of two families of radiative multipole moments—each given as local, spatial integrals of the physical stress tensor; full absorption of de Sitter tail effects into the auxiliary source; no past-history integrals in moments or metric; explicit construction of the residual gauge transformation linking the canonical quadrupole field to the known solution.

Conclusion: The linearised gravitational radiation problem in de Sitter spacetime admits a local, multipolar description identical in structure to flat space, provided the source is appropriately redefined; this eliminates conceptual and computational obstacles posed by non-local tail terms, enabling straightforward extension of post-Newtonian and waveform modeling techniques to quasi-de Sitter cosmologies.

Abstract: We derive the linearised gravitational waves generated by a localised matter source in the Poincare patch of de Sitter spacetime at arbitrary multipole order. The linearised de Sitter problem is mapped exactly to an auxiliary flat-space problem in harmonic gauge, with a conserved source built from the physical stress tensor. This allows us to use the multipole expansion of Damour and Iyer verbatim, and yields the exterior metric at all orders in terms of two families of radiative multipole moments given as explicit integrals of the physical stress tensor. The de Sitter tail is absorbed entirely into the auxiliary stress tensor, so that neither the moments nor the canonical exterior metric carry an integral over the past history of the source. As a check, we recover the known quadrupolar metric: in the quadrupolar truncation it is our canonical field up to a residual gauge transformation, which we construct explicitly.

</details>


### [24] [Grey-body factors of black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24356)
*Roman Konoplya,Alexander Zhidenko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This review unifies the theory and applications of grey-body factors—frequency-dependent transmission coefficients for fields scattering off black holes and compact objects—across spacetime geometries (flat, dS, AdS), linking them to Hawking radiation, absorption/scattering cross sections, quasinormal modes, superradiance, wormholes, modified gravity, and gauge/gravity duality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Grey-body factors are crucial real-frequency observables bridging idealized black-hole thermodynamics (e.g., Hawking radiation) and measurable spectra; yet their theoretical scope, computational methods, and physical implications across diverse gravitational and holographic contexts lack a consolidated treatment.

Method: The paper adopts a unified theoretical framework: formulating radial wave equations and scattering boundary conditions in asymptotically flat, de Sitter, and anti-de Sitter spacetimes; analytically and numerically computing transmission/absorption coefficients; establishing connections to quasinormal mode spectra, stability under potential deformations, and holographic correlators via gauge/gravity duality.

Result: Key results include: (i) systematic mapping between grey-body factors and quasinormal mode spectra; (ii) robustness of grey-body factors under smooth deformations of the effective potential; (iii) characterization of transmission in traversable wormholes and superradiant scattering; (iv) extensions to modified gravity theories; (v) identification of bulk absorption probabilities as dual to boundary thermal correlators and transport coefficients in AdS/CFT.

Conclusion: Grey-body factors serve as central, unifying observables across black-hole physics, gravitational-wave phenomenology, quantum gravity, and holography; their systematic study enables quantitative tests of strong-field gravity and provides concrete bridges between scattering data, thermodynamics, and quantum field theory in curved spacetime.

Abstract: Grey-body factors describe the frequency-dependent transmission of fields through the effective potential generated by a black hole or another compact object. They convert the idealized thermal spectrum of Hawking radiation into the spectrum measured by an observer, determine absorption and scattering cross sections, and provide real-frequency observables complementary to quasinormal modes. In this review, we present a unified account of grey-body factors in black-hole physics and related systems. We formulate the scattering problem for asymptotically flat, de Sitter, and anti-de Sitter spacetimes, and discuss the connections between transmission coefficients, absorption cross sections, Hawking radiation, and ringdown. We also review methods for their computation and survey recent developments, including the correspondence between grey-body factors and quasinormal modes, their stability under deformations of the effective potential, wormhole transmission and superradiance, and their behavior in modified theories of gravity. Finally, we discuss the appearance of grey-body factors in gauge/gravity duality, where absorption probabilities in the bulk are related to correlation functions and transport coefficients in the boundary theory.

</details>


### [25] [Observational constraints on scalar-nonmetricity cosmology: the role of the connection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24402)
*Ghulam Murtaza,Avik De,Emmanuel N. Saridakis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares three symmetric-teleparallel connection branches in scalar-nonmetricity gravity using cosmological data (SNe Ia, BAO, CMB), finding Connection I statistically favored over ΛCDM under AIC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the geometric freedom—three inequivalent symmetric-teleparallel connections allowed even in flat FRW spacetime—in scalar-nonmetricity gravity yields distinguishable observational consequences.

Method: Systematic Bayesian parameter inference and model comparison across all three connection branches, assuming a steep scalar-field potential and power-law nonminimal coupling, using Pantheon+ & DES Y5 SNe Ia, DESI DR2 BAO, and compressed CMB data.

Result: All three branches fit the cosmic expansion history (including matter-to-DE transition), but exhibit distinct observational sensitivities: Connection I is most sensitive to SNe choice and yields Δχ²_min = −13.99 and ΔAIC = −5.99 vs. ΛCDM for DES Y5+CMB+DESI DR2; Connection II shows exceptional stability in reconstructed histories; Connection III is intermediate.

Conclusion: The choice of symmetric-teleparallel connection has observable, testable consequences; Connection I is preferred by AIC under current combined probes, challenging the uniqueness of ΛCDM as the simplest explanation.

Abstract: Scalar-nonmetricity gravity provides a natural scalar-tensor extension of symmetric teleparallel gravity, with the gravitational interaction encoded in nonmetricity. A distinctive feature is that, even for a spatially flat Friedmann-Robertson-Walker geometry, three inequivalent symmetric-teleparallel connection branches are allowed. Whether this geometrical freedom has observable consequences is therefore an important question. In this work we perform a systematic observational comparison of all three connection branches, considering a steep scalar-field potential and a power-law nonminimal coupling. We constrain the models using combinations of Pantheon Plus and DES Year 5 Type Ia supernovae, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations, and compressed cosmic microwave background information. We find that all three branches can reproduce the transition from matter domination to late-time acceleration, but their observational behavior is connection dependent. Connection I exhibits the strongest sensitivity to the supernova compilation, Connection II yields remarkably stable reconstructed histories, while Connection III shows an intermediate behavior. Most notably, for DES Y5+CMB+DESI DR2, Connection I gives $\Delta\chi^2_{\min}=-13.99$ and $\Delta{\rm AIC}=-5.99$ relative to $\Lambda$CDM, and is therefore favored by the Akaike information criterion.

</details>


### [26] [Static tidal Love numbers of Schwarzschild and non-Schwarzschild black holes in quadratic gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24625)

TL;DR: This paper computes the static electric quadrupolar (Love) response of black holes in 4D quadratic gravity (R − αC² + βR²), finding that Schwarzschild black holes have zero quadrupole Love number, while a numerically obtained non-Schwarzschild solution yields a small negative value (k₂^(rₕ) ≈ −0.1837) at α/rₕ² = 1/2; the analysis uses gauge-invariant Kodama–Ishibashi perturbations, enforces horizon regularity and absence of scalar tides to isolate the physical sector, solves three coupled radial equations via shooting/matching, and clarifies consistency with low-energy EFT expectations.
