  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether black holes in quadratic gravity exhibit nonzero static electric quadrupolar deformability (Love numbers), probing how higher-curvature corrections modify tidal response beyond general relativity — especially given the vanishing of Love numbers in GR and potential sensitivity to UV completion.

Method: Numerical solution of static, linearized metric perturbations in Kodama–Ishibashi gauge-invariant formalism; exclusion of scalar-curvature mode via horizon regularity and no applied scalar tide; reduction to three coupled second-order radial ODEs for the remaining tensor-type sector; boundary-value problem solved by shooting from horizon and matching at intermediate radius; definition and extraction of curvature-amplitude-based Love number λ_E and dimensionless tidal deformability k₂^(rₕ).

Result: Schwarzschild black holes in this quadratic gravity theory have exactly zero massless electric quadrupolar Love number; a nearby non-Schwarzschild vacuum solution yields a small negative value: λ_E/rₕ⁵ ≃ −0.1224928 and k₂^(rₕ) ≃ −0.1837391 at α/rₕ² = 1/2, with high numerical precision (outer-endpoint variation ~1.6×10⁻⁷); coupling between equations is essential and separation fails; the negative response vanishes near the Schwarzschild limit.

Conclusion: Black hole static quadrupolar deformability is generically nonzero in quadratic gravity, but only for non-Schwarzschild vacua — confirming that Love numbers are not universal and encode genuine higher-curvature physics; the result is consistent with EFT because curvature-squared terms are irrelevant in the low-energy expansion and do not appear in the leading effective action governing long-wavelength tides.

Abstract: We calculate the static electric quadrupolar response of black holes in four-dimensional quadratic gravity, with action $R-\alpha C^2+\beta R^2$. The Schwarzschild solution has zero massless gravitational Love number under the stated boundary conditions. A numerical non-Schwarzschild solution instead has a nonzero negative response. At $\alpha/r_h^2=1/2$, we find $\lambda_E/r_h^5\simeq-0.1224928$ and $k_2^{(r_h)}\simeq-0.1837391$, with a final outer-endpoint change in $k_2^{(r_h)}$ of about $1.60\times10^{-7}$. We explain how the background is obtained by shooting and how the tidal solution is selected by matching at an intermediate radius. The perturbations are written in Kodama--Ishibashi gauge-invariant variables. After the scalar-curvature perturbation is excluded by horizon regularity and the absence of an applied scalar tide, the remaining static sector has three coupled second-order radial equations. We define the curvature amplitudes in these equations and describe why simple attempts to separate them fail. The coupling scan shows a negative response approaching zero near the Schwarzschild intersection. We also explain why this full-theory result does not conflict with the removal of curvature-squared operators in a perturbative low-energy expansion.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe black-hole 'hair' — i.e., deviations from the no-hair theorem — via observable effects of localized astrophysical dark matter halos on orbital dynamics near black holes, particularly the ISCO radius.

Result: The ADM halo systematically reduces r_ISCO compared to the Schwarzschild case; computed values are r_ISCO ≈ 5.97 (r₀ = 2.0), 5.61 (r₀ = 10.0), and 5.53 (r₀ = 20.0), all satisfying r_ISCO ≤ 6; the inward shift increases with halo core radius.

Abstract: We investigate the geodesic structure of a static, spherically symmetric black-hole in the background of a localized Astrophysical Dark Matter (ADM)-halo. The halo is modeled by a core-radius ($r_0$), regularized density profile that vanishes at the event horizon and exponential factor ensures the finite total halo mass. We solved the Einstein field equations numerically with equation of state with $p=w\rho$ for stiff matter $w=1$ scenario. By analyzing the effective potential and its first and second derivatives, we evaluated the characteristic radius of the time-like innermost stable circular orbit ($r_{\text{ISCO}}$) of massive particle in the vicinity of hairy black-hole. Our numerical analysis is consistent with the recently proposed theoretical upper bound $r_{\text{ISCO}} \le 6$ normalized to unit mass black-hole. We showed that the ADM-halo shifts $r_{\text{ISCO}}$ inward with respect to the Schwarzschild (bald) scenario and the shifts are larger for larger halo core-radius. In particular our numerical finding enumerate $r_{\text{ISCO}}$ are approximately $5.97$, $5.61$, $5.53$ for $r_0=2.0,10.0,20.0$ respectively. We believe that our analysis with ADM-halo model shed light on probing black-hole hair through high-energy astrophysical observables.

</details>


### [28] [Complexification of the Kerr-AdS black hole thermodynamic phase transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24694)
*Fei Guo,Zhen-Ming Xu,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a complex-analytical framework using analytic continuation of the horizon radius to study thermodynamics and phase structure of high-dimensional singly spinning Kerr-AdS black holes, identifying zeroth-order transitions below the critical point and constructing a Widom line for supercritical crossover via Lee-Yang theory.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a systematic, rigorous method for analyzing black hole thermodynamic phase structure—particularly phase transitions and crossover behavior—in high-dimensional singly spinning Kerr-AdS spacetimes, where conventional real-variable approaches face limitations.

Result: A criterion for identifying phase transitions below the critical point; demonstration that zeroth-order transitions preserve the underlying thermodynamic topological structure; construction of a well-defined Widom line characterizing supercritical crossover in Kerr-AdS black holes.

Conclusion: Complex-analytical methods provide a powerful and unifying framework for black hole thermodynamics, enabling precise characterization of both subcritical phase transitions and supercritical crossovers, thereby extending standard thermodynamic concepts to gravitational systems.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish a systematic complex-analytical framework for investigating the thermodynamics of high-dimensional singly spinning Kerr-AdS black holes. By performing an analytical continuation of the black hole horizon radius into the complex plane, we map the thermodynamic behavior for the black hole to the zeros distribution of the corresponding analytical function. In the region below the critical point, this approach provides the criterion for identifying phase transitions and reveals that the zeroth-order phase transition does not alter the underlying thermodynamic topological structure of the black hole. In the supercritical region, based on the Lee-Yang theory of phase transition, we construct the Widom line to characterize the supercritical crossover behavior for the Kerr-AdS black holes. This study provides a new perspective on the thermodynamic analysis of black holes.

</details>


### [29] [Weak Cosmic Censorship in Einstein-Born-Infeld Black Hole: A Parameter-Space Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24731)

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: Einstein-Born-Infeld is a charged black hole solution of general relativity with Born-Infeld nonlinear electrodynamics, which has interesting physical features. It violates Bekenstein's entropy upper bound, and it can overcharge with respect to the Reissner-Nordström black hole. It presents an optimum situation to test the cosmic censorship conjecture, which is another important conjecture in black hole physics. In principle, this analysis requires the calculation of the self-energy of a test particle in the vicinity of the black hole, which, in general, is not feasible because of the electromagnetic nonlinearities. In this work, we propose an alternative approach that allows us to explore the whole parameter space of the Einstein-Born-Infeld black hole and test the validity of the cosmic censorship conjecture.

</details>

Result: A novel (5+7)-dimensional flat-space embedding of Kerr that is global (r > 0), asymptotically flat, and analytically simple; plus a generalization framework applicable to any spacetime admitting a Kerr-Schild-type metric decomposition.

Abstract: We construct a new isometric embedding of the Kerr spacetime into $(5+7)$-dimensional flat spacetime. It is asymptotically flat, covers the entire $r>0$ region and has a very simple form. We also show how this embedding can be generalized to any spacetime admitting a Kerr-Schild-like decomposition of the metric.

### [31] [A Casimir turning point as the beginning of a small flat universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24905)
*Alan Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a cosmological model where a flat three-torus universe contracts and bounces due to negative Casimir energy from Standard Model fields with antiperiodic boundary conditions; the bounce occurs at ~722 ℓₚ, where Higgs stability is marginally ensured by zero-point gauge fluctuations, and observational constraints (e.g., top mass, CMB geometry) test its viability as a beginning—not a cyclic bounce.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically viable, non-singular early-universe scenario without inflaton fields, using only known Standard Model physics and quantum vacuum effects (Casimir energy) in a compact topology, while addressing the singularity problem and Higgs metastability in extreme curvature.

Method: Analyzes quantum field theory on a contracting flat 3-torus with antiperiodic fermions; computes Casimir energy density, incorporates trace anomaly constraints, evaluates Higgs effective potential including zero-mode confinement by constant gauge field fluctuations, estimates survival probability of Higgs vacuum and torus shape under quantum backreaction, and derives CMB observables and Bayesian exclusion bounds from top-quark mass uncertainty.

Result: A contraction-to-expansion turn occurs at L ≈ 722 ℓₚ; Higgs remains stable with >50% probability if m_t^pole < 172.96 GeV; shape survives with 90% probability only if real Gaussian state peaks within 0.35 Hubble times of turn; preferred torus aspect ratios are ~16–18% anisotropic; CMB can probe shortest cycle only if 0.985–1.04 × last-scattering diameter; FCC-ee measurement of m_t > 173.7 GeV would strongly disfavor the model (Bayes factor ≤ 0.03).

Conclusion: The Standard Model in a compact antiperiodic torus can drive a quantum-gravitational beginning via Casimir repulsion, but it is highly constrained—requiring precise Higgs–top–gauge parameter alignment and specific geometric signatures; it is not a generic bounce but a fine-tuned, testable origin of spacetime.

Abstract: We study a flat three-torus universe whose contraction is turned around by the negative Casimir energy of Standard Model fields. The trace anomaly forbids conformal matter alone to turn an anisotropic universe around, so matter with $w<1/3$, most cheaply a positive potential, must supply the negative trace; with antiperiodic fermions the Standard Model supplies the negative energy, and the universe turns at an edge of 722 reduced Planck lengths. There the Higgs quartic coupling is negative, but zero-point fluctuations of the constant gauge modes confine the Higgs zero mode down to a finite-volume threshold $\lambda_c\approx-0.012$, below the Standard Model value $-0.0098$. The Higgs survives with probability above $1/2$ for a top-quark pole mass below $172.96$ GeV ($\bar m_t<163.16$ GeV; $\alpha_s(M_Z)=0.1180$), and with probability $\approx0.7$ for current data, given a curvature coupling $\xi_H\gtrsim0.08$ at the scale $\bar M_{\mathrm{P}}/722$. The turning point can hold only about five quanta of the lowest modes, whereas a reheating coupling in a time-symmetric past would produce radiation exceeding the Casimir energy by at least $10^{94}$, so we read the turn as a beginning rather than a bounce. Negative Casimir energy cannot hold the shape of the torus: the shape state can be real at most once, and for a real Gaussian state survival of the shape on both branches with $90\%$ probability requires that moment to lie within $0.35$ Hubble times of the turn. The natural state, real at the turn, freezes the torus into a nearly rectangular box whose sides typically differ by $16$-$18\%$. The microwave background can read this fossil only if the shortest cycle lies between $0.985$ and about $1.04$ last-scattering diameters. A top-quark pole mass above $173.7$ GeV, within reach of FCC-ee, would exclude this beginning (Bayes factor $\lesssim0.03$).

</details>


### [32] [Scalar fields in the background of a linear Vaidya spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24909)
*Arielle Schutz,Ratindranath Akhoury*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how black hole evaporation—modeled via a linearly evaporating Vaidya metric—affects the entanglement structure of massless scalar fields near the horizon, using semiclassical and modular-theoretic tools; it finds that evaporation induces a geometric phase in the vacuum, reducing CHSH correlations relative to the eternal black hole case, and modifies the thermofield double structure in both quasi-stationary and collapse-formed scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dynamical black hole evaporation alters the quantum entanglement structure of vacuum states near the horizon—beyond the static or eternal black hole idealization—particularly in light of information-theoretic probes like the CHSH inequality and modular theory.

Method: Semiclassical quasi-stationary approximation applied to massless scalar fields in ingoing linear Vaidya spacetime; conformal equivalence to eternal black hole in near-horizon limit enables modular-theoretic analysis; Bogoliubov transformation modified by evaporation-induced conformal factor; CHSH inequality used as quantitative entanglement witness; Wald’s collapse-adapted analysis extended to linear Vaidya geometry.

Result: Evaporation introduces a horizon-adiabatic geometric phase in the vacuum, altering its thermofield-double-like structure; CHSH correlation is suppressed by a factor dependent on the evaporation rate; for collapse-formed linear Vaidya black holes, the near-horizon state deviates from the eternal case—the thermal density operator acquires a mass-evolution correction, preserving but modifying the thermofield double form.

Conclusion: Black hole evaporation fundamentally reshapes vacuum entanglement near the horizon—not merely thermally, but through geometric-phase–driven coherence loss detectable via Bell-type inequalities; this implies that entanglement diagnostics must account for horizon dynamics, even in quasi-stationary regimes.

Abstract: We study, in a semiclassical, quasi-stationary approximation, the entanglement properties of massless scalar fields near the event horizon of a dynamical black hole modeled by an ingoing Vaidya metric with a linearly decaying mass. In the near-horizon limit, the geometry becomes conformally equivalent to an eternal black hole, allowing the use of modular theory to analyze vacuum entanglement. We first study the modifications to the entanglement across the bifurcation surface of such a black hole from that of an eternal one. The conformal factor accounting for the evaporation modifies the Bogoliubov transformation, inducing a phase in the thermofield-double-like structure of the global vacuum. This phase change of the vacuum state is a consequence of the adiabatic shift of the horizon. A quantitative measure of the entanglement change due to the phase is obtained through the Clauser-Horne-Shimony-Holt (CHSH) inequality. We compare the CHSH inequalities in the vacuum states for the static and the linear Vaidya black holes and find that the CHSH correlation quantity for the latter is decreased by a factor depending on the evaporation rate. Next, we discuss the changes that must be considered for the linear Vaidya black hole formed from gravitational collapse. For this purpose we adapt an analysis by Wald for the case of the Schwarzschild black hole. We find, as expected, the near-horizon structure and state do not approach that of the eternal black hole. Rather, the thermal factor is modified by a factor incorporating the change in the black hole mass due to evaporation. The entangled global vacuum state again has a thermofield double structure with the modification mentioned above.

</details>


### [33] [Bayesian Inference of Primary Hair from Black Hole Shadows in Generalized Proca Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24996)
*Jiao-Zhen She,Shu-Min Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole shadows in a rotating generalized Proca theory with primary vector hair, deriving a one-parameter Kerr deformation; it computes shadows and lensing via ray tracing, and applies Bayesian inference to EHT data of M87* and Sgr A* to jointly constrain vector hair, spin, and inclination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect horizon-scale observational signatures—particularly black hole shadows—to the existence and magnitude of primary vector hair in beyond-GR theories, using a well-defined exact rotating solution in generalized Proca theory.

Method: Analytical derivation of a one-parameter Kerr-deformed metric from a coupling slice in generalized Proca theory; determination of regularity conditions for the outer horizon; numerical null geodesic integration (ray tracing) to generate shadow and lensing images; Bayesian inference using shadow area-equivalent radius and axis ratio as observables, applied to EHT 2017 data of M87* and Sgr A*.

Result: The vector-hairy black hole yields noncircular, latitude- and spin-dependent shadow deformations that vanish in the static limit; ray-traced shadows show modest enlargement and altered lensing patterns; Bayesian analysis yields joint posterior constraints: M87* prefers low spin and exhibits strong spin–hair anticorrelation, while Sgr A* provides upper limits on both spin and hair, with inclination poorly constrained.

Conclusion: Primary vector hair produces distinctive, observable imprints on black hole shadows, enabling quantitative, horizon-scale tests of generalized Proca theory using current EHT observations—demonstrating that shadow morphology is a sensitive probe of fundamental vector field degrees of freedom.

Abstract: We investigate black hole shadows and observational constraints on primary vector hair in an exact rotating solution of generalized Proca theory. Selecting a coupling slice yields a simple, one-parameter Kerr deformation that is noncircular and depends on spin and latitude. The deformation vanishes in the nonrotating limit, while the vector field remains nontrivial. We determine the domain admitting a regular outer horizon and construct shadow and lensing images by numerical ray tracing. Ray-traced images show a modest enlargement of the shadow and changes in the surrounding lensing pattern. Bayesian inference using the shadow area-equivalent radius and axis ratio yields joint constraints on hair, spin, and viewing inclination from Event Horizon Telescope observations of M87* and Sgr A*. The M87* posterior favors relatively low spin and exhibits a pronounced spin--hair anticorrelation, while Sgr A* yields upper limits on spin and hair with its inclination remaining essentially unconstrained. These results connect the optical signatures of primary vector hair to quantitative horizon-scale constraints.

</details>


### [34] [Critical limits of accelerating Kerr--NUT spacetimes and the non-expanding Plebański--Demiański family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25031)
*Kenneth Hong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes degenerate-horizon limits of vacuum accelerating Kerr–NUT spacetimes with nonzero NUT charge, identifying a cubic criticality condition (a² = m² + ℓ², αm = 1) under which the near-horizon geometry is locally homothetic to the 'swirling universe'; it embeds extremal, Nariai, and cubic near-horizon metrics into the Plebański–Demiański family via curvature-sector classification and discusses coordinate transformations, scaling limits, and open questions on global regularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the geometric structure and universality of near-horizon geometries in accelerating, rotating, NUT-charged black holes, particularly at critical parameter regimes where horizons coalesce in nonstandard (cubic) fashion beyond standard extremal or Nariai cases.

Method: Analytical study using exact solutions of Einstein’s vacuum equations; projective and Killing coordinate transformations; near-horizon limiting procedures; classification within the Plebański–Demiański family via longitudinal curvature sign; critical scaling analysis near cubic degeneracy.

Result: At cubic criticality, the near-horizon geometry is locally homothetic to the swirling universe; extremal, Nariai, and cubic cases map bijectively to negative-, positive-, and zero-curvature sectors of the non-expanding Plebański–Demiański metrics; a cubic-adapted scaling shows leading-order metric independence from first-order horizon separations; an exceptional Nariai endpoint requires a distinct Killing chart.

Conclusion: Cubic degeneracy defines a new universality class for near-horizon geometries in accelerating Kerr–NUT spacetimes, enriching the taxonomy beyond extremal and Nariai types; however, global identifications and smooth compactification of the critical parent spacetime remain unresolved.

Abstract: We study degenerate-horizon limits of vacuum accelerating Kerr--NUT spacetimes with finite, nonzero NUT parameter. At cubic criticality, $a^2=m^2+\ell^2$ and $\alpha m=1$, a projective coordinate transformation relates the complete critical parent metrics by a local homothety. The cubic horizon has one finite axis and a noncompact angular end, and its near-horizon limit is locally homothetic to the swirling universe. Explicit four-dimensional coordinate transformations place the ordinary extremal, Nariai and cubic near-horizon metrics in the negative-, positive- and zero-curvature longitudinal sectors of the non-expanding Plebański--Demiański family. The Nariai correspondence includes an exceptional endpoint that requires a separate Killing chart. Under a cubic-adapted near-critical scaling, fixed first-order separations between the approaching horizons do not affect the leading metric. The global Killing identifications and regular completion of the critical parent remain open.

</details>


### [35] [Gravitational lensing effects on neutrino oscillations around a Schwarzschild black hole immersed in perfect fluid dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25053)
*Sojida Mannobova,Farruh Atamurotov,Ahmadjon Abdujabbarov,Phongpichit Channuie,Chengxun Yuan,Chen Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM) around a black hole modifies neutrino flavor oscillations in the weak gravitational lensing regime, finding that non-radial neutrino paths acquire PFDM-dependent phase corrections, altering interference patterns and flavor-transition probabilities—especially in the μ–τ channel—and retaining sensitivity to absolute neutrino mass scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize how perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM) surrounding a static, spherically symmetric black hole affects neutrino flavor oscillations in the weak gravitational lensing regime—motivated by the need to probe dark matter properties via high-energy astrophysical neutrinos and test quantum coherence in curved spacetime.

Method: Within a covariant quantum-mechanical framework, the authors derive neutrino oscillation phases for both radial and non-radial ultrarelativistic trajectories in the PFDM-modified Schwarzschild metric; compute flavor-transition probabilities for two coherent lensed paths; and analyze interference effects in two- and three-flavor mixing, including dependence on PFDM parameter λ, neutrino mass scale, mass ordering, and flavor channel.

Result: Non-radial oscillation phases acquire λ-dependent corrections; flavor-transition probabilities (especially P_μτ) show strong sensitivity to λ; oscillation extrema shift non-monotonically with λ, amplitude and contrast vary with mass scale, lens mass, and ordering; path-interference terms encode absolute neutrino mass information.

Conclusion: PFDM induces measurable, configuration-dependent modifications to lensed neutrino oscillations, offering a novel probe of dark matter microphysics and neutrino properties; results lay groundwork for realistic observational modeling incorporating decoherence, averaging, detector resolution, and lensing magnification corrections.

Abstract: We investigate neutrino flavor oscillations in the weak gravitational-lensing regime of a static, spherically symmetric black hole surrounded by perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM). Within a covariant quantum-mechanical framework, we derive the oscillation phases for radial and non-radial propagation of ultrarelativistic neutrinos. While the leading radial phase is independent of the metric function, non-radial trajectories acquire impact-parameter-dependent corrections governed by the PFDM parameter $\lambda$. We construct the flavor-transition probability for two coherent lensed paths and analyze the resulting interference in two and three flavor scenarios. Varying $\lambda$ shifts the oscillation extrema and modifies their amplitudes and contrast, with a non-monotonic dependence on the neutrino mass scale, lens mass, flavor channel, and mass ordering. The $P_{\mu\tau}$ channel shows particularly strong sensitivity to the PFDM contribution for the considered configurations. Moreover, the path-interference terms retain information about the absolute neutrino mass scale. These results characterize PFDM-induced modifications of lensed neutrino oscillations and provide a basis for more realistic treatments including decoherence, averaging, detector resolution, and corrections to lens trajectories and magnifications.

</details>


### [36] [Destinies of Plasmoids Formed from the Magnetic Reconnection Occurring within the Ergo Region of a Rotating Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25097)
*Ho-Yun YuChih,Ye Shen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the Comisso-Asenjo process—energy extraction from rotating black holes via magnetic reconnection in accretion flows—and identifies a critical spin threshold (a = 0.8005) below which energy extraction fails, even with arbitrarily high plasma magnetization, by rigorously analyzing the geodesic escape condition of the positive-energy plasmoid (p₊).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior studies of the Comisso-Asenjo process established the necessity of negative-energy plasmoids (p₋) but neglected the dynamical requirement that the positive-energy counterpart (p₊) must escape to infinity; this work fills that gap by systematically evaluating p₊'s geodesic fate.

Method: Geodesic analysis in Kerr spacetime: trajectories of p₊ are integrated numerically and analytically to determine escape conditions; the covering factor is redefined to count only escaping p₊ events, enabling quantitative assessment of energy-extraction probability as a function of black hole spin (a) and plasma magnetization.

Result: A strict lower bound on black hole spin is found: a_min = 0.8005; for a < a_min, no p₊ can escape regardless of magnetization, rendering energy extraction impossible; thus, black hole spin-down via successive reconnections halts at a ≈ 0.8005.

Conclusion: Successful energy extraction from a rotating black hole via magnetic reconnection requires not only the generation of negative-energy plasmoids but also the guaranteed escape of their positive-energy companions—a condition enforceable only above a critical spin threshold, fundamentally constraining black hole magnetospheric energetics and evolution.

Abstract: It is widely accepted that magnetic reconnections occur successively in black hole accretion flows, especially on the equatorial plane, owing to the formation of the equatorial current sheet. When magnetic reconnection takes place within the ergosphere of a rotating black hole, one of the ejected plasmoids (denoted as $p_-$) may acquire negative energy-at-infinity density, while the companion plasmoid (denoted as $p_+$) correspondingly carries the energy extracted from the black hole. This mechanism, known as the Comisso-Asenjo process, provides a viable astrophysical pathway for extracting energy from a rotating black hole. Generally speaking, two conditions have to be satisfied for successful energy extraction. First, $p_-$ is generated via magnetic reconnection with negative energy-at-infinity density. Second, the associated $p_+$, which carries the extracted energy, must escape to infinity. Though a number of studies have promoted the theory of the Comisso-Asenjo process, the escape of $p_+$ lacks consideration. In this work, we adopt geodesic approach to systematically examine the destinies of $p_+$ after ejection, thereby establishing the complete conditions for energy extraction from a rotating black hole via magnetic reconnection. We redefine the covering factor, for which only escaping $p_+$ are counted. This quantity reflects how probable the successful energy extraction occurs in an accretion system. Through analysis of this revised covering factor, we identify a lower bound on the black hole spin, $a=0.8005$, below which energy extraction via magnetic reconnection can never succeed, no matter how high the magnetization of the bulk plasma is. This limit suggests that the spin-down of a black hole caused by successive magnetic reconnections would terminate once the spin decreases to approximately 0.8005.

</details>


### [37] [A new regime of the C-metric: Super accelerating black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25110)
*Francisco Silva*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the super-accelerating regime of the charged C-metric at large acceleration, revealing a restricted polar coordinate domain that introduces a new symmetry axis and alters conical singularity structure and string/strut interpretation; it derives a simplified Komar mass $M_K = 1/(4A)$ when the south-pole conical defect is removed, and identifies novel near-extremal limiting geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the geometric and physical implications of the charged C-metric in the previously unexplored super-accelerating (large acceleration) regime, particularly how coordinate restrictions modify global structure, conical singularities, mass definitions, and extremal limits.

Method: Analytical study of the charged C-metric spacetime in the large-acceleration limit; coordinate analysis to identify the restricted polar domain; examination of conical singularity structure and its reinterpretation in terms of cosmic strings/struts; computation of Komar mass with careful normalization of the timelike Killing vector; asymptotic analysis of near-extremal limits to uncover novel limiting geometries.

Result: 1. Polar coordinate restriction to a subinterval of $(0,\pi)$ introduces a new axis of symmetry; 2. Conical singularity structure—and thus the physical string/strut interpretation—is modified; 3. Removing the south-pole conical defect yields Komar mass $M_K = 1/(4A)$ (up to Killing vector normalization); 4. New near-extremal limiting geometries are identified in this regime.

Conclusion: The super-accelerating regime of the charged C-metric exhibits qualitatively distinct global geometry and physical interpretation compared to standard regimes; the emergence of a new symmetry axis and simplified mass formula signals a fundamental reorganization of spacetime structure, with implications for acceleration horizons, thermodynamics, and the classification of exact electrovacuum solutions.

Abstract: We define and investigate the super accelerating regime of the charged C-metric, which arises at large acceleration. In this regime, the polar coordinate must be restricted to a smaller interval within $(0,\pi)$, and the resulting boundary defines a new axis of symmetry. This changes the conical singularity structure of the spacetime and, consequently, its string/strut interpretation. We further show that, when the conical defect at the south pole is removed, the Komar mass takes the simple form $M_K=1/(4A)$, up to the normalization of the time translation Killing vector. Finally, we explore the near extremal limits of this regime, identifying new limiting geometries.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: The longstanding issue of whether horizonless compact objects can mimic black holes at horizon scales is addressed, a problem that persists because the shadow diameter alone is degenerate across a wide class of geometries. For slowly rotating traversable wormholes, we precisely formulate the optical appearance as a set of observables derived from equatorial null geodesics, yielding necessary and sufficient conditions for a multi-parameter fingerprint. Using a new lapse function that interpolates between exponential and rational redshift profiles, we obtain the photon-sphere condition, the strong-deflection lensing coefficients, and the Lyapunov exponent in closed form. The equations yield two families of wormhole solutions dependent on logarithmic and exponential entropy parameters. We compare the resulting quantum-modified spacetimes with Kerr and four regular black holes. The shadow diameter is shown to be tunable to within $5\%$ to $11\%$ of Kerr for all models considered, so that $|R_{\rm sh}^{\rm WH}/R_{\rm sh}^{\rm Kerr}-1| \lesssim 0.11$ and the shadow alone cannot separate the models. The degeneracy is lifted when the rotational asymmetry, lensing coefficients, Lyapunov exponent, and orbital frequency are combined: for the $5$-component fingerprint $\mathbf{F}=(\Arot,\abar,\bbar, \Lya,\Oph)$, the fractional deviation $\Delta_i=(F_i^{\rm WH}-F_i^{\rm Kerr})/ F_i^{\rm Kerr}$ satisfies $|\Delta_i|\gtrsim 0.05$ for every component, with the largest departures in the second lensing coefficient and the Lyapunov exponent. The combined discrimination signal reaches a signal-to-noise ratio of about $3.8$ to $3.9$ against Kerr and about $3.7$ against the Kazakov--Solodukhin benchmark, which is sufficient to break the shadow-diameter degeneracy at the $10\%$ measurement level.

### [39] [Photon production at Kepler escape threshold](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25160)
*Michael R.R. Good*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives exact closed-form expressions for total photon count, angularly resolved spectrum, and radiated energy/momentum from a relativistic charged particle moving along a Newtonian Kepler parabola; finds finite, scale-independent photon emission with characteristic asymptotic scalings in rapidity, contrasting IR behavior between parabolic (finite) and hyperbolic (divergent) trajectories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the infrared (IR) structure of bremsstrahlung radiation in the critical parabolic limit—where relativistic motion meets zero-binding-energy trajectories—and to provide exact analytic results beyond approximations used in standard synchrotron or dipole radiation models.

Method: Fully relativistic classical electrodynamics: computes the Liénard–Wiechert fields of a point charge on an exact Kepler parabolic orbit, integrates the spectral-angular energy flux over frequency and solid angle, and extracts photon number via quantized spectral density (using $N(\omega) = dE/d\omega / \hbar\omega$), leveraging analytic properties of the trajectory and Lorentz-covariant radiation formulas.

Result: Exact closed-form total photon count $N$, angularly resolved photon spectrum $d^2N/d\omega d\Omega$, total radiated energy $E$, and linear/angular momenta—all finite and analytic in subluminal speed at periastron; asymptotics $N \sim (7\alpha/\sqrt{6})\eta$, $E \propto e^{3\eta}$ at large rapidity $\eta$; IR-finite at exact parabola, but divergent for hyperbolae arbitrarily near threshold.

Conclusion: The parabolic trajectory constitutes a unique IR-finite bremsstrahlung threshold configuration in relativistic electrodynamics, exhibiting exponential energy hardening and linear photon growth with rapidity—distinct from elliptic (bound) or hyperbolic (unbound) cases—highlighting the role of orbital geometry in radiation finiteness and spectral shaping.

Abstract: We obtain an exact closed-form total photon count for a prescribed Newtonian Kepler parabola, with a fully relativistic treatment of its radiation. We also obtain closed expressions for the angularly resolved photon spectrum and elementary expressions for the total radiated energy and momenta. The count is finite, scale-independent, and analytic in subluminal speed at closest approach. At large maximum rapidity $\eta$, $N\sim(7\alpha/\sqrt6)\eta$, while $E\propto e^{3\eta}$ at fixed passage timescale: the radiation chiefly hardens. For slow motion at fixed coupling and closest distance, a nearby ellipse emits fewer photons but more energy and angular momentum per orbit. The hyperbolic photon count remains IR divergent arbitrarily close to threshold, while the count at the exact parabolic threshold is finite.

</details>

*Richard Dyer,Anuj Kankani,Gareth Arturo Marks,Sean T. McWilliams,Christopher J. Moore*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper refutes claims of a 'direct wave' originating near the event horizon during black hole ringdown, showing instead that early-time non-quasinormal-mode signal features are tied to the light ring—not the horizon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge recent suggestions—based on black-hole perturbation theory and effective-one-body models—that a measurable 'direct wave' in the ringdown originates from inside the light ring or near the event horizon.

Conclusion: Claims of a horizon-originating direct wave in ringdown are unsupported; the observable early signal is causally and dynamically linked to the light ring, reinforcing its role as the dominant scattering boundary in gravitational-wave emission.

Abstract: Based on black-hole perturbation-theory calculations and an effective-one-body picture of binary black hole mergers, it has been suggested that a direct wave may be present and measurable in the ringdown. Furthermore, it has been suggested that this signal might originate from substantially inside the light ring (or photon sphere) of the remnant black hole, perhaps even from close to the event horizon. In this paper, we present three independent strands of evidence against such claims. In the process, we show that there is indeed an early-time part of the ringdown signal that cannot be directly modeled by quasinormal modes. However, there is no evidence that this is a direct wave as it has previously been defined, and we show that the properties of this component are related to the remnant's light ring rather than its horizon.

</details>


### [41] [Probing parity-breaking black-hole geometry with extreme-mass-ratio inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25291)

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe parity violation in strong-field gravity by identifying gravitational-wave imprints of equatorial reflection symmetry breaking in EMRIs around conformal Kerr black holes—a test complementary to propagation birefringence.

Conclusion: Equatorial reflection symmetry breaking induces spin-enhanced, order-ε² phase effects in EMRI waveforms that are potentially detectable by space-based interferometers; however, quantitative constraints on ε remain conditional on improvements in radiation modeling and full Bayesian parameter estimation.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational-wave signatures of equatorial reflection symmetry breaking in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) around a parity-violating conformal Kerr black hole. For the circular-orbit branch connected to equatorial Kerr orbits, we derive the dynamics through second order in the dimensionless deformation parameter $\varepsilon$ and construct adiabatic inspirals and gravitational-wave signals using a mass-quadrupole prescription. Equatorial reflection maps $\varepsilon$ to $-\varepsilon$, making the polar displacement odd and the orbital frequency and energy even in the deformation. The induced first harmonic has a small direct contribution for the configurations studied, while second-order dynamical corrections accumulate over many cycles to produce appreciable dephasing. We find that rapid black-hole rotation enhances these phase effects and the associated noise-weighted waveform mismatch. For a one-year inspiral of a $10M_\odot$ object into a $10^6M_\odot$ black hole with $a/M=0.9$ and viewing inclination $\iota=2\pi/3$, the fixed-parameter mismatch reaches the comparison benchmark $\mathcal M=0.01$ at approximately $\varepsilon=10^{-3}$, without optimization over time, phase, or Kerr parameters. A single-parameter Fisher analysis also indicates improved local sensitivity to $\varepsilon$ at high spin. These results connect the reflection properties of the circular branch to accumulated source-phase effects and complement tests of propagation birefringence. The sensitivity estimates remain conditional on the adiabatic quadrupole model and the adopted comparison procedure; robust observational constraints require more accurate radiation modeling and a full parameter-estimation analysis.

</details>

TL;DR: This paper studies scalar field scattering and absorption by scalarized black holes in Einstein-scalar-Gauss-Bonnet (EsGB) gravity, comparing quartic and exponential scalar–Gauss-Bonnet couplings. Using numerical background solutions (Chebyshev pseudospectral method) and partial-wave analysis—cross-validated with geodesic/glory approximations and Schwarzschild limit—the authors find scalarization suppresses total absorption but yields coupling-specific angular signatures in differential scattering, especially around the glory peak, enabling potential discrimination between coupling models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish between different scalar–Gauss-Bonnet coupling functions (quartic vs. exponential) via observable scattering signatures off scalarized black holes, leveraging deviations from Schwarzschild behavior in absorption and differential scattering cross sections.

Result: Scalarization reduces total absorption cross section relative to Schwarzschild; quartic coupling induces angular deviations predominantly in intermediate scattering angles (70°–140°), leaving forward and glory regions nearly unchanged; exponential coupling modifies scattering across all angles and can enhance the glory peak beyond Schwarzschild levels in certain parameter regimes.

Conclusion: The angular structure of the differential scattering cross section—particularly the behavior of the glory peak—serves as a robust diagnostic to differentiate quartic and exponential EsGB coupling functions, offering a novel observational pathway to constrain scalar-tensor gravity theories.

Abstract: We systematically investigate the scattering and absorption of a massive scalar field by scalarized black holes in Einstein-scalar-Gauss-Bonnet gravity with quartic coupling $f(\phi)=\alpha(\phi^{2}+\beta\phi^{4})$ and exponential coupling $f(\phi)=\alpha(1-\operatorname{e}^{-\beta\phi^{2}})$. The background solutions are obtained numerically via a Chebyshev pseudospectral method, enabling a partial-wave analysis on purely numerical black hole backgrounds. The partial-wave results are cross-validated against geodesic and glory approximations and the Schwarzschild limit with excellent agreements. We find that scalarization suppresses the total absorption cross section relative to the Schwarzschild case, and produces distinctive signatures in the differential scattering cross section. A qualitative difference between the two coupling models is identified: for the quartic coupling model, deviations from Schwarzschild are concentrated in the intermediate-angle region $\sim(70^{\circ},140^{\circ})$, with comparatively small changes at forward angles and near the glory peak; for the exponential coupling model, deviations appear across the full angular range and substantially modify the glory peak, which in some parameter regimes can even exceed the Schwarzschild value. This distinctive angular pattern provides a potential diagnostic for distinguishing EsGB coupling functions from scattering observables.

</details>


### [43] [Interferometric trilemma for ultraviolet-softened gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25994)
*Hiroki Matsui*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines how ultraviolet-softened extensions of general relativity weaken gravity at short distances, showing that such weakening necessarily violates one of three interferometric consistency conditions—visibility amplification, decoherence-energy matching, or causal response—each violated by a distinct ghost prescription in quadratic gravity; thus, observed short-distance weakening would certify violation of a fundamental quantum-gravitational consistency condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify which fundamental property of the gravitational mediator must be sacrificed to achieve universal short-distance weakening of gravity in UV-softened extensions of general relativity, given the constraint imposed by a positive Källén–Lehmann spectral density.

Method: Analyzes Lorentz-invariant Gaussian mediators linearly coupled to the conserved stress tensor; derives and imposes three independent interferometric consistency conditions (non-amplified visibility, decoherence fixed by radiated energy from the difference source, and no advanced response between interferometers); evaluates how each ghost prescription in quadratic gravity (negative-norm, negative-energy, and fakeon) violates exactly one condition at tree level.

Result: Each of the three ghost prescriptions in quadratic gravity violates precisely one of the three interferometric conditions: negative-norm ghosts amplify visibility, negative-energy ghosts cause decoherence uncoupled from positive energy transfer, and fakeons produce advanced (acausal) fringe shifts; hence, any universal short-distance weakening implies violation of at least one condition.

Conclusion: Universal short-distance weakening of gravity certifies that its mediator violates at least one of the three interferometric consistency conditions—thereby turning proposed quantum gravity observables into diagnostic tests of the theory’s UV completion rather than direct evidence of quantumness.

Abstract: Ultraviolet-softened extensions of general relativity can weaken gravity at short distances, whereas a positive Källén--Lehmann spectral density forbids gravity from falling below Newtonian strength. Which property of the mediator must such a weakening sacrifice? For Lorentz-invariant Gaussian mediators coupled linearly to the conserved stress tensor, we formulate three independent interferometric conditions that jointly imply this bound: visibility is never amplified, decoherence is fixed by the energy radiated by the difference source, and no interferometer responds to a later one. At tree level, the three ghost prescriptions of quadratic gravity each violate exactly one: negative-norm ghosts amplify coherence, negative-energy ghosts decohere without positive energy transfer from the difference source, and a time-symmetric fakeon response produces advanced fringe shifts. Any universal short-distance weakening of gravity therefore certifies that its mediator violates one of these conditions, and the observables proposed to certify quantum gravity become consistency tests of its ultraviolet completion.

</details>


### [44] [Searching for lensed gravitational waves with unlensed templates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26080)

TL;DR: This paper introduces a fast, efficient two-image method for detecting gravitationally lensed gravitational-wave signals from binary mergers, leveraging existing SNR time series to avoid costly matched filtering with explicit lensed templates; it significantly improves detection sensitivity—recovering >97% optimal SNR for overlapping images under common lens models and boosting sensitive volume by 2–4× for strongly down-ranked events—while enabling coherent detection of sub-threshold separated image pairs near fold caustics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational lensing can produce multiple images of a binary merger signal, but standard matched-filter searches using unlensed templates miss or mischaracterize these lensed events—especially overlapping (interfering) or sub-threshold separated images—leading to reduced sensitivity and false dismissal due to inconsistency tests.

Method: The authors construct the matched-filter output for two-image lensed templates directly from the SNR time series of unlensed template searches, enabling a marginalized detection statistic without new matched filtering; they apply this to both overlapping (geometric-optics regime) and separated lensed images, incorporating lens models (point-mass, SIS, Chang-Refsdal, cored isothermal) and consistency restoration for overlapping cases.

Abstract: Gravitational lensing can split the signal of a binary merger into two images, which either overlap and interfere or arrive as separate events. We show that the matched-filter output of every two-image template can be straightforwardly constructed from the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) time series that searches already compute with unlensed templates. This enables a marginalized detection statistic that increases search sensitivity without additional matched filtering and is more than 150 times faster than an explicit lensed template bank. For overlapping images in the geometric-optics regime, our two-image method recovers $\gtrsim97\%$ of the optimal SNR from point-mass and singular isothermal lenses, compared with as little as $67\%$ captured by unlensed templates, with substantial gains also for Chang-Refsdal and cored isothermal lenses. Overlapping lensed signals moreover fail the signal-consistency test that is used to down-rank noise transients. Our two-image method restores the consistency and increases the sensitive volume by roughly $2$-$4\times$ for the strongly down-ranked signals. For separated images, our method enables an efficient coherent search for pairs in which neither image is significant alone. Near fold caustics, this increases the sensitive volume by factors of 1.8-2.6 over requiring two independent detections, and up to 1.6 over a targeted counterpart search. Our method is applicable to any matched-filtering search.

</details>


### [45] [Black hole free energy landscapes from constrained instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26094)
*Shan-Ping Wu,Jia-Zhou Liu,Yu-Xiao Liu,Shao-Wen Wei*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses gravitational path integral factorization to give a semiclassical interpretation of generalized black hole free energy landscapes at fixed temperature and angular velocity, deriving the constrained saddle action in terms of renormalized charges and entropy—including Wald entropy in higher-curvature f(Riemann) theories—without requiring small-deficit approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous semiclassical foundation for black hole thermodynamics—particularly the generalized free energy landscape—by leveraging gravitational path integral factorization and constrained instantons, extending beyond Einstein gravity to local parity-even f(Riemann) theories.

Method: Applies boundary integral transforms to express the partition function as an integral over constrained sectors labeled by partial-Neumann data on an auxiliary hypersurface; identifies paired regular subregion solutions as constrained instantons via independent variation; introduces Lagrange multipliers to enforce thermal/rotational matching across interfaces; computes constrained saddle action using renormalized Hamiltonian charges and horizon-cap entropy; generalizes to f(Riemann) theories by retaining full auxiliary interface data and employing Wald entropy.

Result: Derives the constrained saddle action I_constr = βF_gen = β(E_B − Ω_B^I J_B,I) − S, valid for nonextremal stationary black holes with asymptotic boundaries; confirms the same thermodynamic form holds for f(Riemann) theories (e.g., Einstein–Gauss–Bonnet, new massive gravity) with Wald entropy, without small-deficit expansions; verifies consistency with Schwarzschild, Kerr, 5D static AdS, and rotating BTZ black holes.

Conclusion: The gravitational path integral factorization yields a robust semiclassical framework for black hole free energy landscapes that unifies Einstein and higher-curvature gravities under a common thermodynamic structure, where entropy arises geometrically from the horizon cap and constraints encode thermal and rotational equilibrium.

Abstract: We apply gravitational path integral factorization to provide a semiclassical interpretation of generalized black hole free energy landscapes at fixed external temperature and angular velocities. Boundary integral transforms express the formal partition function as an integral over constrained sectors labeled by complete local partial-Neumann data on an auxiliary hypersurface. Independent variation establishes paired regular subregion solutions as constrained instantons, with Lagrange multipliers supporting lapse and tangential shift jumps that accommodate thermal and rotational mismatches. For selected nonextremal stationary families with an asymptotic boundary, the constrained saddle action takes the form $I_{\rm constr}=\beta\mathcal F_{\rm gen}=\beta(E_{\mathcal B}-\Omega_{\mathcal B}^{I}J_{\mathcal B,I})-S$, with $\beta=T^{-1}$. Here $E_{\mathcal B}$ and $J_{\mathcal B,I}$ are renormalized Hamiltonian charges and $S$ is the black hole entropy, whose contribution to the action comes from the regular horizon cap. Interior stationary points of the landscape correspond to smooth equilibrium saddles when the retained variations independently span the entropy and angular momentum directions. We extend the construction to local, parity-even $f(\mathrm{Riemann})$ theories without curvature derivatives by retaining the complete auxiliary interface data. The same thermodynamic form follows with Wald entropy, without a small-deficit expansion. Schwarzschild and Kerr black holes illustrate the Einstein construction, while five-dimensional static AdS black holes in Einstein--Gauss--Bonnet gravity and the rotating BTZ branch of new massive gravity provide explicit checks of the higher-curvature extension.

</details>


### [46] [Periodic orbits and gravitational-wave emission from extreme-mass-ratio inspirals around rotating charged black holes in nonlinear electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26113)

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies rotating, charged black holes in Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg nonlinear electrodynamics (EEH-NED), extending Kerr-Newman geometry with correction terms; it analyzes equatorial timelike geodesics, MBO/ISCO, E-L phase-space deformation, and gravitational-wave waveforms (h+, hx) for EMRIs, showing how spin a and NED charge q contract orbits, shift phase space, enhance zoom-whirl features, shorten periods, and amplify signals — supporting LISA/TianQin tests of the no-hair theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the no-hair theorem by exploring 'hair'-bearing black-hole solutions in nonlinear electrodynamics (NED) within general relativity, specifically via the Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg (EEH) model (s=2), and to assess observational signatures in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) for future space-based gravitational-wave detectors.

Method: Adopts the EEH-NED coupling in Einstein gravity; derives equatorial timelike geodesics and effective potential for rotating, charged NED black holes; computes marginally-bound orbits (MBO) and innermost stable circular orbits (ISCO) numerically; maps energy-angular-momentum (E-L) phase-space deformations; classifies orbits using (z,w,v) taxonomy; computes gravitational-wave polarizations h+ and hx via adiabatic approximation and quadrupole radiation.

Result: Increasing spin a and NED charge q cause inward contraction of bound orbits, shift the E-L phase-space toward lower angular momentum, enhance zoom-whirl behavior, shorten gravitational-wave periods, and increase waveform amplitudes.

Conclusion: EEH-NED black holes produce distinctive, parameter-dependent EMRI dynamics and gravitational-wave signatures that are detectable by LISA and TianQin, offering a concrete pathway to observationally probe deviations from the no-hair theorem and constrain nonlinear electrodynamics in strong-field gravity.

Abstract: Within general-relativistic descriptions, the no-hair theorem dictates that black-hole geometries possess just three intrinsic physical quantities: mass, angular momentum, and electric charge. Black holes in nonlinear electrodynamics (NED) are equivalent to those composed of anisotropic matter and can develop "hair" structures near the event horizon, serving as ideal testbeds for modified theories of gravity. Working within Einsteinian gravitational theory, we adopt a coupling construction between gravity and NED matter, focusing explicitly on the Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg (EEH) variant with the power-index parameter fixed at s = 2. In this framework, a rotating, charged NED black hole extends the Kerr-Newman geometry with extra correction terms. Treating spin a and NED charge q as primary control variables, we examine test-particle dynamics inside extreme-mass-ratio inspiral (EMRI) configurations. We derive equatorial timelike geodesics and the effective potential. Through numerical evaluation of marginally-bound orbits (MBO) and innermost stable circular orbits (ISCO), we explore how these parameters reshape the energy-angular-momentum (E-L) phase-space. Using the (z, w, v) integer-valued orbit taxonomy, we compute the two gravitational-wave polarization modes h+ and hx via the adiabatic approximation and quadrupole radiation prescription. Larger values for a and q drive inward contraction for bound trajectories while displacing the E-L phase-space toward smaller angular-momentum values. Periodic-orbit zoom-whirl signatures grow increasingly prominent, while larger parameter values shorten the waveform periods and enhance signal amplitudes. Our results provide theoretical support for space-borne gravitational-wave observatories including LISA and TianQin, enabling prospects for discriminating NED black-hole spacetimes and performing observational checks on the no-hair theorem.

</details>


### [47] [Local scale symmetry and black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26208)
*Malik Almatwi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole solutions coupled to a real scalar field under local scale symmetry, analyzing thermodynamic properties and scalar field distribution for both λ=0 and λ≠0 cases.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how local scale symmetry affects black hole solutions and scalar field dynamics, particularly examining thermodynamic quantities and spatial distribution of the scalar field.

Abstract: We derive solutions for a black hole coupled to a real scalar field under considerations of local scale symmetry. Starting with the simple case $(\lambda=0)$, we find the solutions and derive the black hole's temperature, entropy, and its effective mass, and observe an adiabatic accretion of the scalar field onto the black hole. As a result, the effective mass of the black hole grows. In the general case $(\lambda\ne 0)$, the situation becomes more intricate; The horizons (the vanishing points of $g_{tt}$) depend on the mass of the black hole, scalar field strength, $\lambda$ and other positive parameter $R_\phi$. In this case, we propose two possible behaviors: When the black hole's mass is small enough, the field is mostly concentrated far from the black hole, whereas when the black hole's mass is sufficiently large, it is mostly concentrated inside the black hole.

</details>

Main category: gr-qc
