  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive a physically meaningful retarded propagator on discrete causal sets that reproduces continuum field-theoretic behavior in curved spacetimes, and to understand the origin of any emergent curvature coupling.

Method: Constructs a link-based retarded propagator using the expectation value of the squared link matrix L² on sprinklings into 2+1D Minkowski, anti-de Sitter, and de Sitter spacetimes; analyzes its high-density limit, localization near null boundaries of Alexandrov intervals, and normalization; compares resulting kernels to continuum Klein–Gordon propagators.

Result: The propagator correctly reproduces the continuum retarded Klein–Gordon propagator for a nominally massless scalar with effective curvature coupling ξ_link = 1/6 in all three spacetimes; this value is not the 2+1D conformal coupling (1/8), and persists across 1+1D, 2+1D, and 3+1D results.

Conclusion: The recurring value ξ_link = 1/6 points to an intrinsic ultraviolet property of the causal-set link structure—possibly linked to the (1/6 − ξ)R term in local heat-kernel expansions—rather than a low-energy conformal or geometric coincidence.

Abstract: We study a link-based retarded scalar propagator on causal sets sprinkled into $2+1$-dimensional Minkowski, anti-de Sitter, and de Sitter spacetimes. The construction is based on the squared link matrix $\mathbf{L}^2$, whose expectation value counts two-link paths between fixed endpoints. In the high-density limit, the dominant contribution localizes near the intersection of the null boundaries of the Alexandrov interval, and the flat-space normalization remains unchanged in constant curvature. The resulting kernels agree with the corresponding continuum Klein-Gordon propagators for a nominally massless scalar with effective curvature coupling $\xi_{\rm link}=1/6$. Since the conformal coupling in $2+1$ spacetime dimensions is $1/8$, this value is not a conformal-coupling effect. Together with link-based results in $1+1$ and $3+1$ dimensions, where the same coefficient $1/6$ appears, this suggests that the coupling may reflect an ultraviolet property of the link structure, reminiscent of the combination $(1/6-\xi)\mathcal{R}$ in local heat-kernel expansions.

</details>


### [49] [Geodesics of the five-dimensional Kerr-(A)dS black hole in closed form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26242)
*Özgür Sarıoğlu,Enes Yılmaz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides a general closed-form solution to the Hamilton-Jacobi equation for geodesic motion in the five-dimensional Kerr-(A)dS black hole, unifying treatment across root structures and geodesic types (timelike/null), using Biermann–Weierstrass theory; it corrects prior errors in the inverse metric and radial potential, and expresses all coordinates via real formulas and elliptic integrals of the third kind.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior integration of the separated geodesic equations in 5D Kerr-(A)dS was case-by-case, dependent on the root structure of radial and polar polynomials; a unified, closed-form, manifestly real solution valid for all geodesic types and root configurations was lacking.

Method: Exploits the quartic nature of the radial and polar polynomials in dimensionless variables and applies the Biermann–Weierstrass theorem to obtain manifestly real parametric solutions for the radial and polar coordinates; time and azimuthal angles are obtained via quadratures reduced exclusively to elliptic integrals of the third kind; affine parameter follows similarly; corrections to previously published inverse metric and radial potential are incorporated.

Result: General closed-form, real-valued parametric solution for all coordinates (r, θ, t, φ, ψ) and affine parameter along timelike and null geodesics in 5D Kerr-(A)dS; all quadratures reduce solely to elliptic integrals of the third kind; corrected expressions for inverse metric and radial potential.

Conclusion: The geodesic motion in 5D Kerr-(A)dS admits a fully unified, analytic, and manifestly real solution independent of root classification—resolving a long-standing limitation in higher-dimensional black hole physics and enabling systematic study of orbits, observables, and stability.

Abstract: The Hamilton-Jacobi equation for geodesic motion in the five-dimensional Kerr-(A)dS black hole separates in Boyer-Lindquist coordinates, but the resulting first-order system has so far been integrated case by case, according to the real roots of the radial and the polar polynomial. We give the general solution in closed form. In dimensionless variables both polynomials are quartic, and a theorem of Biermann and Weierstrass then yields the radial and the polar coordinate as one pair of manifestly real formulas, parametrized by an arbitrary point of the orbit rather than by a turning point, and valid for timelike and null geodesics alike whatever the root structure. The time coordinate and the two azimuthal angles follow by quadratures that reduce to elliptic integrals of the third kind alone, and the affine parameter by one more of the same type. Along the way we correct the inverse metric and the radial potential as published.

</details>


### [50] [Analog regular black holes and black hole mimickers in a Bose-Einstein condensate: from the core to the light rings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26257)
*Valentin Pomakov,Stefano Liberati*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes an experimental scheme to emulate spherically symmetric regular black holes and mimickers in quasi-2D Bose–Einstein condensates, achieving exact horizon and surface gravity matching via acoustic geometry; it corrects light-ring shifts with tailored interaction profiles, derives fully realizable external potentials and interaction strengths, and provides experimentally feasible designs using ³⁹K condensates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe inner-horizon and inner-light-ring instabilities of regular black holes and mimickers in laboratory settings using analogue gravity in Bose–Einstein condensates.

Method: Acoustic metric emulation in a quasi-two-dimensional BEC: matching gravitational and acoustic line elements on an equatorial disc to fix velocity-to-sound-speed ratio; correcting residual sound-speed-induced light-ring shifts via engineered interaction profiles; deriving closed-form external potential and interaction strength from continuity, Bernoulli, and metric correspondence constraints; translating design into experimental feasibility criteria (confinement, healing length, three-body loss, flow stability).

Result: Exact reproduction of horizon locations and surface gravities; analytical correction of light-ring position shift; closed-form, tunable expressions for external potential and interaction strength parameterized by mass M and regularization length ℓ; three experimentally viable designs for ³⁹K BECs meeting all key physical constraints.

Conclusion: Regular black-hole spacetimes with controllable ℓ/M can be faithfully emulated in current quasi-2D BEC platforms, enabling lab-based investigation of inner-structure instabilities—particularly inner light-rings—previously inaccessible to observation or numerical simulation.

Abstract: We show how static, spherically symmetric regular black holes and black-hole mimickers with radius-dependent mass function $m(r)$ can be emulated in a quasi-two-dimensional Bose--Einstein condensate, with the aim of probing inner-horizon and inner-light-ring instabilities in the laboratory. Matching the acoustic and gravitational line elements on an equatorial disc fixes the ratio of radial flow velocity to sound speed and reproduces both horizon locations and surface gravities exactly. The analogue geometry nevertheless contains a residual radially varying sound-speed factor in $g_{tt}$, which shifts the light-ring positions and reduces the range of the regularization parameter $\ell/M$ for which a light-ring pair exists. We derive the interaction profile that removes this shift. Combining the metric correspondence with the continuity and Bernoulli equations, we then obtain closed-form expressions for the external potential and interaction strength realizing the full target spacetime, with $M$ and $\ell$ directly tunable through parameters of the potential. Finally, we translate the construction into experimental requirements (quasi-two-dimensional confinement, healing-length resolution, three-body lifetime, and stationary-flow lifetime) and present three fiducial designs achievable with present-day $^{39}\mathrm{K}$ platforms.

</details>


### [51] [Outer trapped surface formation from gravitational waves collision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26320)
*Junbin Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper rigorously constructs outer trapped surface formation in smooth, asymmetric vacuum spacetimes via collision of two short-pulse gravitational waves; the surface is built from two smoothly joined pieces with continuous outer null normals, and future incompleteness is proved by adapting Penrose’s singularity argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a symmetry-free, rigorous demonstration of outer trapped surface formation—key to gravitational collapse and cosmic censorship—in vacuum general relativity, inspired by Penrose’s earlier work on ultra-relativistic particle collisions.

Method: Constructs short-pulse, transversely localized, nearly plane-fronted gravitational wave initial data; glues two smooth null hypersurfaces along a curve ensuring C⁰ continuity of future-directed outer null normals; applies geometric analysis and characteristic initial value formulation to evolve the data and locate the outer trapped surface; adapts Penrose’s null geodesic incompleteness argument to prove future boundary singularities.

Result: Existence of a smooth outer trapped surface in the maximal future development of generic (non-symmetric) vacuum initial data; proof that the maximal development is future-inextendible as a Lorentzian manifold (i.e., singular), confirming formation of a black hole region without symmetry assumptions.

Conclusion: Trapped surface formation—and thus strong gravitational collapse—can occur robustly in vacuum spacetimes without symmetries, supporting the validity of the weak cosmic censorship conjecture in a broad dynamical setting.

Abstract: We give a new rigorous construction of outer trapped surface formation in smooth vacuum spacetimes without symmetry assumptions. The outer trapped surface is produced by the collision of two transversely localized, nearly plane-fronted gravitational waves described by short pulse data. The outer trapped surface is obtained by joining two smooth pieces along a curve with their future-directed outer null normals matching continuously. The incompleteness of the maximal future development is then established by adapting Penrose's original argument. This construction is inspired by Penrose's argument in constructing a marginally outer trapped surface in the collision of two ultra-relativistic particles modeled by the Aichelburg--Sexl solution.

</details>


### [52] [Primordial black holes in braneworld gravity: A gateway to hidden dimensions and the dark Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26482)
*Itzi Aldecoa-Tamayo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This thesis explores primordial black holes (PBHs) in the Randall–Sundrum Type II braneworld, showing that higher-dimensional effects strongly enhance early accretion and modify evaporation, enabling sub-Planck-mass PBHs to constitute all dark matter while satisfying current observational constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile PBHs as viable dark matter candidates within higher-dimensional gravity (Randall–Sundrum II), accounting for modified gravitational collapse, accretion, and evaporation dynamics absent in standard 4D cosmology.

Method: 1) Fully relativistic PBH accretion modeling using Michel theory within the covariant Shiromizu–Maeda–Sasaki formalism for braneworld gravity; 2) Re-evaluation of observational constraints (Hawking radiation, gamma-ray backgrounds, microlensing, CMB energy injection) incorporating dimensionally modified evaporation and updated survey data.

Result: Higher-dimensional effects boost early accretion onto light PBHs ∝ t^0.33, causing mass discrepancies orders of magnitude larger than 4D estimates by end of strong-accretion epoch; revised constraints identify viable parameter space where PBHs below the 4D Planck mass can fully account for dark matter.

Conclusion: PBHs in RS-II braneworld cosmology represent a physically consistent, observationally allowed dark matter candidate with distinct formation, growth, and evaporation histories—challenging conventional 4D PBH mass thresholds and signatures.

Abstract: This thesis investigates the cosmological dynamics of primordial black holes (PBHs) within the Randall--Sundrum Type II braneworld scenario. The work comprises a preliminary discussion on gravitational collapse in the small-black-hole limit at early cosmological epochs, and two principal studies. The first study presents a fully relativistic treatment of PBH accretion in the early Universe, employing Michel accretion theory within the covariant Shiromizu--Maeda--Sasaki formalism. The analysis reveals that higher-dimensional effects substantially enhance early-time accretion onto light PBHs, with discrepancies between our black hole masses and previous estimates growing with cosmic time as $t^{0.33}$ --- a difference that reaches several orders of magnitude by the end of the strong-accretion epoch. This finding fundamentally alters the mapping between the initial PBH mass spectrum and present-day populations. The second study reassesses the observational and cosmological constraints on higher-dimensional PBHs in Randall--Sundrum cosmologies. Constraints from direct Hawking radiation, unresolved diffuse gamma-ray backgrounds, microlensing surveys, and energy injection into the cosmic microwave background are reanalysed in light of the modified higher-dimensional evaporation dynamics and updated observational surveys. Taken together, these results identify viable regions of parameter space in which PBHs can account for the entirety of dark matter while remaining consistent with current observational bounds. Notably, due to the enhanced accretion epoch, PBHs capable of constituting a significant fraction of dark matter may originate from masses below the effective four-dimensional Planck scale. This thesis thus puts forward a dark matter candidate with a distinct evolutionary history and observational signatures today.

</details>


### [53] [GPU-accelerated Bayesian inference for diffracted gravitational wave signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26619)
*Zihao Lin,Jef Heynen,Mick Wright,Thomas C. K. Ng,Chris Van Den Broeck,Justin Janquart*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces Jim-lensing, a GPU-accelerated Bayesian inference framework for full-wave-optics gravitational lensing of gravitational waves, enabling 60× speedup over CPU-based methods while maintaining accuracy and stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Growing gravitational wave detection rates increase the likelihood of observing lensed events; however, wave-optics lensing parameter estimation is computationally prohibitive on CPUs, hindering significance assessment and model exploration.

Method: Implementation of a point-mass lens model within Jim—a high-performance GPU-based Bayesian parameter estimation pipeline—supporting end-to-end wave-optics likelihood evaluation and Bayes factor computation without surrogate or training data.

Result: Full parameter estimation under the point-mass lens hypothesis reduced from >10 hours (CPU) to ~10 minutes (GPU); validation confirms numerical stability and agreement with state-of-the-art CPU results.

Conclusion: Jim-lensing enables scalable, robust Bayesian and frequentist analysis of lensed gravitational waves, unlocking broader lens modeling and reliable significance quantification in upcoming observing runs.

Abstract: As the rate of gravitational wave detections continues to grow, the probability of observing a gravitationally lensed gravitational wave also increases. When the Schwarzschild radius of the lens is comparable in size to the gravitational wave wavelength, wave-optics effects induce a frequency- dependent modulation on the signal. CPU-based parameter estimation analyses for wave-optics lensing are computationally intensive, limiting the scale of studies to determine the significance of any potential lensing candidate. In this work, we implement a point-mass lens model in Jim, a high-performance GPU-based parameter estimation pipeline. This enables, for the first time, GPU-accelerated Bayesian inference in full-wave optics, including the computation of Bayes factors, without the need for training data. We call this framework Jim-lensing. Using this framework, we are able to speed up a full parameter estimation run under the point-mass lens hypothesis from more than 10 hours to the order of 10 minutes compared to the current state-of-the-art CPU-based pipeline. We show that our implementation is stable and obtains comparable results to this state-of- the-art pipeline. The substantial speed gain over existing CPU pipelines paves the way for analyzing gravitational wave data under a broader range of lensing models with greater capacity to produce robust frequentist significance estimates.

</details>


### [54] [Cosmological perturbations from localized sources: a gauge-invariant multipolar theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26623)
*Geoffrey Compère,Sk Jahanur Hoque*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a first-principles, gauge-invariant multipolar formalism for linearized gravitational fields in general FLRW cosmologies—beyond slow-motion or geometric-optics approximations—incorporating curvature tails, cosmic fluid coupling, and exact retarded solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend gravitational multipole theory beyond Minkowski and de Sitter spacetimes to arbitrary smooth FLRW cosmologies (e.g., ΛCDM), while retaining full retardation, gauge invariance, and physical interpretability of source moments—including coupling to the cosmic fluid—without relying on slow-motion or high-frequency approximations.

Method: Generalizes the Blanchet-Damour multipolar formalism to spatially flat FLRW backgrounds; constructs the full retarded metric and fluid perturbation from prescribed stress-energy and cosmologically stationary past field; defines gauge-invariant mass, current, and canonical fluid moments directly in position space; avoids nonlocal interior projections by bypassing scalar-vector-tensor decomposition; relates canonical multipole metric to standard cosmological perturbation variables.

Result: A complete, gauge-invariant, position-space multipolar expansion valid for arbitrary FLRW expansion histories; explicit identification of a novel cosmic fluid moment driving scalar response (vanishing only in Minkowski and pure de Sitter); analytical retarded solution including curvature tails in ΛCDM with radiation; recovery of known Minkowski/de Sitter limits; effective multipolar sources defined for sub-Hubble extended sources via Cartesian moment truncation.

Conclusion: The framework unifies post-Minkowskian multipolar methods with cosmological perturbation theory, enabling precise modeling of gravitational radiation and backreaction from localized sources (e.g., binaries, compact mergers) in realistic expanding universes—without ad hoc approximations—thereby improving theoretical foundations for gravitational-wave cosmology.

Abstract: We develop a first-principles description of the linearized gravitational field of localized sources in spatially flat Friedmann-Lema\^ıtre-Robertson-Walker universes with arbitrary smooth expansion histories. The construction determines the complete retarded metric and the induced perturbation of the surrounding cosmic fluid from prescribed source stresses and a cosmologically stationary past field. It includes all multipole orders and accounts for curvature tails, through which the field depends on the source's past history inside the light cone. Generalizing the Blanchet-Damour multipolar formalism, we identify gauge-invariant mass and current moments together with a canonical cosmic fluid moment that determines an independent scalar response. This response vanishes in Minkowski and pure de Sitter spacetime, although curvature tails persist in de Sitter. The gauge-invariant multipoles are obtained directly in position space. This gives an exterior description without the nonlocal spatial projection through the source interior required by the usual scalar-vector-tensor decomposition. We also relate the canonical multipole metric to the standard variables of cosmological perturbation theory. The retarded construction requires neither a slow-motion nor a geometric-optics approximation. We define effective multipolar sources by truncating the moments of slowly varying sub-Hubble extended sources at a prescribed Cartesian multipole order, recovering the earlier quadrupolar and octupolar models in de Sitter spacetime. We recover the known Minkowski and de Sitter multipolar fields and obtain an analytical representation for gravitational propagation in the $\Lambda$CDM model including radiation.

</details>


### [55] [A new class of rotating black hole solutions endowed with a scalar hair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26678)
*Hugo Candan,Philippe Grandclement*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper numerically constructs new rotating, hairy black hole solutions in a Horndeski scalar-tensor theory without curvature coupling; they deviate from Kerr, possess primary scalar hair from linear time dependence, admit a Killing horizon but violate circularity and the zeroth law of black hole thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct stationary, axisymmetric black hole solutions with scalar hair in a well-motivated scalar-tensor theory (Horndeski) that avoids pathological curvature couplings, and to explore deviations from Kerr geometry and black hole thermodynamics.

Method: Numerical construction using the Kadath spectral library; no metric ansatz is assumed—instead, the solution is obtained in adapted numerical coordinates; the scalar field has linear time dependence to generate primary hair.

Result: New non-Kerr, stationary, axisymmetric black hole solutions with primary scalar hair; they possess a Killing horizon but are non-circular and violate the zeroth law of black hole thermodynamics.

Conclusion: Primary scalar hair can coexist with stationarity and axisymmetry in Horndeski gravity without curvature coupling, yielding physically distinct black holes that challenge standard thermodynamic assumptions.

Abstract: We present new hairy stationary and axisymmetric black hole solutions. These solutions belong to a subclass of Horndeski scalar-tensor theories, in which there is no non-minimal coupling to curvature. The full rotating solutions are obtained numerically using the Kadath library based on spectral methods. The metric does not rely on an explicit ansatz but rather on the use of a set of numerical coordinates. Black holes obtained differ from Kerr in general, and depend on a new independent parameter, a primary hair, coming from a linear time dependence in the scalar field. These solutions admit a Killing horizon like the Kerr black hole, but are non-circular, and break the zeroth law of black hole thermodynamics.

</details>


### [56] [Gravitational fingerprints of a black hole solution in Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26692)
*Carlos F. S. Pereira,Araújo Filho,Marcos V. de S. Silva,N. Heidari*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole physics in Palatini Kalb–Ramond gravity, revealing that key orbital features (photon sphere, ISCO, critical impact parameter) remain Schwarzschild-like when expressed in terms of the orbital mass $\bar{M}$, while geometric and dynamical quantities (tidal forces, photon orbit instability, quasinormal modes, light deflection) scale with a radial deformation parameter $\sigma$, leading to observable degeneracies in black hole shadow fits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how modified gravity—specifically Palatini Kalb–Ramond theory with vanishing cosmological constant—affects black hole structure, particle dynamics, wave propagation, and observational signatures, distinguishing physical (orbital) mass from conserved mass and isolating geometric deformation effects.

Method: Analytical and semi-analytical analysis of geodesic motion, photon orbits, tidal tensors, light deflection (weak/strong lensing), black hole shadows, and linear perturbations (scalar, electromagnetic, Dirac, axial gravitational); use of third-order WKB for quasinormal mode estimation; comparison with time-domain evolution; explicit treatment of conical asymptotics and areal vs. orbital parametrization.

Result: At fixed orbital mass $\bar{M}$, photon sphere radius, ISCO, and critical impact parameter coincide with Schwarzschild values; radial frequency ratios are unchanged under fixed areal release and particle radii; proper distances, infall times, and orbital angles depend on $\sigma$; tidal eigenvalues scale as $\sigma^{-2}$, photon orbit instability exponent as $\sigma^{-1}$; light deflection incorporates conical asymptotics; shadow comparisons with Sgr A* and M87* show coupling degeneracy; scalar/EM/Dirac perturbations probe physical metric; axial gravitational perturbations admit exact Schwarzschild spectral map with oscillation/attenuation rates scaling as $\sigma^{-1}$; WKB matches time-evolution frequencies within 0.7%.

Conclusion: Palatini Kalb–Ramond black holes are 'Schwarzschild-mimetic' for orbit-based observables when parameterized by $\bar{M}$, but deviate in geometry-sensitive and time-domain observables governed by $\sigma$; this yields testable degeneracies in EHT shadow analyses and distinct imprints in tidal dynamics and quasinormal spectra.

Abstract: We investigate particle motion, optical observables, and linear wave propagation around a static black hole in Palatini Kalb Ramond gravity with a vanishing cosmological constant. The analysis distinguishes the conserved mass $M$ from the orbital mass $\bar{M}=M/\rho$ and isolates the radial deformation $\sigma^2=1-q/2$, where $q=\ell_2/(1-\ell_1)$. At fixed $\bar{M}$, the photon sphere, critical impact parameter, and innermost stable circular orbit retain their Schwarzschild values. Radial frequency ratios also preserve their Schwarzschild profiles for the same areal release and particle radii, while proper distances, infall times, and accumulated orbital angles depend on $\sigma$. For radial fall, the tidal eigenvalues acquire a factor $\sigma^{-2}$, and the instability exponent of the photon orbit scales as $\sigma^{-1}$. In addition, we examine light deflection in the weak and strong regimes with an explicit account of the conical asymptotics. The shadow comparison with Sgr A* and M87* exhibits a coupling degeneracy when the orbital mass and areal distance are set. Scalar, electromagnetic, and massless Dirac perturbations probe the physical metric, whereas a homogeneous axial gravitational sector with $\delta B_{\mu\nu}=0$ admits an exact Schwarzschild spectral map. In this sector, both oscillation and attenuation rates scale as $\sigma^{-1}$ at fixed $\bar{M}$. Third order WKB estimates agree with frequencies extracted from time evolution to within $0.7\%$ for the reported fundamental modes.

</details>


### [57] [Spectroscopy as a Probe of Spacetime Foam](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26766)
*A. G. Kim,P. E. Nugent,L. Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using spectral distortions—specifically, temporal coherence loss and spectral line broadening induced by quantum-gravitational spacetime foam—to constrain the foam's temporal variation exponent α, complementing spatial-variation constraints from imaging. It derives bounds from GRB 221009A MeV lines (α ≳ 0.63) and COBE/FIRAS CMB spectrum (α ≳ 0.52), finding them consistent with the holographic value α = 2/3; only a stringent TeV survival bound (α ≳ 0.74, requiring near-Planck-timescale foam decorrelation) would conflict with current observational windows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the temporal structure of quantum-gravitational spacetime foam — complementary to existing imaging-based constraints on its spatial structure — by identifying observable spectroscopic signatures of stochastic, achromatic arrival-time jitter induced by dynamical foam.

Method: Analyzes how spacetime foam-induced stochastic phase jitter modifies first-order temporal coherence g^(1)(τ), leading to spectral splitting into coherent core and incoherent pedestal; derives analytic expressions for spectral distortion, line broadening, and broadband deficits; applies to MeV gamma-ray lines from GRB 221009A and COBE/FIRAS CMB monopole spectrum; computes Bayesian/likelihood bounds on foam exponent α under dynamical-foam assumptions.

Result: GRB 221009A MeV lines yield α ≳ 0.63 (weak, lever-arm-limited); COBE/FIRAS CMB fit gives α ≳ 0.52 at 95% CL; next-gen CMB spectrometers forecast α ≳ 0.54–0.55, limited by foam–temperature degeneracy; line-width and CMB bounds are lower floors consistent with α = 2/3; only conditional TeV survival bound (α ≳ 0.74, requiring τ_foam ≲ 4×10⁻²⁸ s) would exclude the current imaging window.

Conclusion: Spectral coherence loss provides a viable, complementary channel to constrain temporal foam dynamics; current bounds are consistent with holographic expectations and do not exclude the GRB 221009A imaging window; stronger tests require either higher-energy (TeV+) sources with precise timing or breaking the foam–CMB temperature degeneracy.

Abstract: Quantum-gravitational spacetime foam varying in space and in time imprints, respectively, a transverse phase decoherence and an achromatic, stochastic arrival-time jitter on light from distant sources. We show that the jitter modifies the first-order temporal coherence $g^{(1)}(\tau)$ and hence the observed spectrum, providing a spectroscopic probe of the temporal variation, complementary to existing imaging-channel constraints on the spatial variation. In the dynamical-foam limit, where the foam decorrelates within the light's coherence time, the spectrum splits into a coherent line core suppressed by $e^{-\sigma_\phi^2}$, with $\sigma_\phi^2$ the variance of the foam-induced phase jitter, and an incoherent pedestal, yielding a spectral shape-distortion bound on the foam exponent $\alpha$. Three probes follow: the outright survival of broadband flux, the fractional width of a spectral line, and a chromatic deficit across a broadband continuum. Applied to the MeV emission lines of GRB\,221009A we obtain $\alpha \gtrsim 0.63$, a weak, lever-arm-dominated floor. For the broadband CMB the foam induces an $e^{-\sigma_\phi^2(\nu)}$ deficit ($\sigma_\phi^2 \propto \nu^2$); a fit to the COBE/FIRAS monopole spectrum reaches $\alpha \gtrsim 0.52$ ($95\%$ CL), and next-generation spectrometers reach only $\alpha\gtrsim 0.54$--$0.55$ (forecast), limited by the foam--temperature degeneracy. The pedestal-independent line-width and CMB bounds are floors below the holographic value $\alpha = 2/3$, consistent with and excluding no part of the imaging window reported for GRB\,221009A; only the conditional TeV survival bound ($\alpha\gtrsim0.74$), which holds only for near-Planck-refresh foam ($\tau_{\rm foam}\lesssim4\times10^{-28}$\,s), would exclude it --- and, with it, the window.

</details>


### [58] [Spherically symmetric solutions with dust and radiation sources in Schouten-Codazzi Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26775)
*Sebastián Nájera,Roberto A. Sussman,Juan Carlos Hidalgo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies exact solutions of Schouten-Codazzi gravity (SCG), a second-order metric theory extending GR by adding a Codazzi tensor to the Einstein equations. It analyzes spherically symmetric Vaidya and LTB spacetimes, showing how Codazzi constraints introduce two integration functions that yield an effective cosmological constant and extra dynamical freedom—modifying the Misner-Sharp mass in Vaidya case and yielding cubic evolution equations in LTB case, enabling accelerated expansion without dark energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether Schouten-Codazzi gravity—a geometric extension of GR incorporating a Codazzi tensor—can provide viable cosmological models without invoking dark energy, by examining exact solutions in physically relevant spacetimes.

Method: Analytical derivation of exact solutions for spherically symmetric geometries (generalized Vaidya and LTB) in SCG; enforcement of Codazzi compatibility conditions; identification and interpretation of integration functions arising from the Codazzi constraint; comparison with GR predictions (e.g., Misner-Sharp mass, Friedmann-like evolution).

Result: Codazzi tensor contributions generate two arbitrary integration functions that induce an effective cosmological constant and an extra degree of freedom; in Vaidya spacetime, they modify the Misner-Sharp mass; in LTB spacetime, they yield a cubic evolution equation enabling accelerated, ever-expanding solutions absent in standard GR.

Conclusion: Schouten-Codazzi gravity naturally accommodates cosmic acceleration and expanding cosmologies without dark energy, making it a compelling alternative framework for gravitational physics and cosmology.

Abstract: We examine in this paper exact solutions of Schouten-Codazzi gravity (SCG), a second order metric theory in which a Codazzi tensor is added to the Einstein field equations. We consider two spherically symmetric geometries that describe null radiation and inhomogeneous dust, corresponding to generalized Vaidya and Lemaître-Tolman-Bondi (LTB) spacetimes. The Codazzi tensor has to comply the Codazzi equations, this imposes restrictions on the geometry of spacetime that are determined up to two integration functions that naturally generate an effective ``cosmological constant'' and an additional degree of freedom. We show that for the Vaidya geometry, these Codazzi contributions modify the Misner-Sharp mass, while the LTB models have as an evolution equation a cubic polynomial whose coefficients depend on the Codazzi integration functions. This leads to a broader class of dynamics than those of General Relativity, and allows for an accelerated expansion and ever-expanding models without the introduction of ``dark energy''. Overall, our analysis suggests that Schouten-Codazzi gravity provides an appealing framework for applications in cosmology.

</details>

### [59] [Trapped-ion quantum simulation of (1+1)D SU(2) lattice gauge theory via loop-string-hadron formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24091)
*Toshiaki Kaji,Jesse R. Stryker,Yutaro Iiyama,Lento Nagano,Enrico Rinaldi,Christian W. Bauer,Koji Terashi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reports a quantum simulation of the 1+1D SU(2) lattice gauge theory with one fermion flavor on a trapped-ion quantum processor (Quantinuum H2), using the loop-string-hadron (LSH) formulation; it encodes both gauge (bosonic) and matter (fermionic) degrees of freedom on qubits, extends beyond the common j_max=1/2 electric flux truncation to j=1 via multiqubit bosonic registers, and demonstrates scalable circuit optimizations and accurate Trotterized dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance quantum simulation of physically realistic non-Abelian lattice gauge theories—particularly by overcoming limitations of low electric-flux truncation (e.g., j_max = 1/2) and by unifying bosonic gauge and fermionic matter encoding on qubits—enabling future extension to higher dimensions and more complex theories.

Method: Employs the loop-string-hadron (LSH) basis to map SU(2) gauge-matter Hilbert space onto qubits; represents electric flux j = 0, 1 (beyond j = 1/2) using a multiqubit register for bosonic degrees of freedom; implements Trotterized time evolution on Quantinuum H2 trapped-ion hardware; applies circuit-construction optimizations proven to scale polynomially with lattice size.

Result: Successful experimental realization of the SU(2) LGT in 1+1D with one fermion flavor on Quantinuum H2; accurate reproduction of Trotterized dynamics; demonstration of j = 1 electric flux sector using multiqubit bosonic encoding; validation of local gauge-field representation and scalable compilation strategy.

Conclusion: Modern trapped-ion quantum hardware can now support key structural features of realistic non-Abelian lattice gauge theories—including explicit local gauge fields, unified fermion–gauge encoding, and physically relevant electric-flux truncations—paving the way for scalable, high-fidelity simulations in higher dimensions.

Abstract: We present a quantum simulation of the SU(2) lattice gauge theory in 1+1 dimensions with one flavor of fermions, performed using the trapped-ion Quantinuum System Model H2. This work is based on the loop-string-hadron (LSH) formulation of the gauge theory, and is distinguished by using qubits to represent both bosonic-gauge and fermionic-matter degrees of freedom. Furthermore, we access the $j=1$ electric-flux sector beyond the $j_{\max}=1/2$ truncation commonly used in SU(2) quantum simulations. To that end, we digitize an electric-field degree of freedom using a multiqubit register for representing the bosonic quantum number. Our simulation therefore synthesizes several key ingredients relevant to quantum simulations in higher spatial dimensions, in particular explicit local gauge-field degrees of freedom represented here by a multiqubit bosonic register, while relying solely on circuit-construction optimizations that are expected to scale polynomially with lattice size. This work demonstrates that modern quantum hardware is increasingly able to accommodate the features of complicated but physically important lattice gauge theories while successfully reproducing the expected Trotterized dynamics.

</details>


### [60] [One-loop mixing matrix of the N=1 SYM Supercurrent operator upon renormalization on the Lattice using stout-smeared Links](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24632)
*Marios Costa,Elias Antoniou,Constantinos Costa,Salomi Dimou,Haralambos Panagopoulos*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper extends perturbative analysis of supercurrent renormalization and mixing in lattice N=1 SYM using stout-smeared links, SLiNC gluino action, and Symanzik-improved gauge actions; computes the one-loop mixing matrix—including gauge-variant operators—and studies how stout smearing affects mixing structure to aid SUSY restoration in the continuum limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To refine fine-tuning strategies for restoring supersymmetry in the continuum limit of lattice N=1 SYM, by systematically understanding how operator mixing—especially of the supercurrent—is affected by improved discretizations (stout smearing, SLiNC, Symanzik actions).

Method: Perturbative one-loop computation of the supercurrent mixing matrix in lattice N=1 SYM, using stout-smeared gauge links, the SLiNC fermionic action for gluinos, and a family of Symanzik-improved gauge actions; includes mixing with both gauge-invariant and gauge-variant operators.

Result: The full one-loop mixing matrix of the supercurrent is obtained, revealing the impact of stout smearing parameters on mixing patterns—particularly suppression or modification of unwanted gauge-variant contributions—compared to prior clover/Wilson setups.

Conclusion: Stout smearing alters supercurrent mixing in a controllable way, offering improved handles for fine-tuning toward supersymmetry restoration; the results establish a foundation for both higher-order perturbative and future nonperturbative studies of SUSY on the lattice.

Abstract: We extend the perturbative investigation of the renormalization and mixing patterns of the supercurrent operator in lattice ${\cal N}=1$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills (SYM) theory, using stout-smeared gauge links. We employ the SLiNC action for gluinos and a family of Symanzik improved gauge actions for gluons. Building upon previous studies employing clover-improved fermions and the Wilson gauge action, we compute the one-loop mixing matrix of the supercurrent operator among gauge-invariant and gauge-variant operators. Particular emphasis is placed on the structure of operator mixing in the presence of stout smearing parameters. Our results contribute toward refining fine-tuning strategies necessary for the restoration of supersymmetry in the continuum limit, and provide a stepping stone for future perturbative and nonperturbative investigations in supersymmetric theories on the lattice.

</details>


### [61] [Recent Advances in Transverse-Momentum--Dependent Distributions in Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24785)
*Huey-Wen Lin,Wei Wang,Yong Zhao*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent lattice QCD advances in extracting Transverse Momentum Dependent parton distributions (TMDs), highlighting methods like LaMET and Mellin-moment ratios, key results (e.g., Collins-Soper kernel, Boer-Mulders function), and challenges in continuum/infinite-momentum limits—aiming at first-principles 3D hadron structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable first-principles, ab initio determination of transverse-momentum–dependent parton structure of hadrons—essential for interpreting spin- and transverse-momentum–sensitive experiments at JLab, COMPASS, LHC, and the Electron-Ion Collider.

Method: Lattice QCD approaches including ratios of Mellin moments, large-momentum effective theory (LaMET), and Coulomb-gauge techniques to access TMD-related quantities (e.g., Collins-Soper kernel, soft functions, TMDPDFs, TMD wave functions).

Result: First lattice extractions of unpolarized and spin-dependent TMDs—including the Boer-Mulders function—and demonstration of feasibility to access transverse-momentum–dependent information directly from QCD; validation of key nonperturbative inputs like the Collins-Soper kernel.

Conclusion: Lattice QCD has matured into a viable tool for 3D hadron tomography via TMDs; however, systematic control over continuum limits, infinite-momentum extrapolations, and factorization uncertainties remains critical for precision physics at future facilities.

Abstract: Unraveling the three-dimensional structure of hadrons is a central objective in modern nuclear and particle physics. Transverse Momentum Dependent parton distributions (TMDs) provide crucial insights into the interplay between partonic transverse motion and spin, and are indispensable for describing a wide range of high-energy processes at facilities such as JLab, COMPASS, the LHC, and the upcoming Electron-Ion Collider. In recent years, significant progress has been made in accessing TMDs from lattice QCD using novel approaches. Methods based on ratios of Mellin moments, large-momentum effective theory (LaMET), and Coulomb-gauge techniques have enabled first-principles investigations of the Collins-Soper kernel, intrinsic soft functions, TMD wave functions, and TMDPDFs. Recent lattice results for both unpolarized and spin-dependent distributions, including the Boer-Mulders function, demonstrate the feasibility of extracting transverse-momentum--dependent information directly from QCD. This paper reviews recent advances in lattice-QCD studies of TMDs, outlines remaining challenges such as continuum and infinite-momentum extrapolations, and discusses future directions toward precision determinations of hadron structure in three dimensions.

</details>


### [62] [Expansion schemes for finite density QCD equation of state: which one is the best?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25494)
*Szabolcs Borsányi,Zoltán Fodor,Jana N. Guenther,Piyush Kumar,Paolo Parotto,Attila Pásztor,Chik Him Wong*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper compares convergence properties of two expansion schemes—based on constant baryon density vs. constant entropy density contours—for extrapolating the QCD equation of state to finite baryon chemical potential, using high-order susceptibilities (up to order 8) computed via extreme-statistics lattice simulations on a 16³×8 lattice; it finds the entropy-based method converges faster, requiring only up to fourth-order susceptibilities for μ_B ≈ 500 MeV, and maintains superiority in the full three-chemical-potential space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct lattice QCD simulations at finite baryon density suffer from the sign problem; thus, reliable extrapolation methods based on Taylor expansions in chemical potentials are needed—yet their convergence behavior is poorly quantified. The paper aims to identify which susceptibility-based contour scheme (baryon density vs. entropy density) yields optimal convergence for constructing the finite-density equation of state.

Method: High-precision lattice QCD simulations using extreme-statistics techniques on a 16³×8 anisotropic lattice with Wilson-clover fermions and tree-level Symanzik-improved gauge action; computation of baryon, electric, and strangeness susceptibilities up to eighth order; comparison of two contour-based reparameterizations: (i) constant normalized baryon density n_B/T³ and (ii) constant entropy density s/T³; convergence assessed by truncation error and coverage of physical μ_B range.

Result: The entropy-density-based contour method converges significantly faster: truncating at fourth-order susceptibilities suffices to accurately describe the equation of state up to μ_B ≈ 500 MeV; in contrast, the baryon-density-based method requires higher orders for comparable accuracy. This superiority persists in the full (μ_B, μ_Q, μ_S) parameter space.

Conclusion: Entropy-density contours provide a more robust and rapidly convergent framework than baryon-density contours for constructing the finite-density QCD equation of state via susceptibility expansions—making them preferable for phenomenological applications such as neutron star modeling and heavy-ion collision simulations.

Abstract: The finite density equation of state of QCD is a fundamental quantity for understanding the phase structure of the theory, and also for the realistic modeling of the QCD medium in simulations. While direct lattice simulations at finite density are extremely challenging, several expansion schemes have been devised to extrapolate into the QCD phase diagram. All of these schemes are based on susceptibilities of conserved charges, i.e. derivatives of the pressure with respect to the chemical potential(s). In this work we set out to determine which of these schemes possesses the best convergence properties. To this end, we employ extreme-statistics lattice simulations on a $16^3\times8$ lattice, which allow us to obtain susceptibilities up to order eight with remarkably small errors. We compare in particular two methods, based on contours of constant (normalized) baryon density and of constant entropy density. We find that the latter converges more quickly, effectively requiring susceptibilities only up to order four to cover the chemical potential range up to at least $\mu_B \approx 500$ MeV. We also extend the comparison to the three dimensional space of chemical potentials, and find that the entropy-based method consistently shows superior convergence.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

### [63] [Electric Flux as a Criterion for String Breaking and Schwinger Pair Production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24175)
*Sebastian Grieninger*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper defines string breaking via midpoint electric field dropping to half a flux unit in the massive Schwinger model, links it to entanglement entropy peaks through binary flux uncertainty, identifies emergent encoded Bell pairs near breaking and vacuum transitions, reveals shared entanglement spectral structure between string breaking and θ-vacuum transitions, and constructs high-fidelity wavefunctions using RBM ansatz.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the quantum information structure underlying string breaking and vacuum transitions in 1+1D QED (Schwinger model), particularly how entanglement signatures encode physical configurations (intact vs. screened string) and topological sectors (θ-vacua).

Method: Analytical energy-balance argument for string-breaking separation; numerical lattice simulations to probe entanglement entropy, capacity of entanglement, entanglement asymmetry, and Robustness of Magic; restricted Boltzmann machine (RBM) variational wavefunction ansatz for constructing string and θ-vacuum states.

Result: String breaking occurs at midpoint electric field = 0.5 flux units; entanglement entropy peak arises from maximal binary uncertainty between intact/screened configurations; near-breaking and vacuum-transition regimes host approximate encoded Bell pairs reflecting flux screening status; string-breaking and θ-vacuum transitions share minimum in capacity of entanglement and matching entanglement spectra; entanglement asymmetry and Robustness of Magic signal coherent rearrangement of degrees of freedom; RBM ansatz achieves high-fidelity reconstruction of both string and θ-vacuum wavefunctions.

Conclusion: String breaking and θ-vacuum transitions are unified through shared quantum information structures—particularly binary flux encoding, emergent Bell-pair formation, and universal entanglement spectrum features—suggesting deep connections between confinement dynamics and topological vacuum structure in gauge theories.

Abstract: We define string breaking by the midpoint electric field falling to half a flux unit. In the massive Schwinger model, an energy balance between intact string and screened configuration predicts this separation using only vacuum observables and meson masses as input. We observe numerically that this half flux point corresponds to equal weights of the two configurations thus explaining the observed peak of the entanglement entropy as maximal uncertainty of the binary flux. The same binary uncertainty explains the entanglement peak at the vacuum transition. Near breaking, screening organizes part of the spatial entanglement between the string interior and exterior into a nearly maximally entangled bit. Within the selected flux sectors, this approximate encoded Bell pair records whether the flux is screened and nearly separates from the remaining correlations. The vacuum transition exhibits the same structure. The string and the $\theta$-vacuum reveal a matching structure of the capacity of entanglement minimum at their transition point, linking their entanglement spectra. The transition rearranges degrees of freedom coherently as revealed by the entanglement asymmetry and Robustness of Magic of disjoint subregions. Finally, we construct the $\theta$-vacuum and string wavefunctions with high fidelity using a restricted Boltzmann machine ansatz.

</details>


### [64] [Axion identification in multi-phase models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24028)
*Zhen-Yu Lei,Jian-Wei Cui*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a systematic method to identify the physical axion in multi-phase models (e.g., DFSZ-like), showing it emerges as an effective field on an S¹ orbit after gauge identification and integrating out heavy phase fields; while the axion locally coincides with the lightest mass eigenstate, its global properties (period, domain-wall number) are rigorously determined from the UV Lagrangian—yet topological defects (e.g., strings with gauge flux or derivative-heavy configurations) cannot be fully captured by the single-axion reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Axion identification in multi-phase models is ambiguous because local mass eigenstates do not encode global topological data (e.g., fundamental period or domain-wall number); existing approaches fail to consistently relate low-energy axion properties to the UV multi-field structure and defect physics.

Method: Two-step reduction: (1) gauge identification — quotienting the compact phase-field space by gauge orbits; (2) integrating out heavy phase fields — restricting low-energy dynamics to nonlinear sigma-model orbits; analyzed explicitly in two representative two-phase models under derivative expansion and stated symmetry assumptions.

Result: The physical low-energy field space is a finite union of S¹; the axion is uniquely identified as the Goldstone mode on one such S¹ orbit, matching the lightest mass eigenstate near vacuum; its fundamental period and domain-wall number are calculable from the original Lagrangian; however, certain topological defects (e.g., axion strings carrying gauge flux or configurations requiring heavy-field derivatives) evade the single-axion description.

Conclusion: A well-defined axion emerges in multi-phase models via geometric field-space reduction, enabling robust prediction of periodicity and domain-wall number—but the single-axion effective theory is incomplete for topological defect physics, revealing intrinsic distinctions between multi- and single-phase UV completions.

Abstract: Axion identification in multi-phase models is subtle because local mass eigenstates do not by themselves determine global properties such as the fundamental period and domain-wall number. In this paper, we develop a route to identify the axion in a broad class of multi-phase models, including DFSZ-like theories. The construction involves two reductions: gauge identification and integrating out heavy phase fields. We study these reductions explicitly in two representative two-phase models. Gauge identification reduces the compact field space of the phase fields to a quotient space by identifying points connected by gauge transformations, while integrating out heavy phase fields restricts the low-energy dynamics to nonlinear orbits. Under the stated assumptions and within the validity of the derivative expansion, the physical low-energy field space forms a finite union of $S^1$. The axion is identified as the effective field on an $S^1$ orbit and coincides locally with the lightest mass eigenstate near the vacuum. Its fundamental period and domain-wall number are well-defined and can be determined from the original Lagrangian. Nevertheless, the reduction to a single axion does not capture all properties of topological defects in the original multi-phase theory. In particular, axion strings can retain nontrivial gauge flux, while special defect configurations can involve non-negligible spacetime derivatives of the heavy phase fields and invalidate the single-field reduction. These effects represent fundamental differences between multi-phase and single-phase theories at the level of topological defects.

</details>


### [65] [Testing Leptogenesis via CP asymmetries in Lepton-Number-Violating $W$ Decays at Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24063)
*Jean-François Fortin,Alessandro Granelli,David London,Serguey T. Petcov,Nicolas Sanfaçon*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores CP asymmetries in rare W-boson decays (W± → e±e±(q̄q′)∓) as a probe of low-scale leptogenesis in the GeV-scale type-I seesaw model with quasi-degenerate heavy neutrinos; it identifies viable parameter regions where both successful baryogenesis and observable collider CP violation at HL-LHC/FCC-hh coexist.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test low-scale leptogenesis via CP-violating observables at colliders, specifically using rare CP-asymmetric W decays mediated by heavy right-handed neutrinos — bridging cosmological baryogenesis and high-energy experiment.

Method: Analytical calculation of CP asymmetry in W± → e±e±(q̄q′)∓; numerical scan over presently-allowed parameter space of the type-I seesaw; solution of density matrix equations for leptogenesis; determination of minimum observable branching ratio for given statistical significance at HL-LHC and FCC-hh.

Result: Identifies sizeable regions of parameter space where (i) the observed baryon asymmetry is reproduced via low-scale leptogenesis, and (ii) the CP asymmetry in the specified W decay is statistically observable at HL-LHC and FCC-hh.

Conclusion: Observation of CP asymmetry in W± → e±e±(q̄q′)∓ within the identified parameter regions would constitute strong evidence for low-scale leptogenesis as the origin of the matter–antimatter asymmetry.

Abstract: In principle, searches for CP asymmetries in rare $W$-boson decays can test low-scale leptogenesis in the GeV-scale type-I seesaw with three quasi-degenerate-in-mass heavy right-handed neutrinos. To investigate this, we focus on the lepton-number-violating heavy-neutrino-mediated decay $W^{\pm}\to e^{\pm} e^{\pm} (\bar{q} q')^{\mp}$, with $(\bar{q} q')^+ = (\bar{d}u)^+$ and $(\bar{q} q')^- = (\bar{u}d)^-$. We determine the minimum branching ratio required for a CP asymmetry to be observable at colliders with a given statistical significance. We then perform a numerical scan of the presently-allowed parameter space, also solving the density matrix equations for low-scale leptogenesis, and identify regions where, simultaneously, the present baryon asymmetry of the Universe can be achieved and the collider CP asymmetry for the considered decay is observable at HL-LHC and FCC-hh. We find that these regions are sizeable. A future observation of a CP asymmetry in $W^{\pm}\to e^{\pm} e^{\pm} (\bar{q} q')^{\mp}$ within the regions identified here would provide evidence in favour of low-scale leptogenesis as the origin of the matter-antimatter asymmetry of the Universe.

</details>


### [66] [BeAGLE++: A modern modular framework for eA physics at the EIC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24095)
*Arjun Kumar,Abhay Deshpande,Zhoudunming Tu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: BeAGLE++ is a new Monte Carlo event generator for lepton-nucleus collisions, extending BeAGLE with an improved time-ordered intranuclear cascade and statistical de-excitation to better model final-state interactions and nuclear breakup.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve modeling of hadronic final-state interactions (FSIs) and nuclear breakup in high-energy lepton-nucleus collisions—critical for precision physics at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC)—by addressing limitations in the legacy BeAGLE generator.

Method: BeAGLE++ implements a formation-zone intranuclear cascade with discrete, time-ordered treatment of hadronic interactions and light-cluster production, followed by statistical de-excitation of the residual nucleus; it retains modular architecture for extensibility and model testing.

Result: Comparisons with E665 data and legacy BeAGLE show broad agreement on inclusive observables but reveal significant differences in FSI-sensitive final states—enabling assessment of model dependence and constraints on cascade dynamics.

Conclusion: BeAGLE++ provides a flexible, physics-motivated simulation framework for EIC studies and serves as a platform for validating and developing new nuclear interaction models against existing and future experimental data.

Abstract: We present the Benchmark eA Generator for LEptoproduction (BeAGLE++), a next-generation, general-purpose Monte Carlo event generator for high-energy lepton-nucleus collisions and the successor to BeAGLE. The framework incorporates a formation-zone intranuclear cascade to describe hadronic final-state interactions (FSIs), followed by statistical de-excitation of the residual nucleus. Compared with the legacy implementation, the BeAGLE++ cascade introduces a discrete time-ordered treatment of hadronic interactions and light-cluster production during intranuclear transport. Comparisons with E665 measurements and legacy BeAGLE calculations show broad agreement for selected observables, while revealing differences in final states sensitive to FSIs. These differences provide opportunities to assess the model dependence of nuclear-breakup predictions and constrain the underlying cascade dynamics. With its modular architecture, BeAGLE++ provides a flexible simulation framework for the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) and a platform for developing and testing new physics models against existing data and future EIC measurements.

</details>


### [67] [Collider event distances from the phase space sphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24319)
*Nathaniel Craig,Brian Henning,Andrew J. Larkoski,Iñigo Valenzuela Lombera*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a distance-based classification method leveraging Lorentz-invariant phase space topology and reconstructed masses to distinguish collider processes; includes a polynomial-time approximation for scalability; demonstrates strong performance on challenging benchmarks like 4t vs. tt̄+jets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish collider processes (e.g., rare signal vs. dominant background) using the full geometric structure of phase space—not just kinematic variables—especially where traditional observables fail.

Method: Introduces a metric on Lorentz-invariant phase space that preserves reconstructed invariant masses; defines event-level distances based on global topological structure; develops a polynomial-time approximation algorithm to compute these distances efficiently.

Result: Achieves high discrimination power in simulated benchmarks, notably fully hadronic four-top production versus t̄t + jets—a notoriously difficult fully-hadronic classification task.

Conclusion: Phase-space geometry, when properly encoded via invariant metrics and scalable approximations, provides a robust and physically grounded basis for high-fidelity event classification in collider physics.

Abstract: Can the distribution of collider events in phase space be used to distinguish the processes that produced them? We present a distance-based classification method that uses the global topology of Lorentz-invariant phase space and retains reconstructed masses. To tackle particularly challenging classification tasks that may benefit from higher event resolution, we develop a polynomial-time approximation to compute the distances. The resulting classifiers achieve strong discrimination across several simulated benchmarks, including fully hadronic four-top production against $t\bar t+\text{jets}$.

</details>


### [68] [Common origin of the Belle-BESIII line-shape difference of $Λ_c^+\barΛ_c^-$, $D_s D_{s1}$ and $D_s D_{s2}^*$ from $e^+ e^-$ annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24634)
*Cheng-Xi Liu,Tian-Le Gao,Cheng-Ping Shen,Xiang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes radiative production of higher charmonia (e.g., η_c(6S), χ_c2(5P)) as a unified explanation for Belle–BESIII line shape discrepancies in e⁺e⁻ → Λ_c⁺Λ̄_c⁻, D_sD_{s1}(2536), and D_sD_{s2}^*(2573), leveraging ISR luminosity normalization effects and enabling searches for missing higher charmonia at Belle II.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resolve persistent Belle–BESIII discrepancies in cross section line shapes for charmed baryon and meson pair production, where Belle observes enhancements absent or suppressed in BESIII data.

Method: Introduces radiative production of higher charmonia (η_c(6S), χ_c2(5P)) via initial-state radiation; incorporates luminosity normalization effects specific to Belle’s ISR analysis; uses BESIII vector charmonium data to constrain background contributions.

Result: A pseudoscalar η_c(6S) component explains the Λ_c⁺Λ̄_c⁻ enhancement; a tensor χ_c2(5P) component simultaneously accounts for enhancements in both D_sD_{s1}(2536) and D_sD_{s2}^*(2573) channels.

Conclusion: Radiative higher-charmonium production provides a consistent, physics-motivated explanation for the observed discrepancies; predicts observable signatures for discovery of η_c(6S) and χ_c2(5P) at Belle II.

Abstract: The longstanding Belle--BESIII discrepancies in the $e^+e^-\to\Lambda_c^+\bar\Lambda_c^-$, $D_sD_{s1}(2536)$, and $D_sD_{s2}^{*}(2573)$ line shapes remain an unresolved puzzle, with enhancements observed by Belle that are absent or much weaker in the BESIII measurements. In this work, we propose a new mechanism based on the radiative production of higher charmonia to explain these differences. Such processes may contribute to Belle's initial-state radiation samples, and the luminosity normalization increases their contribution to the extracted cross sections. With the vector contributions scaled by BESIII data, a pseudoscalar charmonium $\eta_c(6S)$ contribution can describe the baryonic enhancement, while an introduced $\chi_{c2}(5P)$ contribution can describe the enhancements in both meson channels. This mechanism offers a possible common explanation for these differences and, as a bonus, suggests a way to search for missing higher charmonia at Belle II.

</details>


### [69] [Global analyses of PDFs in the precision era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24691)
*Juan M. Cruz-Martinez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper discusses the evolution of global fits for parton distribution functions (PDFs), highlighting advances from partial NNLO to approximate N³LO theoretical accuracy and the challenges in maintaining reliable uncertainty quantification amid growing data complexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the increasing complexity and scale of experimental data (from fixed-target, HERA, Tevatron, and LHC) and the concurrent advancement in perturbative QCD accuracy, requiring robust methodologies for PDF determination and uncertainty estimation.

Method: Global QCD analysis combining diverse high-energy scattering data with state-of-the-art perturbative calculations—progressing from partial NNLO to approximate N³LO—and employing statistical fitting frameworks with careful treatment of systematic and theoretical uncertainties.

Result: Contemporary PDF fits achieve approximate N³LO accuracy; however, reconciling higher-order theory with heterogeneous datasets while preserving statistically sound uncertainty bands remains a key challenge.

Conclusion: Sustained progress in PDF determinations hinges on tighter integration of advanced perturbative calculations, consistent treatment of experimental systematics, and rigorous validation of uncertainty propagation across evolving theoretical and experimental landscapes.

Abstract: Parton distribution functions or PDFs are determined through global fits to an ever-increasing array of data, including deep-inelastic scattering at fixed-target experiments and HERA, and hadron collisions at the Tevatron and LHC. As the dataset has expanded, theoretical accuracy has improved from partial next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) coverage about 20 years ago to approximate N$^3$LO global fits today. The fitting collaborations must reconcile these complex calculations and data analyses while ensuring that quoted PDF uncertainty bands remain reliable.

</details>


### [70] [Where are the higher excited $B$ and $B_s$ mesons with coupled-channel effects?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25125)
*Ru-Hui Ni,Jia-Jun Wu,Xian-Hui Zhong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies higher-wave B and Bs mesons using an unquenched quark model, finding that coupled-channel effects significantly lower masses and alter level orderings—especially for B(2³P₀) and B(2³D₁)—and predict narrow states observable in specific hadronic decay channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the newly observed B_{s0}^*(5700)^0 and systematically assess coupled-channel (unquenched) effects on the mass spectra and strong decays of higher radial and orbital excitations of B and B_s mesons.

Method: Unquenched quark model incorporating OZI-allowed two-body strong decays and coupled-channel effects; mass spectra and decay widths computed for 3S-, 2P-, 2D-, and 1F-wave B/B_s mesons.

Result: Coupled-channel effects substantially reduce masses and reshape level spacings: B(2³P₀) mass drops below quenched predictions and dominantly decays to Bπ; mass ordering of B(2³D₁) and B(3³S₁) is inverted, with B(2³D₁) lighter and decaying mainly to B₁(5721)π; several higher excitations are predicted narrow and detectable in B^{(*)}π, B^{(*)}K, B₁(5721)π/K, and B₂^*(5747)π/K.

Conclusion: Coupled-channel effects are crucial for quantitatively describing excited B/B_s meson spectra and decays; the predicted states—especially B(2³P₀) and B(2³D₁)—offer clear experimental signatures to test unquenched dynamics in heavy-light systems.

Abstract: Inspired by the newly observed $B_{s0}^*(5700)^0$, whose mass agrees well with our previous unquenched prediction for $B_s(1^3P_0)$ [Phys.~Rev.~D \textbf{109}, 116006 (2024)], we study the mass spectra and OZI-allowed two-body strong decays of the higher $3S$-, $2P$-, $2D$-, and $1F$-wave $B$ and $B_s$ mesons in the same unquenched quark model. Our results show that coupled-channel effects significantly lower the masses and modify the relative level spacings. The scalar state $B(2^3P_0)$ is strongly affected: its mass is lowered well below the quenched quark model predictions, and it mainly decays into $B\pi$. Furthermore, the mass ordering of the vector states $B(2^3D_1)$ and $B(3^3S_1)$ is reversed, and the lighter $B(2^3D_1)$ state mainly decays into $B_1(5721)\pi$. To test these results, the $B(2^3P_0)$ and $B(2^3D_1)$ states are worth searching for in the $B\pi$ and $B_1(5721)\pi$ channels, respectively. In addition, some higher excitations are predicted to be relatively narrow and can be searched for in the $B^{(*)}\pi$, $B^{(*)}K$, $B_1(5721)\pi/K$, and $B_2^*(5747)\pi/K$ channels. These predictions provide useful references for future experimental searches and help clarify the role of coupled-channel effects in the heavy-light meson spectrum.

</details>


### [71] [Measurement of the neutrino cross section across the TeV scale using IceCube data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24068)
*Rasmi Hajjar,Mauricio Bustamante,Sergio Palomares-Ruiz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents the most precise measurement to date of muon-neutrino-nucleon charged-current cross sections from 150 GeV to 40 TeV using eight years of IceCube through-going muon neutrino data, achieving sub-20% uncertainties and enabling first separate TeV-scale measurements of neutrino vs. antineutrino cross sections — all consistent with Standard Model predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reduce large (20–40%) uncertainties in TeV-scale neutrino-nucleon cross sections, probe nucleon structure in poorly constrained regimes, and search for beyond-Standard-Model signatures where current measurements lack precise, continuous coverage across sub-TeV to tens-of-TeV energies.

Method: Analysis of eight years of through-going muon neutrino events in IceCube; energy-dependent unfolding of muon-neutrino-nucleon charged-current cross sections; separation of neutrino and antineutrino contributions via event topology and kinematics; comparison with SM predictions (e.g., CT18 PDFs + electroweak corrections).

Result: Cross sections measured with <20% uncertainty across 150 GeV–40 TeV; first evidence for separable ν and ν̄ cross sections above 1 TeV; average ν+ν̄ cross section agrees with SM; unified calibration bridging accelerator-based and astrophysical neutrino data.

Conclusion: The results establish a new benchmark for TeV-scale neutrino-nucleon interactions, marking the transition of high-energy neutrino physics into the precision era and constraining both nucleon structure models and potential BSM effects.

Abstract: Neutrinos interact with matter exclusively through the weak force, resulting in extremely small cross sections that are difficult to measure. At TeV energies, neutrino-nucleon interactions probe the structure of the nucleon in regimes where our knowledge of it is less complete, and signatures of physics beyond the Standard Model could manifest. Recent measurements using collider, atmospheric, and astrophysical neutrinos have begun to explore this regime, but no single measurement has provided precise coverage from sub-TeV to tens of TeV, and uncertainties remain at the level of about 20-40%. Here we present a determination of the muon-neutrino-nucleon charged-current cross sections as a function of energy across 150 GeV-40 TeV using eight years of through-going muon neutrino data collected by the IceCube Neutrino Observatory. We measure the average neutrino-antineutrino cross section, enabling direct comparison with previous results, and find, for the first time above 1 TeV, mild indications that the neutrino and antineutrino cross sections can be measured separately. The measured cross sections are consistent with Standard Model predictions and are determined with uncertainties below 20% throughout the explored energy range, achieving the most precise characterization of neutrino-nucleon interactions across the TeV scale to date. These results provide a unified measurement of neutrino-nucleon cross sections in this energy regime, connecting accelerator-based and previous higher-energy neutrino observations - thereby advancing high-energy neutrino physics into the precision era.

</details>


### [72] [Breaking the axion abundance-isocurvature relation: Scale invariance and high-scale inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24070)
*Mariaveronica De Angelis,Javier Rubio*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a scale-invariant mechanism to decouple the QCD axion's relic abundance from its primordial isocurvature fluctuations, resolving CMB constraints on high-scale inflation in the classic axion window; it achieves this via a dynamical field-space metric set by a field ratio, and realizes it explicitly in a Higgs-Dilaton model yielding an α-attractor inflaton and an ultralight axion testable via black-hole superradiance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between the classic QCD axion dark matter window and high-scale inflation imposed by CMB isocurvature bounds—arising because the same decay constant governs both relic density and primordial fluctuations.

Method: Introduces a scale-invariant class of models where the axion's kinetic normalization (and hence field-space metric) is dynamically set by a field ratio, breaking the fixed-decay-constant link; implements this in the Higgs-Dilaton model via deformation of the PQ kinetic term, yielding an α-attractor inflaton near the Starobinsky point and preserving adiabaticity via exact scale and PQ symmetries.

Result: Enables high-scale inflation compatible with the classic axion window without fine-tuning the misalignment angle or requiring super-Planckian decay constants; yields a super-Planckian present-day decay constant (anthropic window), an ultralight QCD axion, and predictions testable via black-hole superradiance.

Conclusion: The dynamical field-space metric mechanism successfully decouples axion abundance from isocurvature, reviving the classic axion DM scenario under high-scale inflation while embedding it in a predictive, symmetry-protected framework with observational signatures.

Abstract: A QCD axion accounting for dark matter through pre-inflationary misalignment is strongly constrained by CMB isocurvature bounds, which place the classic axion window in tension with high-scale inflation. The tension arises because the same decay constant controls both the relic abundance and the primordial axion fluctuations. We show how this link can be broken in a broad class of scale-invariant models, without introducing any dimensionful parameter into the classical action. Rather than imposing a fixed Peccei-Quinn scale, the axion's kinetic normalisation is set by a dynamical field ratio. The resulting field-space metric controls the abundance through its value today and the primordial fluctuations through its value at horizon crossing. A sufficiently large metric during inflation suppresses isocurvature, allowing high-scale inflation in the classic axion window with an untuned misalignment angle and sub-Planckian decay constants. The exact scale and Peccei-Quinn symmetries also enforce a non-turning inflationary attractor, conserving the super-Hubble curvature perturbation and preserving the single-field adiabatic spectrum. We realise the mechanism in the Higgs-Dilaton model by deforming the Peccei-Quinn kinetic term. The same deformation turns the inflaton sector into an $\alpha$-attractor pinned near the Starobinsky point. This explicit realisation has a super-Planckian present decay constant, which places it in the anthropic window, now reopened at high inflationary scales, with an ultralight QCD axion constrained by black-hole superradiance.

</details>


### [73] [Phenomenology of Spontaneous CP-violation in 2HDMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24404)
*Tanmoy Mondal,Kei Yagyu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores spontaneous CP violation (SCPV) in the two-Higgs-doublet model (2HDM), emphasizing non-decoupling Higgs dynamics and flavor-misaligned Yukawa couplings—particularly in the up-type quark sector—to generate the CKM phase. It identifies testable deviations: ~−10% in h→γγ, ~−4% in h→Zγ, and O(100)% corrections to the trilinear Higgs coupling; predicts viable flavor-violating top-charm Yukawa interactions yielding distinctive LHC signatures (same-sign tt, 3t, scalar cascades); and establishes correlations between Higgs self-coupling deviations and neutral Higgs decay patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of CP violation and observed CP-violating phenomena in the quark sector via spontaneous CP violation (SCPV), using the two-Higgs-doublet model (2HDM) as a minimal, theoretically consistent framework where SCPV is realized without fine-tuning and with testable phenomenology.

Method: Analyzes SCPV in a CP-violating 2HDM with non-decoupling additional Higgs bosons and flavor-misaligned Yukawa couplings—specifically aligning down-type quark and lepton Yukawas while allowing up-type flavor violation to reproduce the Kobayashi-Maskawa phase. Imposes combined theoretical (e.g., vacuum stability, perturbativity) and experimental constraints (electroweak precision data, flavor observables, EDMs, LHC Higgs and BSM searches) to scan and constrain the parameter space.

Result: A sizable flavor-violating top-charm Yukawa coupling remains allowed, leading to observable LHC signatures: same-sign top-quark pairs, three-top production, and scalar cascade decays. The trilinear Higgs self-coupling deviation strongly correlates with decay modes of additional neutral Higgs bosons (e.g., H/A → t c̄ + h.c.), linking precision Higgs physics and direct BSM searches.

Conclusion: Probing SCPV requires a multi-pronged strategy: synergistic precision measurements of the 125 GeV Higgs (especially γγ, Zγ, and λ_hhh), direct searches for additional Higgs bosons at the LHC, and complementary constraints from flavor physics and electric dipole moments.

Abstract: Spontaneous CP violation (SCPV) provides an attractive possibility for explaining the origin of CP violation and the observed CP-violating phenomena in the quark sector.
