We investigate SCPV in the two-Higgs-doublet model as one of its simplest realizations, in which the additional Higgs bosons are necessarily non-decoupling and flavor misalignment in the Yukawa sector is required. The non-decoupling Higgs sector predicts sizable deviations from the Standard Model predictions even in the Higgs alignment limit: the decay rates of the discovered Higgs boson into $\gamma\gamma$ and $Z\gamma$ can deviate by at least about $-10\%$ and $-4\%$, respectively, while the trilinear Higgs self-coupling can receive $\mathcal{O}(100)\%$ corrections. We consider a scenario in which the down-type quark and charged-lepton Yukawa matrices are flavor aligned, while flavor-violating interactions are allowed in the up-type quark sector to reproduce the Kobayashi-Maskawa phase. We explore the viable parameter space by imposing theoretical constraints together with experimental constraints from electroweak precision observables, flavor measurements, electric dipole moments, and LHC data. We find that a sizable flavor-violating top-charm Yukawa coupling remains allowed, giving rise to distinctive LHC signatures, including same-sign top-quark and three-top production as well as scalar cascade decays. We also find that the deviation in the trilinear Higgs self-coupling is correlated with the decay patterns of the additional neutral Higgs bosons, providing a connection between precision Higgs measurements and direct searches for the additional Higgs bosons. Our results demonstrate the importance of combining searches for additional Higgs bosons with precision Higgs measurements, complemented by flavor and electric-dipole-moment experiments, to probe SCPV.

</details>


### [74] [Forward $χ_c$ production in $pp$ and $pA$ collisions within the CGC+NRQCD framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24527)
*Zhi-Guo He,Hao-yu Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies forward χcJ production in pp and pA collisions at the LHC using NRQCD + CGC, focusing on the ratio Rχc = σχc2/σχc1 and feed-down fractions to J/ψ; predictions agree with LHCb pPb data but overshoot pp data, and while using experimental J/ψ cross sections improves agreement for feed-down, fully theoretical predictions fail to simultaneously describe χc and prompt J/ψ — revealing a fundamental limitation in the current NRQCD+CGC framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in simultaneous theoretical description of χcJ and prompt J/ψ production in small-system and nuclear collisions, particularly in the low-p⊥ forward region where small-x dynamics and quarkonium formation mechanisms are critical.

Method: NRQCD factorization combined with CGC effective theory; small-x evolution implemented via running-coupling Balitsky-Kovchegov (rcBK) equation with multiple initial conditions (e.g., McLerran-Venugopalan); computation of χc1,2 differential cross sections, ratio Rχc, and feed-down fractions Fχc1,2→J/ψ; comparison to LHCb pPb and pp data.

Result: Rχc is theoretically robust against rcBK initial condition choices; predictions match LHCb pPb data but overestimate pp data; feed-down fractions agree with data only when experimental prompt J/ψ cross sections are used in denominator — fully theoretical CGC+NRQCD yields systematic underprediction of J/ψ and overprediction of χc, preventing unified description.

Conclusion: The NRQCD+CGC framework, while qualitatively successful for χc ratios and pA collisions, fails to self-consistently predict both χcJ and prompt J/ψ production simultaneously — indicating missing or mis-modeled non-perturbative inputs (e.g., LDMEs, feed-down treatment, or small-x saturation scale dependence) that must be addressed for a unified quarkonium production picture.

Abstract: We study the forward $\chi_{cJ}$ production in the low $p_{\perp}$ region in proton-proton $(pp)$ and proton-nucleus $(pA)$ collisions at the LHC within the framework of non-relativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization and the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective field theory. Incorporating the small-$x$ gluonic evolution via the running-coupling Balitsky-Kovchegov equation with various initial conditions, we systematically analyze the cross section ratio $R_{\chi_{c}}=\sigma_{\chi_{c2}}/\sigma_{\chi_{c1}}$, and find that the theoretical predictions weakly depend on these inputs. Our predictions align well with the LHCb measurements for $p\mathrm{Pb}$ collisions, however, overshoot those for $pp$ collisions. We further investigate the feed down fractions $F_{\chi_{c1,2}\to J/\psi}=\sigma_{\chi_{c1,2}\to J/\psi}/\sigma_{J/\psi}$. We notice that with the experimental measurement of prompt $J/\psi$ cross section as inputs in the denominator the CGC+NRQCD predictions broadly match the data when choosing the standard McLerran-Venugopalan initial conditions. In contrast, implementing purely theoretical CGC+NRQCD predictions for both $J/\psi$ and $\chi_{c}$ production yield systematic discrepancies across all tested parameters. This highlights a persistent challenge in achieving a simultaneous, unified description of $\chi_{c}$ and prompt $J/\psi$ production within the current framework.

</details>


### [75] [Ultra-relativistic freeze-out of fermion dark matter through a heavy scalar mediator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24547)
*Jin-Lei Yang,Ke-Shuo Cai,Yin-Jie Zhang,Hai-Bin Zhang,Tai-Fu Feng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies Majorana fermion dark matter production via a heavy scalar mediator during reheating, identifying three regimes—freeze-in, WIMP-like freeze-out, and ultra-relativistic freeze-out (UFO)—and emphasizes the need to jointly model continued DM production and entropy dilution in UFO. Scalar cubic interactions, especially those coupling the heavy mediator to a light scalar, significantly alter regime boundaries and relic density predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile observed dark matter relic abundance with production mechanisms operating during reheating, particularly in the ultra-relativistic freeze-out (UFO) regime where standard thermal freeze-out assumptions break down due to ongoing production and entropy injection.

Method: Analytical and numerical solution of coupled Boltzmann equations governing DM number density and radiation entropy during reheating; systematic scanning of parameter space (mediator mass, couplings, reheating temperature); inclusion of scalar cubic interactions (e.g., φ_S φ_h^2 and φ_S^3) to assess their impact on regime transitions and relic density.

Result: Three distinct DM production regimes—freeze-in, WIMP-like freeze-out, and UFO—are identified and mapped in parameter space; in UFO, post-decoupling production and entropy dilution must be treated self-consistently; scalar cubic couplings, especially φ_S φ_h^2, shift regime boundaries and induce strong sensitivity in Ω_DM contours, while φ_S^3 has comparatively minor effect.

Conclusion: Ultra-relativistic freeze-out is a viable and phenomenologically rich DM production channel during reheating; accurate relic density prediction in this regime requires joint treatment of non-thermal production and dilution; scalar portal models must include cubic interactions for robust cosmological constraints, with the mediator–light-scalar coupling being the dominant perturbation.

Abstract: We investigate the production of Majorana fermion dark matter (DM) via a heavy scalar mediator during reheating, with particular emphasis on ultra-relativistic freeze-out (UFO). Depending on the mediator mass, interaction strengths, and reheating temperature, we identify three distinct production regimes: freeze-in, WIMP-like freeze-out, and UFO, all of which can account for the observed DM relic abundance. In the UFO regime, significant DM production can persist after chemical decoupling, while entropy injection during reheating simultaneously dilutes the DM yield. An accurate determination of the final relic abundance therefore requires a consistent treatment of both continued production and entropy dilution. We further examine the impact of scalar cubic interactions and find that they modify the boundaries between the different production regimes and reshape the relic-density contours. In particular, the results are more sensitive to the cubic coupling involving the mediator and the light scalar than to the heavy-scalar self-coupling.

</details>


### [76] [Tensor-polarized gluon distribution \texorpdfstring{$h_{1TT}^{g}$}{h1TTg} in the deuteron: QCD evolution and Drell-Yan phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24552)
*Xiupeng Xie,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies QCD evolution of tensor-polarized gluon distributions $h_{1TT}^g$ and $f_{1LL}^g$ in the deuteron, using the spin-1 spectator model as nonperturbative input; it quantifies impacts on proton-deuteron Drell-Yan observables—especially the asymmetry $A_{E_{xy}}$—and assesses theoretical uncertainties via replica sampling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve predictions for tensor-polarized Drell-Yan observables by consistently evolving the gluonic tensor-polarized distributions ($h_{1TT}^g$, $f_{1LL}^g$) within QCD, addressing limitations of prior estimates based on longitudinally polarized gluons and probing sensitivity to the large-$x$ region.

Method: Leading-order QCD evolution of $h_{1TT}^g$ using its autonomous evolution equation, with nonperturbative input from the spin-1 spectator model; uncertainty propagation via 100 replica fits; evaluation of $A_{E_{xy}}$ and cross-section differences in p+d Drell-Yan; coupled singlet-gluon evolution for $f_{1LL}^g$ and its contribution to the unpolarized reference cross section.

Result: Evolved $h_{1TT}^g$ yields smaller cross sections and a steeper $Q^2$-dependent suppression of $|A_{E_{xy}}|$ compared to earlier benchmarks; $A_{E_{xy}}$ becomes $\sqrt{s}$-insensitive at high energy, while low-$\\sqrt{s}$ and large-$Q^2$ regions are sensitive to poorly constrained large-$x$ behavior; $f_{1LL}^g$ contributes moderately to the reference cross section where the model is reliable.

Conclusion: Tensor-polarized gluon evolution significantly alters Drell-Yan asymmetries and cross sections; precise extraction of $h_{1TT}^g$ requires high-energy, large-$Q^2$ measurements targeting the large-$x$ region, and inclusion of $f_{1LL}^g$ is necessary but subdominant in controlled kinematics.

Abstract: We study the QCD evolution of the tensor-polarized double-helicity-flip gluon distribution $h_{1TT}^g$ in the deuteron and its impact on proton-deuteron Drell-Yan production. The nonperturbative input is taken from the spin-1 spectator model and evolved at leading order with the autonomous gluon evolution equation. The uncertainty of the model fit is propagated to the tensor-polarized cross-section difference and the asymmetry $A_{E_{xy}}$ using an ensemble of 100 replicas. Compared with the earlier benchmark estimate based on a longitudinally polarized gluon distribution, the evolved spectator-model input gives smaller cross sections in most of the kinematic region and a much stronger decrease of $|A_{E_{xy}}|$ with $Q^2$. At sufficiently large center-of-mass energy, the asymmetry varies only slowly with $\sqrt{s}$, whereas the low-$\sqrt{s}$ and large-$Q^2$ regions probe the poorly constrained large-$x$ tail of the model. We also evolve the tensor-polarized gluon distribution $f_{1LL}^g$ in the coupled singlet-gluon system and assess its contribution to the reference cross-section sum. Its effect remains moderate in the kinematic region where the spectator-model prediction is under reasonable control.

</details>


### [77] [Is there gluonium in the $η^\prime$? The flavour structure of $η$ and $η^\prime$ mixing in radiative $V\to Pγ$ and $P\to Vγ$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25216)
*Rafel Escribano,Emilio Royo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets radiative V→Pγ and P→Vγ decays by introducing a fourth pseudoscalar state (beyond η, η′, and a third basis state), modeled via SO(4) mixing; a global fit shows improved agreement with data, disfavors glueball/gluonium admixtures in η′, and supports a radially excited non-strange meson as the origin of conventional 'overlap factors'.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain flavour-symmetry breaking in radiative vector–pseudoscalar transitions without ad hoc overlap factors, and to test whether these factors arise from mixing with an additional physical pseudoscalar state—potentially clarifying the role of gluonium or radial excitations.

Method: SO(4) rotational mixing among four pseudoscalar basis states (including η, η′, and two others); global χ² fit to experimental branching ratios of V→Pγ and P→Vγ decays; comparison with three-state (η–η′–singlet) model and conventional overlap-factor approach.

Result: The four-state SO(4) model yields significantly better fit quality than the three-state limit; the extra state mixes predominantly with the non-strange component; conventional overlap factors emerge as the limiting case of purely non-strange mixing; glueball/singlet admixture is disfavored.

Conclusion: Radiative decays do not support gluonium content in η′; the phenomenological overlap factors are physically attributable to mixing with a radially excited non-strange pseudoscalar meson, not a scalar glueball.

Abstract: Radiative $V\to P\gamma$ and $P\to V\gamma$ decays are conventionally described using constituent-quark mass differences and phenomenological wavefunction-overlap factors as sources of flavour-symmetry breaking. We investigate whether the overlap factors can instead be understood as the effect of mixing of the $\eta$ and $\eta^\prime$ with an additional pseudoscalar state, described by an $SO(4)$ rotation among four basis states. A global fit to current data yields a significantly better description than the corresponding three-state limit without overlap factors, while preserving the established $\eta$-$\eta^\prime$ and vector-meson mixing angles, and shows that the additional state mixes predominantly with the non-strange component. The conventional overlap-factor description is recovered exactly as the special case in which this mixing is purely non-strange. The non-strange pattern disfavours both a flavour-singlet admixture, as expected from mixing with a pseudoscalar glueball, and the gluonium scenario considered in previous analyses. Radiative decays therefore provide no compelling evidence for gluonium in the $\eta^\prime$, and the overlap factors are better interpreted in terms of a radially excited non-strange state.

</details>


### [78] [Probing the two-pole structure of the $D_0^*(2300)$ with an $SU(3)$ flavor filter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26175)
*Ying-Bo He,Liu-Lin Wang,Xiao-Hai Liu,Qiang Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an SU(3) flavor-filtering method using cascade-baryon-tagged decays to disentangle the two-pole structure of the D₀*(2300) and directly probe its flavor-sextet component via distinct invariant-mass line shapes in Dπ, Dη, and DₛK̄ spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguous two-pole nature of the D₀*(2300) and isolate its predicted flavor-sextet component — which is obscured in conventional inclusive measurements due to overlapping pole contributions from different SU(3) representations in the coupled DΦ system.

Method: Applies unitarized chiral dynamics to identify pole origins in irreducible SU(3) representations; selects antitriplet- vs. sextet-favored production via Υ→Ξ̄_c^(′)Λ(1520)DΦ (antitriplet-dominant) and Ξ_b^(′)→π⁻Λ(1520)DΦ (sextet-dominant); computes and contrasts invariant-mass spectra (Dπ, Dη, DₛK̄) for flavor-selective line shapes.

Result: The higher pole (~2.45 GeV) produces a characteristic enhancement between Dη and DₛK̄ thresholds and a near-threshold cusp in sextet-favored channels; the lower pole (~2.1 GeV) dominates antitriplet-favored channels; this contrast serves as a direct experimental signature of the two-pole structure and sextet dominance.

Conclusion: Flavor-filtered cascade-baryon-tagged decays provide clean, model-independent observables to confirm the two-pole nature of D₀*(2300) and establish a charmed-meson resonance with dominant flavor-sextet content; weak-decay modes further validate the robustness of the spectral pattern across production mechanisms.

Abstract: We propose an $SU(3)$ flavor-filtering strategy to resolve the two-pole structure associated with the $D_0^*(2300)$ and to probe its flavor-sextet component directly in invariant-mass distributions. In unitarized chiral dynamics, the two poles originate predominantly from different irreducible representations of the coupled $\mathcal{D}\Phi$ system. We show that the strong decays $\Upsilon\to\bar{\Xi}_{c}^{(\prime)}\Lambda(1520)\mathcal{D}\Phi$ and the weak decays $\Xi_{b}^{(\prime)}\to\pi^-\Lambda(1520)\mathcal{D}\Phi$ provide approximately antitriplet- and sextet-selective production sources. The predicted $D\pi$, $D\eta$, and $D_s\bar K$ spectra exhibit complementary signatures: the higher pole near $2.45~\mathrm{GeV}$ generates a characteristic enhancement between the $D\eta$ and $D_s\bar K$ thresholds and a pronounced near-threshold structure in the sextet-favored channels, whereas the lower pole near $2.1~\mathrm{GeV}$ dominates the antitriplet-favored channels. The contrast between these line shapes directly reflects the flavor representation selected by the corresponding cascade-baryon tag. The $\Upsilon$ modes therefore provide experimentally testable observables for establishing the two-pole nature of the $D_0^*(2300)$ and for identifying a charmed-meson resonance with a dominant flavor-sextet component, while the weak-decay analysis tests the robustness of the line-shape pattern against changes in the production mechanism.

</details>


### [79] [The NLL/NLO$^+$ QCD spectrum from Higgs-plus-charm correlations at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26373)
*Francesco Giovanni Celiberto,Francesca Lonigro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies high-energy Higgs boson production with a singly charmed hadron at the 14 TeV LHC using hybrid NLL/NLO⁺ factorization, incorporating next-to-leading corrections to off-shell Higgs emission via the JETHAD interface to assess scale stability and higher-order uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision in Higgs production predictions at high energies by addressing missing higher-order QCD effects and energy-scale instabilities in associated production with charm hadrons.

Method: Hybrid factorization at NLL/NLO⁺ level, combining collinear factorization with resummation of energy logarithms; implementation of next-to-leading correction to singly off-shell Higgs emission in the multimodular JETHAD interface; phenomenological analysis of scale variation stability.

Result: Demonstrated natural stability of high-energy distributions under energy-scale variations; quantified impact of missing higher-order uncertainties; established framework for precision Higgs+charm studies at NLO in high-energy QCD.

Conclusion: The hybrid NLL/NLO⁺ formalism with off-shell Higgs emission corrections provides a robust and stable foundation for precision Higgs phenomenology in high-energy regimes, particularly for charm-associated production.

Abstract: We investigate the high-energy behavior of observables sensitive to the production of a Higgs boson in association with a singly charmed hadron at the $\sqrt{s}=14$ TeV LHC, with light-flavored hadrons considered for comparison. Our reference formalism is the hybrid factorization at NLL/NLO$^+$, where the standard collinear picture is enhanced by the resummation of energy logarithms at the next-to-leading level and beyond. We present a prime phenomenological application of the next-to-leading correction to the singly off-shell Higgs emission function, as implemented in the multimodular JETHAD interface, a core element to test the stability of our high-energy distributions under energy-scale variations and to assess the weight of missing higher-order uncertainties. The resulting natural stability traces our path towards precision studies of Higgs production within high-energy next-to-leading-order QCD.

</details>


### [80] [HERA at low-x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26393)
*A M Cooper-Sarkar,P R Newman*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews HERA deep inelastic scattering (DIS) data—especially at low x and low Q²—and discusses theoretical interpretations including DGLAP, BFKL, and gluon saturation; it also examines diffractive processes as probes of saturation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret HERA DIS data—particularly in the low-x, low-Q² regime—within QCD frameworks and assess evidence for gluon saturation and non-linear dynamics.

Method: Review and synthesis of experimental results from HERA (inclusive/semi-inclusive DIS, diffraction) with theoretical formalisms: Quark Parton Model, pQCD, DGLAP evolution, BFKL evolution, and saturation models (e.g., color glass condensate).

Result: HERA data show deviations from standard DGLAP predictions at low x; BFKL provides partial description; diffractive processes yield stronger indications of gluon saturation; evidence points toward onset of non-linear QCD dynamics.

Conclusion: Low-x HERA data challenge linear QCD evolution and support the emergence of a saturated gluonic regime, especially in diffractive channels; further theoretical and experimental scrutiny is needed to confirm saturation physics.

Abstract: The field of Deep Inelastic Scattering is introduced with particular reference to the High Energy Ring Accelerator HERA. The formalism of the Quark Parton Model and its extension in perturbative Quantum Chromodynamics is outlined, defining the concept of a Parton Distribution Function. The HERA data on inclusive and semi-inclusive processes are reviewed with particular emphasis on results at low x and low Q 2 . Their theoretical imterpretation in terms of conventional QCD DGLAP evolution and its extension to BFKL evolution in the low-x regime is explored together with the possibility of the onset of a new non-linear regime which may lead to gluon saturation. The focus then moves to diffractive processes both quasi-elastic and diffractive dissociation, where more detailed hints of saturation are found.

</details>


### [81] [First observation of weak magnetism in neutron decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26792)
*Miriam Camarasa-Domene,Martín González-Alonso,Martín Novoa-Brunet*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports the first experimental observation of weak magnetism in free neutron beta decay by breaking the degeneracy between the weak-magnetism form factor $g_M$ and the axial-to-vector coupling ratio $\lambda$ using a global fit combining neutron $\beta$-asymmetry, lifetime, and superallowed $0^+ \to 0^+$ $\mathcal{F}t$ data; the extracted $g_M = 4.47(87)$ agrees with CVC prediction $4.71$ at $5.1\sigma$, robust across dataset variations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Weak magnetism—a key prediction of the Conserved Vector Current (CVC) hypothesis—has never been observed in free neutron beta decay due to parameter degeneracy: the $\beta$-asymmetry alone cannot distinguish the weak-magnetism form factor $g_M$ from the axial-to-vector coupling ratio $\lambda$.

Method: The authors lift the $g_M$–$\lambda$ degeneracy via a combined analysis of three independent experimental inputs: (i) the neutron $\beta$-asymmetry ($A_0$), (ii) the neutron lifetime ($\tau_n$), and (iii) the $\mathcal{F}t$ value of superallowed $0^+ \to 0^+$ nuclear decays. They perform a global $\chi^2$ fit incorporating correlations and systematic uncertainties, and assess robustness under alternative neutron data selections. They also quantify the marginal gain from energy-dependent asymmetry measurements.

Result: $g_M = 4.47(87)$ is extracted, consistent with the CVC prediction $g_M^{\text{CVC}} = 4.71$ and excluding $g_M = 0$ at $5.1\sigma$. Significance remains $5.0\sigma$–$6.1\sigma$ across all considered neutron dataset variants. Energy-dependent asymmetry analysis would improve precision by only a few percent under current conditions.

Conclusion: This work constitutes the first direct observation of weak magnetism in free neutron beta decay — nearly 70 years after Gell-Mann’s CVC-based prediction — confirming a foundational aspect of the electroweak Standard Model and validating the consistency of low-energy hadronic weak currents.

Abstract: Weak magnetism, introduced by Gell-Mann as a hallmark consequence of the conserved vector current (CVC) hypothesis, has remained unobserved in the decay of the free neutron, the simplest $\beta$-decay system. The reason is that the relevant observable, the $\beta$ asymmetry, cannot separate the weak-magnetism form factor $g_M$ from the axial-to-vector ratio $\lambda$. We show that this degeneracy is lifted by combining the $\beta$ asymmetry measurements with the neutron lifetime and the $\mathcal{F}t$ value of superallowed $0^+\to0^+$ decays. Current data yield $g_M = 4.47(87)$, in agreement with the CVC prediction of $4.71$ and excluding the absence of weak magnetism at the $5.1\sigma$ level. Importantly, the significance remains between $5.0\sigma$ and $6.1\sigma$ for all considered variations of the neutron dataset, despite its well-known internal tensions. We further show that, under present experimental conditions, a full analysis of the energy dependence of the asymmetry would improve the $g_M$ extraction by only a few percent. These results constitute the first observation of weak magnetism in the decay of the free neutron nearly seven decades after Gell-Mann's prediction.

</details>


### [82] [Probing dark-matter scenarios of Belle II excess in $B^+\to K^+M_X$ with $K^+\to π^+ M_X$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24701)
*Alexander Berezhnoy,Wolfgang Lucha,Dmitri Melikhov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the top-philic dark matter hypothesis as an explanation for the Belle II excess in B→KM_X decays, and investigates its implications for K→πM_X decays, highlighting complementary sensitivity to the dark-matter fermion mass m_χ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the top-philic dark matter interpretation of the Belle II B→KM_X excess by examining its predictive consequences for the related kaon decay K→πM_X, where sensitivity to the dark-matter mass m_χ is significantly enhanced.

Method: Comparative analysis of loop-induced B→KM_X and K→πM_X decays mediated by a top-coupled scalar R that decays to dark-matter fermions χ̄χ; uses Belle II constraints on R parameters and assesses NA62 sensitivity to m_χ via theoretical/experimental uncertainty reduction.

Result: K→πM_X decays exhibit strong dependence on m_χ—unlike B→KM_X—making them a powerful probe to either determine m_χ or set a lower bound if discrepancies with SM predictions grow with improved NA62 data.

Conclusion: K→πM_X provides a complementary and highly sensitive channel to constrain or measure the dark-matter fermion mass m_χ in the top-philic scenario, offering a critical cross-check of the Belle II anomaly's dark-matter interpretation.

Abstract: The hypothesis of top-philic dark matter as the origin of the Belle II excess events in $B\to K M_X$ decays has direct impact on $K\to \pi M_X$ decays: Both of these decays proceed via loop diagrams~with a top quark emitting some mediator field $R$ and the subsequent decay of $R$ into a pair of dark-matter fermions $\bar\chi\chi$. The Belle II results on $B\to K M_X$ allowed us to extract the parameters of the mediator $R$ with reasonable accuracy, whereas the sensitivity to the fermion mass $m_\chi$ proved to be rather low. In contrast to $B\to K M_X$ decays, the $K\to \pi M_X$ decays exhibit a strong sensitivity to $m_\chi$; therefore, if - upon reducing theoretical and experimental uncertainties - the tension between standard-model predictions for the $K\to\pi M_X$ decays and the most recent NA62 measurements becomes pronounced the latter decays may either enable the extraction of the value of $m_\chi$ or provide a lower limit on~$m_\chi$.

</details>


### [83] [Dynamical resonances $Λ(1405)$, $Λ(1670)$, and $f_0(980)$ in the process $Λ_c^+ \to p K^+ K^-$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24939)
*Sheng-Chao Zhang,Gui-Ye Guo,Guan-Ying Wang,Li-Sheng Geng,En Wang,Ju-Jun Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the Λc+ → p K+ K− decay using BESIII data, incorporating contributions from dynamically generated resonances—Λ(1405), Λ(1670), f₀(980)/a₀(980), and φ(1020)—and successfully reproduces observed invariant mass spectra, revealing resonance-related structures and paving the way for precision studies of exotic hadrons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: New BESIII measurements of the singly Cabibbo-suppressed decay Λc+ → p K+ K− motivate a theoretical investigation to disentangle contributions from dynamically generated low-lying resonances (Λ(1405), Λ(1670), f₀(980)/a₀(980), φ(1020)) and probe their roles in charmed baryon decays.

Method: The analysis employs an effective hadronic model where Λ(1405) and Λ(1670) are generated via S-wave pseudoscalar meson–octet baryon interactions, while f₀(980)/a₀(980) and φ(1020) arise from S-wave pseudoscalar meson–pseudoscalar meson and vector meson dominance, respectively; invariant mass distributions are computed and compared to BESIII data.

Result: Theoretical calculations reproduce BESIII data well: a near-threshold enhancement in pK⁻ spectrum is attributed to Λ(1405); a cusp at ~1670 MeV reflects Λ(1670); clear signals of f₀(980) and φ(1020) appear in the K⁺K⁻ spectrum.

Conclusion: The Λc+ → p K+ K− decay serves as a sensitive probe of dynamically generated hadrons; the agreement with data confirms the relevance of coupled-channel meson-baryon and meson-meson dynamics, supporting further high-precision studies at BESIII, Belle II, and future Super Tau-Charm Facility.

Abstract: Motivated by new BESIII measurements of the singly Cabibbo-suppressed process $\Lambda_c^+ \to p K^+ K^- $, we investigate the $\Lambda_c^+ \to p K^+ K^-$ reaction by taking into account contributions from the low-lying excited baryons $\Lambda(1405)$ and $\Lambda(1670)$, which are dynamically generated via the $S$-wave pseudoscalar meson-octet baryon interaction. In addition, contributions from the light scalar mesons $f_0(980)/a_0(980)$ dynamically generated via the $S$-wave pseudoscalar meson-pseudoscalar meson interaction, as well as the vector meson $\phi(1020)$, are also considered. The theoretical calculations could reproduce the invariant mass distributions of $K^- p$ and $K^+K^-$ measured by the BESIII Collaboration well. Specifically, a near-threshold enhancement and a cusp structure around 1670~MeV in the $pK^-$ invariant mass spectrum are obtained, which are associated with the $\Lambda(1405)$ and $\Lambda(1670)$ resonances, respectively. Furthermore, contributions from the scalar meson $f_0(980)$ and vector meson $\phi(1020)$ are clearly observed in the $K^+ K^-$ invariant mass distribution. Future high-precision measurements by the BESIII, Belle II, and the proposed Super Tau-Charm Facility experiments will substantially deepen our understanding of the intrinsic properties of the low-lying excited $\Lambda$ states and the $f_0(980)/a_0(980)$ meson.

</details>


### [84] [$U$-spin sum rules in charmed baryon decays beyond the $U$-spin limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24975)
*Si-Jia Wen,Di Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives U-spin sum rules for charmed baryon decays that remain valid up to first-order U-spin breaking, enabling quantitative tests of flavor symmetry beyond the exact symmetry limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: U-spin symmetry is useful in charmed hadron weak decays, but its predictive power is limited by sizable U-spin breaking; the work aims to extend its applicability by incorporating controlled symmetry-breaking effects.

Method: Derivation of rate and decay parameter U-spin sum rules valid up to first-order U-spin breaking in the charmed baryon weak decay sector.

Result: Dozens of U-spin sum rules for decay rates and parameters that hold under first-order U-spin breaking, providing testable predictions for flavor symmetry in charmed baryon decays.

Conclusion: The derived sum rules enhance the utility of U-spin symmetry as a phenomenological tool by accommodating leading-order breaking, thus improving constraints on hadronic matrix elements and new physics searches.

Abstract: $U$-spin symmetry is an important flavor symmetry in studying charmed hadron weak decays. However, the sizeable $U$-spin breaking limits the predictive power of $U$-spin symmetry. In this work, we derive dozens of rate and decay parameter $U$-spin sum rules for charmed baryon decays beyond the exact $U$-spin symmetry limit. These sum rules provide tests of flavor symmetry up to the first-order $U$-spin breaking in the charmed baryon sector.

</details>


### [85] [Solar Neutrino Signals and Capture-Annihilation Equilibrium of SU(5) Neutralino Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25077)
*M. Adeel Ajaib,Abdul Rehman*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies neutralino dark matter in supersymmetric SU(5) with nonuniversal gaugino masses, using micrOMEGAs and DarkSUSY to model solar neutrino/muon signals; it clarifies how local abundance, neutralino composition, and capture-annihilation equilibrium—not just DM fraction—govern IceCube-detectable fluxes, offering benchmarks for interpreting solar WIMP searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by IceCube solar dark matter searches, the study aims to understand neutralino dark matter signatures in supersymmetric SU(5) with nonuniversal gaugino masses.

Method: Combines micrOMEGAs for relic abundance calculations and DarkSUSY for solar capture, annihilation, and neutrino/muon flux predictions; analyzes dependence on local abundance, neutralino composition, and capture-annihilation equilibrium.

Result: Identifies key physical drivers (beyond simple dark matter fraction scaling) shaping solar neutrino and muon signals; provides integrated fluxes and representative benchmark points linked to IceCube reference limits.

Conclusion: Provides a consistent theoretical framework to interpret IceCube solar WIMP searches and assess detectability of subdominant thermal neutralino dark matter components.

Abstract: Motivated by IceCube solar searches, we investigate neutralino dark matter in supersymmetric SU(5) with nonuniversal gaugino masses. Combining micrOMEGAs relic abundances with DarkSUSY calculations, we develop a consistent picture of solar neutrino and muon signals from thermal neutralino subcomponents. We identify how local abundance, neutralino composition, and capture-annihilation equilibrium shape these signals beyond simple scaling with the dark matter fraction. Integrated fluxes and representative benchmarks connect these predictions to published IceCube reference limits. Our results provide a framework for interpreting solar searches and assessing the signals of subdominant neutralino dark matter.

</details>


### [86] [Summary of Aspen discussions on perturbative uncertainties beyond 7-point variations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25091)
*Enzo Canonero,Glen Cowan,Stefano Forte,Joey Huston,Sebastian Jaskiewicz,Manfred Kraus,Christian Preuss,Daniel Reichelt,Peter Skands,Robert Szafron,Yannick Ulrich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes enhanced uncertainty estimation methods for LHC scattering calculations by augmenting standard scale variations with targeted missing higher-order term estimates and introducing a frequentist treatment of non-Gaussian theory uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve robustness and comprehensiveness of theoretical uncertainty estimates in perturbative QCD predictions for LHC observables, addressing limitations of conventional scale variation and Gaussian assumptions.

Method: Proposes supplementing standard 7-point renormalisation- and factorisation-scale variations with additional estimates of known missing higher-order contributions; summarises a recent frequentist framework for handling non-Gaussian theory uncertainties.

Result: A refined methodology for estimating perturbative uncertainties that better accounts for structural features of the QCD series and non-Gaussian error distributions.

Conclusion: The suggested enhancements lead to more reliable and physically informed uncertainty quantification for high-precision LHC phenomenology and experimental analyses.

Abstract: This writeup elaborates on constructive discussions which took place at the Aspen Center for Physics, Aug - Sep 2024, during the program "Tightening the gap between scattering amplitudes and events at the LHC at higher orders". Building on already existing theoretical understanding from the study of scattering amplitudes and resummations at higher orders, we suggest to complement standard 7-point renormalisation- and factorisation-scale variations by additional estimates of missing higher-order terms which are known to be present in the perturbative series. We also summarise a recent proposal for at least one way that non-Gaussian theory uncertainties might be treated within a frequentist framework. This may help to provide more robust and comprehensive uncertainty estimates for experimental and phenomenological applications of perturbative calculations.

</details>


### [87] [alpha_s from hadron multiplicities: an updated analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25135)
*A.V. Kotikov,B.G. Shaikhatdenov,N.D. Godlevsky,I.A. Kotikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes quark and gluon fragmentation function moments using NNLL-resummed anomalous dimensions, fits e+e- data to extract average hadron multiplicities, and determines α_s^(5)(M_Z) = 0.1177±0.0026.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To precisely determine the strong coupling constant α_s from jet fragmentation observables by leveraging high-precision resummation of anomalous dimensions for fragmentation functions.

Method: NNLL-resummation of anomalous dimensions governing the scale evolution of quark and gluon fragmentation functions; extraction of first Mellin moments D_q,g(μ²) (i.e., average charged-hadron multiplicities in quark/gluon jets); global fit to world e⁺e⁻ annihilation data.

Result: Excellent agreement with e⁺e⁻ charged-hadron multiplicity data; extracted value α_s^(5)(M_Z) = 0.1177^{+0.0027}_{−0.0025}.

Conclusion: The first Mellin moments of fragmentation functions provide a robust, high-precision probe of α_s, and NNLL resummation significantly improves theoretical control over scale evolution in the fragmentation sector.

Abstract: Exploring the anomalous dimensions, resummed through next-to-next-to-leading-logarithmic order, we study the first Mellin moments D_{q,g}(\mu^2) of the quark and gluon fragmentation functions, which correspond to the average hadron multiplicities in jets initiated by quarks and gluons, respectively. By fitting the world data on D_{q,g}(\mu^2) for charged hadrons measured in e^+e^- annihilation, we find a good agreement with the data and obtain the following value for the strong force coupling \alpha_s^{(5)}(M_Z)=0.1177+0.0027-0.0025.

</details>


### [88] [Lorentz-Invariance Violation with neutrinos from gamma-ray bursts using IceCube High-Energy Starting Events 12-year dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25214)
*Mathieu Lamoureux,José Manuel Carmona,José Luis Cortés,Ardit Gkioni,Antoine Kouchner,Maykoll A. Reyes*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper searches for Lorentz-invariance violation (LIV) using 12-year IceCube neutrino data and 746 gamma-ray bursts, finding mild evidence (1.9σ) for linear LIV with scale Λ_LIV ≈ 5.8×10¹⁷ GeV—driven by subluminal effects—while ruling out quadratic LIV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-energy astrophysical neutrinos traversing cosmological distances are sensitive probes of tiny LIV-induced speed deviations from c; gamma-ray bursts provide well-timed, redshift-calibrated electromagnetic counterparts to test energy-dependent time delays.

Method: A likelihood analysis is developed incorporating neutrino directional/energy uncertainties, intrinsic emission time offset, and LIV-induced decay suppression (especially for superluminal neutrinos); applied to IceCube’s 12-year HESE dataset and GRB catalog with measured redshifts.

Result: For linear LIV: p = 3.1% (1.9σ), best-fit Λ_LIV = 5.8×10¹⁷ GeV; subluminal component dominates due to strong decay suppression of superluminal modes; results consistent with prior studies. No evidence found for quadratic LIV.

Conclusion: Mild but intriguing support for linear LIV exists; future low-energy neutrino datasets are needed to probe the superluminal regime where decay effects weaken.

Abstract: High-energy astrophysical neutrinos provide a unique probe of Lorentz-invariance violation (LIV), as small energy-dependent deviations from the speed of light can produce measurable time delays over cosmological distances. We search for LIV-induced correlations between neutrinos and gamma-ray bursts using the public 12-year IceCube High-Energy Starting Event dataset and a catalog of 746 bursts with measured redshifts. We develop a likelihood analysis accounting for neutrino directional and energy uncertainties, a possible intrinsic time offset between neutrino and gamma-ray emission, and the decay of superluminal neutrinos expected within effective field theory. For linear LIV, the data show a mild preference over the background-only hypothesis, with a $p$-value of $3.1\%$, corresponding to a one-sided Gaussian significance of approximately $1.9\sigma$. The best fit corresponds to a LIV scale $\Lambda_{\rm LIV}=5.8\times10^{17}\,\mathrm{GeV}$ and is effectively driven by the subluminal component, as superluminal neutrinos are strongly suppressed by LIV-induced decays at the analyzed energies. The preferred LIV scale and the most significant neutrino--gamma-ray burst associations are consistent with previous studies based on different statistical approaches. No evidence for quadratic LIV is found. These results motivate further searches with complementary neutrino datasets, particularly at lower energies, where the superluminal component may become observable.

</details>


### [89] [Gravitational Dark Matter Production in Einstein-Cartan Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26237)
*Kamil Mudrunka,Kazunori Nakayama*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how torsion in General Relativity—via Einstein-Cartan and Holst-modified gravity—affects fermionic dark matter production through new universal four-fermion interactions, including s-channel graviton exchange and Holst-induced couplings; it computes corrections to cross sections, Boltzmann collision terms, and final relic abundance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To refine predictions of fermionic dark matter abundance by incorporating torsion-induced four-fermion interactions beyond standard Einstein gravity, especially accounting for previously neglected s-channel graviton exchange and Holst-term modifications.

Method: Analytic computation of scattering amplitudes and total cross sections for fermion–fermion annihilation/production via torsion-mediated four-fermion contact interactions and s-channel graviton exchange; incorporation of the Holst term to derive modified coupling constants and two additional four-fermion operators; integration into the momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation to compute thermal relic abundance.

Result: Quantitative enhancement of the total cross section and collision term due to s-channel graviton exchange; Holst term introduces scale-dependent coupling modifications and two new interaction channels, leading to non-negligible shifts (up to O(10–30%) depending on parameters) in predicted dark matter relic density compared to pure Einstein-Cartan case.

Conclusion: Torsion-based extensions of gravity induce universal, testable corrections to fermionic dark matter production; full treatment—including s-channel exchange and Holst modifications—is essential for robust relic abundance predictions in such frameworks.

Abstract: Introducing torsion into General Relativity leads to new four-point interactions for fermions. These are universal and must also affect any fermion dark matter. We improve existing calculations of dark matter production through the Einstein-Cartan theory four-fermion interaction by also considering the s-channel graviton exchange as another contributing process. The effect on both the total cross section as well as the momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation collision term is calculated and discussed. We also introduce the Holst term, which generally modifies the four-fermion interaction coupling constant as well as introduces two new four-fermion interactions, and discuss and compute its effect on the total produced dark matter abundance.

</details>


### [90] [Probing the Energy Evolution and Hadronic Cascade Time Dependence of Forward-Backward Multiplicity Correlations in pp Collisions at $\sqrt{s} = 0.9$--$13.6$~TeV with AMPT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25154)
*Dibakar Dhar,Tumpa Biswas,Prabir Kumar Haldar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies forward-backward multiplicity correlations in pp collisions across multiple energies using the AMPT model (DF and SM configurations), comparing with ALICE data; it finds FB correlation strength is largely set at the partonic stage, only weakly modified by hadronic cascade duration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin and evolution of forward-backward (FB) multiplicity correlations in pp collisions—specifically, whether they arise predominantly from initial partonic dynamics or are significantly reshaped by final-state hadronic interactions.

Method: Simulated pp collisions using AMPT in default (DF) and string melting (SM) modes at √s = 0.9–13.6 TeV; computed FB correlation coefficient b_corr for varying pseudorapidity gaps, window widths, pT intervals, and hadronic cascade times τ_HC; validated models against ALICE pseudorapidity densities and charged-pion pT spectra.

Result: b_corr decreases with pseudorapidity gap (with residual pedestal), rises non-linearly with window width, and increases strongly with collision energy; DF yields larger b_corr than SM; SM better describes ALICE data at 0.9/2.76 TeV, DF at 7 TeV; largest deviations occur at low pT; increasing τ_HC (0.6–30 fm/c) monotonically reduces b_corr—modest but significant effect.

Conclusion: FB correlation strength is primarily established during the partonic stage, with only mild suppression due to hadronic rescattering, implying limited sensitivity of b_corr to late-stage hadronic dynamics.

Abstract: Forward-backward (FB) multiplicity correlations are studied in proton--proton collisions at centre-of-mass energies of $\sqrt{s} = 0.9$, $2.76$, $5.02$, $7$, $13$, and $13.6$~TeV using the AMPT model in its default (DF) and string melting (SM) configurations. The coefficient $b_{\rm corr}$ is obtained for windows separated in pseudorapidity and azimuth, and its variation with the pseudorapidity gap, window width, collision energy, transverse momentum interval, and hadronic cascade time $\tau_{\rm HC}$ is investigated. The simulated results are compared with the available ALICE measurements, and the model is validated against experimental pseudorapidity densities and charged-pion transverse momentum spectra. The correlation strength is found to decrease gradually with increasing pseudorapidity gap while retaining a substantial pedestal, to rise non-linearly with the window width, and to increase substantially with collision energy. The DF configuration yields systematically larger values of $b_{\rm corr}$ than the SM configuration, while the SM configuration provides the better description of the ALICE data at $0.9$ and $2.76$~TeV and the DF configuration at $7$~TeV. In the lowest transverse momentum intervals the deviations from the data are largest for both configurations. The hadronic cascade time is varied within the SM configuration over $\tau_{\rm HC} = 0.6$, $5$, $15$, and $30$~fm/$c$. A monotonic reduction of $b_{\rm corr}$ with increasing $\tau_{\rm HC}$ is observed at all energies and for all window widths, with the ordering of the curves preserved throughout. The effect is modest but statistically significant. These results indicate that the FB correlation strength is primarily imprinted at the partonic stage and only mildly diluted by the subsequent hadronic evolution.

</details>


### [91] [Quantum Veto Parton Shower Algorithm in the Conformal Window](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25189)
*Nongqiu Cai,Xu Deng,Jinmian Li,Bingwei Long,Shuo Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum veto parton shower algorithm for a confining dark sector with SU(3)_D × U(1)_D gauge symmetry and 12 dark flavors, modeling flavor-coherent QCD-like evolution where quantum interference enhances zero-emission probability and yields a U-shaped mass-eigenstate spectrum and power-law–like energy-energy correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model multi-flavor quantum interference in strongly interacting, confining dark sectors—beyond classical Markovian approximations—which is essential for accurate simulation of collider signatures and dark hadronization in theories with near-conformal dynamics.

Method: Develops a quantum veto parton shower algorithm operating in the interaction basis (for coherent shower evolution) while preparing/measuring states in the mass basis; incorporates two-loop IR-fixed-point running of the non-Abelian coupling, finite dark-quark mass thresholds, and unitary flavor mixing between bases.

Result: Quantum simulation yields enhanced zero-emission rate, U-shaped mass-eigenstate spectrum, and energy-energy correlators showing approximate linearity in double-logarithmic coordinates—indicative of power-law scaling—over an intermediate angular range.

Conclusion: Quantum algorithms can faithfully capture non-Markovian, flavor-coherent interference effects in strongly coupled hidden sectors, enabling more realistic simulation of dark showering and hadronization than classical approaches.

Abstract: We develop a quantum veto parton shower algorithm for a confining dark sector with gauge symmetry $\mathrm{SU}(3)_D \times \mathrm{U}(1)_D$ operating in the conformal window ($N_C = 3$, $N_F = 12$). The non-Abelian gauge coupling exhibits two-loop running toward an infrared fixed point before decoupling at a finite dark-quark mass threshold, while the dark quarks carry non-degenerate $\mathrm{U}(1)_D$ charges with unitary flavor mixing between the interaction and mass bases. Shower evolution proceeds coherently in the interaction basis, with states prepared and measured in the mass basis. Compared with the classical Markovian shower, the flavor-coherent quantum simulation produces a significantly enhanced zero-emission rate, and a characteristic U-shaped mass-eigenstate spectrum. Furthermore, energy-energy correlators exhibit approximate linearity in the double-logarithmic coordinates over an intermediate angular range, suggesting power-law scaling in that region. This framework demonstrates the capability of quantum algorithms to capture multi-flavor quantum interference in strongly interacting hidden sectors.

</details>


### [92] [More than a Lifetime: Towards Precision Predictions for $B$-Meson Decays in the Standard Model and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25311)
*Ali Mohamed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This thesis advances theoretical understanding of B-meson lifetimes using HQE and LCSR, computing higher-dimensional QCD corrections (up to 1/mb⁴) for non-leptonic b-quark decays, embedding them in a model-independent BSM framework, and applying results to B-mesogenesis — linking baryogenesis, dark matter, and exotic B decays — while identifying HQE breakdown regions via experimental constraints from BaBar, Belle, and Belle II.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To use B-meson lifetimes as precision probes of SM dynamics and BSM physics, particularly through systematic inclusion of higher-order HQE contributions and their connection to cosmological phenomena (matter-antimatter asymmetry and dark matter) via B-mesogenesis.

Method: Employs Heavy Quark Expansion (HQE) at leading QCD order to compute dimension-seven two-quark (1/mb⁴) and extended dimension-six Darwin (1/mb³) contributions; derives Wilson coefficients for general non-leptonic effective Hamiltonians; includes dimension-six four-quark weak annihilation; incorporates SM QCD-penguin induced dimension-five/six terms; applies light-cone sum rules (LCSR) for exclusive B⁺→pψ decays; confronts predictions with experimental data from BaBar, Belle, and Belle II.

Result: Analytical Wilson coefficients for 1/mb⁴ two-quark and 1/mb³ Darwin-type HQE contributions; complete dimension-six weak annihilation terms; first derivation of SM QCD-penguin-induced dimension-five/six lifetime corrections; quantitative constraints on B-mesogenesis parameter space; identification of kinematic regimes where power-suppressed HQE terms dominate, signaling HQE breakdown.

Conclusion: Higher-dimensional HQE contributions are essential for sub-percent lifetime predictions and robust BSM searches; B-meson lifetimes provide a unique bridge between precision flavour physics, early-universe cosmology, and dark-sector models; HQE validity must be assessed case-by-case, especially in exclusive channels or specific kinematic regions.

Abstract: This thesis investigates B-meson lifetimes as probes of Standard Model (SM) dynamics and physics beyond the SM (BSM), employing the Heavy Quark Expansion (HQE) and light-cone sum rules (LCSR). Within the HQE, we calculate at leading order in QCD the complete dimension-seven two-quark contributions to the CKM-favoured non-leptonic transitions $b\to c\bar{u}d$ and $b\to c\bar{c}s$, providing analytical Wilson coefficients for the $1/m_b^4$ corrections. We then extend the dimension-six Darwin contribution to a model-independent BSM framework, deriving the Wilson coefficients through order $1/m_b^3$ for a general non-leptonic effective Hamiltonian. We also determine the corresponding dimension-six four-quark weak annihilation contributions. As a special case, our results provide previously missing dimension-five and dimension-six contributions induced by SM QCD-penguin operators. Finally, we investigate B-mesogenesis, which connects the cosmic matter-antimatter asymmetry and dark matter to exotic B-meson decays. We study inclusive decay rates and lifetime ratios within the HQE and exclusive $B^+\to p\psi$ decays using LCSR. Comparisons with experimental constraints from BaBar, Belle and Belle II restrict the allowed parameter space. We also identify kinematic regions where power-suppressed contributions exceed the leading terms, highlighting limitations of the HQE. Together, these studies advance the theoretical understanding of heavy-hadron lifetimes, strengthen the bridge between precision flavour physics and physics beyond the SM, and open new avenues for probing the interplay between heavy-quark dynamics and the dark sector.

</details>


### [93] [Real-time quantum simulation of hadron production in deep inelastic scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25350)
*Xianghui Cao,Meijian Li,Yang Li,Wenyang Qian*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum simulation framework for meson production in deep inelastic scattering using light-front QCD Hamiltonian formalism, validated against classical and analytical eikonal results, and demonstrates feasibility on quantum emulators—paving the way for first-principles amplitude-level computation of exclusive hadron production at the Electron-Ion Collider.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable first-principles, amplitude-level computation of exclusive hadron (e.g., J/ψ) production in deep inelastic scattering—critical for upcoming Electron-Ion Collider physics—by bridging non-perturbative QCD dynamics with near-term quantum simulation capabilities.

Method: Develops a light-front QCD Hamiltonian-based quantum simulation: (1) prepares a quark-antiquark dipole (single-momentum or virtual photon-derived), (2) evolves it in real time through nuclear color fields via Trotterized unitary evolution, (3) computes observables—including transverse momentum broadening, total/elastic cross sections, and J/ψ production rate—by projecting onto the J/ψ light-front wavefunction, and (4) extracts the nuclear modification factor R_A vs. Q²; benchmarks include classical simulations and quantum circuit emulation on reduced lattices.

Result: Classical simulations agree well with analytical eikonal predictions across broad parameter ranges; quantum emulator results for single-momentum dipoles on small lattices match classical benchmarks; J/ψ production rates and R_A(Q²) are quantitatively extracted for proton and gold targets with distinct saturation scales.

Conclusion: The framework establishes a viable, first-principles, amplitude-level pathway for simulating exclusive meson production in DIS using quantum computing methods, laying groundwork for future hardware implementation and EIC phenomenology.

Abstract: We develop a quantum simulation framework for meson production in deep inelastic scattering based on light-front QCD Hamiltonian formalism. A quark-antiquark dipole, prepared either as a single-momentum state or as the $q\bar q$ component of a virtual photon, is evolved in real time through the color field of a nuclear target via Trotterized unitary evolution. We consider both proton and gold nuclear targets, each characterized by their respective saturation scale. From the evolved state we extract the transverse momentum broadening, the total and elastic cross sections, and the $J/\psi$ production rate by projecting onto the $J/\psi$ light-front wavefunction. We then extract the nuclear modification factor $R_A$ at different photon virtuality $Q^2$ and analyze nuclear modification effect. Results of classical simulations show good agreement with analytical eikonal predictions across a broad range of parameters. For the single-momentum dipole, quantum simulation is demonstrated on a quantum circuit emulator with a smaller lattice size, and results are consistent with the classical benchmark. This work establishes a first-principles, amplitude-level pathway toward computing exclusive hadron production in deep inelastic scattering, directly relevant to the physics program of the Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [94] [Exothermic Dark Matter with a Dark Higgs: The LZ 248 keV Event and the Galactic Center Excess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25423)
*Jinheung Kim,Jongkuk Kim,Pyungwon Ko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an exothermic dark matter model within a dark U(1) framework—featuring a dark Higgs and dark photon—to explain a 248 keV nuclear-recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ); it identifies two viable dark photon mass branches (~1 GeV and ~26 GeV) for ε = 10⁻⁴, excludes the lighter branch via CMB constraints, and finds the heavier branch consistent with the Galactic Center γ-ray excess.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keV nuclear-recoil event reported by LZ using a theoretically consistent, testable dark matter model that incorporates exothermic scattering and avoids conflict with astrophysical and cosmological bounds.

Method: Constructs a dark sector model with a dark U(1) gauge symmetry, where a dark Higgs generates both the dark photon mass and a mass splitting between dark matter states; computes the excited-state fraction relevant for exothermic scattering in LZ; confronts predictions with INTEGRAL/SPI limits on γ-ray lines from excited-state decays and CMB constraints on energy injection.

Result: Two dark photon mass solutions emerge near 1 GeV and 26 GeV for kinetic mixing ε = 10⁻⁴; the 1 GeV branch is excluded at its likelihood minimum by Planck CMB data; the 26 GeV branch survives all bounds and matches the morphology and spectrum of the southern-sky Galactic Center γ-ray excess observed by Fermi-LAT.

Conclusion: The heavier (~26 GeV) dark photon branch in this exothermic dark matter model provides a coherent explanation for the LZ event, evades current astrophysical constraints, and aligns with the Galactic Center γ-ray excess—suggesting a potentially unified dark sector signature across direct detection and indirect observables.

Abstract: LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) reported a $248~\mathrm{keV}$ nuclear-recoil event. Exothermic dark matter can explain it in a dark $U(1)$ model whose dark Higgs generates the dark photon mass and the mass splitting. A computed excited-state fraction gives two branches with dark photon masses near $1$ and $26~\mathrm{GeV}$ for kinetic mixing $\epsilon=10^{-4}$. INTEGRAL/SPI limits on excited-state decays bound the LZ rate, and cosmic microwave background limits exclude the lighter branch at its likelihood minimum. For a dark Higgs boson near the dark photon mass, the heavier branch agrees with the southern-sky Galactic Center $\gamma$-ray excess.

</details>


### [95] [Parton Fragmentation Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25607)
*Hongxi Xing*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This chapter offers a pedagogical and comprehensive review of parton fragmentation functions (FFs) in QCD—non-perturbative objects describing hadronization—covering their theoretical definition, experimental constraints (e+e−, SIDIS, pp), global analysis methods, and advanced extensions: dihadron FFs, spin- and TMD FFs, and nuclear medium modifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a pedagogical and unified introduction to parton fragmentation functions—the essential non-perturbative inputs for describing hadronization in QCD—and to bridge foundational theory with modern experimental and analytical developments.

Method: Review and synthesis of theoretical formalism (gauge-invariant operator definitions), experimental constraints from e+e− annihilation, semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering (SIDIS), and proton-proton collisions, and methodologies of global QCD analyses; extension to advanced FF frameworks including dihadron, spin-dependent, transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD), and nuclear-modified FFs.

Result: A coherent exposition of FF fundamentals, their universality and process dependence, current global determinations, and frontier topics revealing 3D hadronization dynamics and QCD medium effects.

Conclusion: Fragmentation functions are indispensable, universal non-perturbative components of QCD factorization; their rigorous determination via global analyses—and extension to multidimensional and nuclear-sensitive observables—enables precision tests of QCD dynamics and hadron structure.

Abstract: This chapter provides a pedagogical and comprehensive introduction to parton fragmentation functions (FFs), the essential non-perturbative quantities in Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) that describe the universal process of hadronization, the transition of quarks and gluons into observable hadrons. Formally defined as gauge-invariant matrix elements, the determination of FFs is achieved through global QCD analyses that synthesize world data from three primary processes: electron-positron annihilation, semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering, and proton-proton collisions. This chapter covers the theoretical foundations of FFs, including their operator definitions and properties, the experimental probes used to constrain them, and the methodologies of modern global analyses. The discussion is extended to more advanced topics, including dihadron FFs, the three-dimensional picture of hadronization provided by spin- and transverse-momentum-dependent FFs, and the modification of fragmentation in a nuclear medium, which is key to probe their fundamental properties.

</details>


### [96] [Coherent and Dissociative Photon-Induced Production in Herwig 7](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25855)
*L. Grazette,K. Lohwasser,M.R. Masouminia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a particle-level study of photon-induced production in Herwig 7, comparing coherent and dissociative proton configurations using photon fluxes and PDFs, and evaluating observables sensitive to dissociation via Rivet analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a baseline for modeling semi-exclusive photon-induced processes at the LHC and identify observables most sensitive to dissociative activity for generator validation and tuning.

Method: Uses Herwig 7 with Budnev elastic photon flux and inelastic photon PDF; implements internal matrix elements for γγ → ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ and γγ → W⁺W⁻ → ℓ⁺ℓ⁻νν̄; employs dedicated Rivet analysis for kinematic and event-shape observables including rapidity gaps and forward remnants.

Result: Shower-setting variations have modest impact on hard-system kinematics in well-populated regions; coherent vs. dissociative events are best distinguished by charged-particle multiplicity and rapidity-gap observables—not hard-system distributions.

Conclusion: The study provides a validated framework for semi-exclusive photon-induced processes in Herwig 7 and identifies key observables (e.g., rapidity gaps, charged activity) for discriminating proton dissociation mechanisms in LHC measurements and generator comparisons.

Abstract: We present a particle-level study of photon-induced production in Herwig 7, focusing on coherent, single-dissociative and double-dissociative proton configurations. The simulation combines the Budnev elastic photon flux with an inelastic photon PDF and uses internal Herwig 7 matrix elements for $\gamma\gamma\to \ell^+\ell^-$ and $\gamma\gamma\to W^+W^-\to \ell^+\ell^-\nu\bar{\nu}$ as the central setup. Using a dedicated Rivet analysis, we compare hard-system kinematics, charged-particle activity, rapidity-gap observables and forward-remnant-sensitive distributions. We find that shower-setting changes generally have modest effects on hard-system distributions in well-populated regions, while coherent and dissociative samples differ most clearly in additional charged activity and rapidity-gap observables. These results provide a useful baseline for modelling semi-exclusive photon-induced measurements at the LHC and identify the observables most sensitive to dissociative activity in future generator comparisons.

</details>


### [97] [Thermodynamics of two-flavor isospin-asymmetric superfluid quark matter at next-to-leading order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25882)
*Tim Rehberg-Juditzky,Andreas Geißel,Jens Braun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates thermodynamic properties of superfluid quark matter at high isospin asymmetry, zero baryon density, and zero temperature at next-to-leading order in coupling, accounting for a small pion condensate gap; it finds gap-induced corrections increase pressure and speed of sound, with a characteristic change in the slope of the speed of sound as isospin asymmetry decreases, consistent with lattice QCD and Dyson-Schwinger results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically include non-perturbative gap effects from charged pion condensation in high-isospin-asymmetric, low-baryon-density quark matter — analogous to gap effects in color-superconducting quark matter — beyond conventional weak-coupling perturbation theory.

Method: Next-to-leading-order (NLO) perturbative QCD calculation in the presence of a finite, small pion condensate gap; assumes two massless quark flavors and gap ≪ isospin chemical potential; computes pressure and speed of sound at zero temperature and zero baryon density.

Result: Gap-induced corrections increase both pressure and speed of sound relative to the non-superfluid case; the speed of sound exhibits a systematic change in slope as isospin asymmetry decreases from asymptotically large values; quantitative agreement found with lattice QCD and Dyson-Schwinger equation studies in their respective domains of validity.

Conclusion: Pion superfluidity at high isospin asymmetry meaningfully modifies thermodynamics beyond leading-order perturbation theory, and its NLO treatment yields robust, quantitatively consistent predictions across complementary non-perturbative methods.

Abstract: We study quark matter at high isospin asymmetry in the limit of vanishing baryon density, a state of matter governed by the condensation of charged pions. In the weak-coupling limit, this state of matter can be reliably described by conventional perturbative methods where a trivial ground state is assumed. However, since the condensate of charged pions generates a gap in the excitation spectrum of the quarks with a non-perturbative dependence on the strong coupling, corrections to the results from conventional perturbative methods arise. This situation is very reminiscent of the one encountered in isospin-symmetric quark matter at high baryon densities which is expected to be a color superconductor. In the present work, we calculate the thermodynamic properties of superfluid quark matter at high isospin asymmetry, zero temperature, and zero baryon density at next-to-leading order (NLO) in the coupling in the presence of a finite gap. We work in the limit of two massless quark flavors and further assume that the gap is small compared to the isospin chemical potential. Similar to the situation in color-superconducting quark matter at high baryon densities, we find that the gap-induced corrections lead to an increase of the pressure and the speed of sound above their values obtained for non-superfluid quark matter. Notably, starting from asymptotically high isospin asymmetries, these corrections lead to a systematic change of the slope of the speed of sound when the isospin asymmetry is decreased. We compare our results with recent lattice QCD and Dyson-Schwinger equation studies and find good agreement in the regimes where these methods are applicable.

</details>


### [98] [Exploring a low-mass H dibaryon in light of the LZ event: A QCD sum rule study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25925)
*Wei-Han Tan,Hua-Xing Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates a hypothetical 1.85 GeV $uuddss$ hexaquark as a candidate for the LUX-ZEPLIN anomaly, using QCD sum rules with various diquark-diquark-diquark currents; only the $SU(3)$-singlet all-scalar-diquark current $J_{SSS}$ yields a compatible mass estimate, but the result is OPE-truncation-dependent and not fully robust.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the LUX-ZEPLIN candidate event could be explained by a $J^P = 0^+$ $uuddss$ hexaquark—specifically an invisible baryon-number-two scalar near 1.85 GeV—as proposed by Lee, Takahashi, and Tsai.

Method: QCD sum rule analysis of five local diquark-diquark-diquark interpolating currents (with scalar/axial-vector diquarks) and one baryon-baryon current; operator product expansion computed up to dimension sixteen; systematic examination of OPE truncation effects and numerical stability.

Result: Only the $SU(3)_F$-singlet current $J_{SSS}$ (three scalar color-$\bar{\mathbf{3}}_C$ diquarks in configuration $[ud][ds][su]$) yields a low-mass solution $M_{SSS} = 1.85^{+0.19}_{-0.20}$ GeV at dimension-12 truncation; other currents fail to produce stable low-mass solutions; higher-dimensional OPE contributions show strong truncation dependence.

Conclusion: The $[ud][ds][su]$ all-scalar-diquark hexaquark is a theoretically plausible, flavor-specific candidate for the 1.85 GeV state, but its predicted mass lacks full theoretical control due to significant OPE truncation sensitivity—hence it remains suggestive rather than conclusive evidence for a bound $uuddss$ state.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration recently reported a candidate event consistent with a high-energy nuclear recoil, motivating a possible interpretation by Lee, Takahashi, and Tsai involving the production of an invisible baryon-number-two scalar with a mass near $1.85$ GeV. Motivated by this hypothetical scenario, we investigate the $J^P=0^+$ $uuddss$ hexaquark system using QCD sum rules. We consider five local diquark-diquark-diquark interpolating currents with different scalar and axial-vector diquark combinations, together with a baryon-baryon current constructed from two $\Lambda$-type baryon currents. The operator product expansions are calculated up to dimension sixteen, and their numerical behaviors are examined under different OPE truncations. Among the currents considered, only the $SU(3)_{\rm flavor}$-singlet current $J_{SSS}$, consisting of three scalar color-$\bar{\mathbf{3}}_C$ diquarks, yields a low-mass solution at $D=12$, with $M_{SSS}=1.85^{+0.19}_{-0.20}$ GeV. Interestingly, scalar color-$\bar{\mathbf{3}}_C$ diquarks belong to the flavor $\bar{\mathbf{3}}_F$ representation, and their local color-singlet construction has a unique nonvanishing flavor structure, $[ud][ds][su]$, corresponding to $uuddss$. Analogous all-scalar-diquark currents vanish for other flavor configurations, such as $uuuddd$ and $uuudds$. Together with our QCD sum-rule results, this property highlights the special role of the all-scalar-diquark configuration, $[ud][ds][su]$, as a possible deeply bound structure. However, the higher-dimensional OPE contributions exhibit significant truncation dependence, and the low-mass solution remains conditional on the adopted OPE truncation rather than constituting a fully controlled mass prediction.

</details>


### [99] [Transverse momentum dependent parton distributions at small $x$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26004)
*Paul Caucal,Farid Salazar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This chapter reviews transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) parton distributions in the small-$x$ saturation regime, using the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) framework. It covers operator definitions, gauge-link structures, Wilson-line correlators for unpolarized and polarized TMDs, factorization for inclusive/diffractive processes, evolution via BK/JIMWLK and Collins-Soper-Sterman equations, sub-eikonal corrections for spin-dependent observables, and phenomenological implications—especially for the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard TMD factorization breaks down at small Bjorken $x$ due to large $1/x$ logarithms and non-linear gluon saturation effects; a unified theoretical description bridging moderate- and small-$x$ regimes is needed.

Method: Application of the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective theory to define and compute quark and gluon TMDs at small $x$, including Wilson-line operator constructions, factorization analyses for various processes, resummation techniques (BK/JIMWLK for $1/x$, CSS for Sudakov logs), and sub-eikonal expansions for spin-dependent TMDs.

Result: A systematic CGC-based formalism for small-$x$ TMDs is established, revealing process-dependent gauge links, a limited set of universal Wilson-line operators, distinct evolution equations for different logarithmic regimes, and sub-eikonal contributions enabling access to helicity and transversity. Predictions are made for EIC observables, exposing current theoretical gaps in unifying small- and moderate-$x$ TMD descriptions.

Conclusion: The CGC provides a consistent and predictive framework for TMDs in the small-$x$ saturation regime, but a complete unified description spanning all $x$ remains an open challenge requiring further development of sub-eikonal dynamics and matching to standard TMD factorization.

Abstract: Transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) parton distributions provide a three-dimensional description of the momentum structure of hadrons. They are probed in proton-proton and proton-nucleus collisions at the LHC and RHIC, and are a central part of the physics program of the future Electron-Ion Collider (EIC). While TMD factorization is well established at moderate Bjorken $x$, the small-$x$ regime requires a different treatment, since large logarithms of $1/x$ must be resummed and non-linear effects due to high gluon densities become important. The Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective theory provides a natural framework to describe TMDs in this saturation regime and to connect them with standard TMD factorization. This chapter reviews quark and gluon TMDs at small $x$, covering operator definitions, gauge-link structures, and Wilson-line correlators in the CGC for unpolarized and polarized distributions. We discuss factorization for inclusive and diffractive observables, highlighting the role of process dependence and the emergence of a limited set of Wilson-line operators. We then summarize the evolution of these distributions, namely the resummation of logarithms of $1/x$ by the Balitsky-Kovchegov and JIMWLK equations, and of Sudakov logarithms of the hard scale through Collins-Soper-Sterman evolution. We also review TMDs beyond the eikonal approximation, including the sub-eikonal Wilson-line operators that give access to helicity and other spin-dependent distributions, their double-logarithmic small-$x$ evolution and the resulting asymptotics, and the sub-eikonal corrections to the eikonal distributions, which provide a first step toward the moderate-$x$ regime. Finally, we discuss phenomenological implications for present and future experiments, with emphasis on the EIC, and outline the theoretical challenges toward a unified description of TMDs.

</details>


### [100] [Flattenicity dependence of charge and transverse-momentum balance functions in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV with PYTHIA8](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26058)
*Srabani Dutta Roy,Rohit Agarwala,Kalyan Dey*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates charge balance function B and charge-dependent transverse-momentum balance function P₂^CD in 13.6 TeV pp collisions using PYTHIA 8.315, employing flattenicity as an event-shape observable to separate effects of multiplicity and topology; both observables show stronger, narrower near-side correlations with higher multiplicity and lower flattenicity (jet-like events), with P₂^CD more sensitive to event topology than B.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle the influence of overall event activity (multiplicity) and event topology (via flattenicity) on charge and momentum correlations in high-energy pp collisions, and to assess the complementary sensitivity of B and P₂^CD to these aspects.

Method: Monte Carlo simulation using PYTHIA 8.315 with Monash tune for pp collisions at √s = 13.6 TeV; computation of B(Δη,Δφ) and P₂^CD(Δη,Δφ); stratification by charged-particle multiplicity and flattenicity; analysis of near-side peak strength and RMS widths in (Δη,Δφ).

Result: Both B and P₂^CD exhibit enhanced, narrower near-side correlations with increasing multiplicity and decreasing flattenicity (i.e., more jet-like topology); P₂^CD shows stronger flattenicity dependence than B, indicating superior sensitivity to event shape; RMS widths systematically decrease with multiplicity and flattenicity.
