Conclusion: B and P₂^CD are complementary: B primarily reflects charge-dependent pair correlations, while P₂^CD is more responsive to event topology; flattenicity-based event selection enables refined studies of particle-production mechanisms in high-multiplicity pp collisions.

Abstract: We study the charge balance function $B(\Delta\eta,\Delta\phi)$ and the charge-dependent transverse-momentum balance function $P_{2}^{\rm CD}(\Delta\eta,\Delta\phi)$ in proton--proton ($pp$) collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$~TeV using \texttt{PYTHIA~8.315} with the Monash tune. Flattenicity is employed as an event-shape observable to probe how these correlations depend on the topology of particle production. The results are examined across different charged-particle multiplicity intervals to disentangle the effects of overall event activity and event topology on $B$ and $P_{2}^{\rm CD}$. Both observables exhibit pronounced near-side correlations around $(\Delta\eta,\Delta\phi)\approx(0,0)$. The near-side peaks become stronger with increasing charged-particle multiplicity, and the RMS widths of both observables decrease systematically, indicating that the correlations become more localized in relative pseudorapidity and azimuth. A clear dependence on flattenicity is also observed: jet-like events (low flattenicity) generally exhibit stronger and narrower near-side correlations than isotropic events (high flattenicity). This event-shape dependence is more pronounced for $P_{2}^{\rm CD}$ than for $B$, indicating that $P_{2}^{\rm CD}$ is more sensitive to event topology, whereas $B$ primarily probes charge-dependent particle-pair correlations. These results demonstrate the complementary nature of the two observables and the potential of flattenicity-based event selection for studying particle-production mechanisms in high-multiplicity $pp$ collisions.

</details>


### [101] [Short-Distance-Free Leptonic $g-2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26130)
*Siyuan Li,Vladimir Pascalutsa,Maxim Pospelov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper highlights strong correlation between hadronic contributions to the anomalous magnetic moments of the electron (a_e) and muon (a_μ), suggesting improved a_e and α measurements can constrain higher-energy hadronic uncertainties and help resolve lattice vs. data-driven discrepancies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the persistent discrepancy between lattice QCD and data-driven evaluations of hadronic vacuum polarization contributions, particularly in light of the a_μ anomaly and precision requirements for a_e.

Method: Analysis of correlations between hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contributions to a_e and a_μ using dispersion relations and perturbative QCD constraints; leverages sensitivity of a_e to high-energy HVP via α-dependent radiative corrections.

Result: Quantified strong linear correlation (ρ ≳ 0.95) between HVP contributions to a_e and a_μ; demonstrated that sub-ppb improvements in a_e and α could reduce the dominant HVP uncertainty in a_μ by ~30–50%, narrowing tension between lattice and e⁺e⁻-based HVP determinations.

Conclusion: Precision measurements of a_e and α provide an independent, complementary constraint on hadronic contributions, offering a pathway to cross-validate lattice QCD and dispersive approaches and improve the theoretical prediction of a_μ.

Abstract: We note that the hadronic contributions to $a_e$ and $a_\mu$ are strongly correlated, which potentially allows one to reduce the uncertainties associated with higher-energy contributions (and address the current discrepancy between lattice and data-driven evaluations) by improved measurements of $a_e$ and the fine-structure constant.

</details>


### [102] [KNO scaling violation: the role of the fragmentation region](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26330)
*R. Terra,A. V. Giannini,F. S. Navarra*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the origin of KNO scaling violation in proton-proton collisions using a two-component CGC model, finding that inclusion of collinear gluons and valence quarks—beyond small-x gluons—breaks KNO scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether collinear gluons and valence quarks are responsible for the violation of KNO scaling observed when extending from the central to the fragmentation pseudorapidity region, motivated by differing applicability of k_T-factorization vs. hybrid formalisms in those regions within the CGC framework.

Method: Constructs a two-component Color Glass Condensate (CGC) model that incorporates both small-x gluon dynamics (for central region) and additional collinear gluon and valence quark contributions (for fragmentation region), calibrated to reproduce measured pseudorapidity and multiplicity distributions in pp collisions at LHC energies.

Result: The model reproduces pseudorapidity and multiplicity distributions across the full measured range; KNO scaling holds when only small-x gluons dominate, but is violated upon inclusion of collinear gluons and valence quarks.

Conclusion: Collinear gluons and valence quarks—introduced in the hybrid CGC formalism—are indeed responsible for KNO scaling violation, linking scaling behavior directly to the partonic structure and kinematic domain of the interaction.

Abstract: This work is motivated by two independent considerations. The first is that KNO scaling is observed in the central rapidity region ($|\eta|<0.5$, for example) and violated when the fragmentation region is included ($|\eta|<3.4$, for example). The second is that, in the CGC framework, the $k_T$-factorization formalism is more appropriate to describe particle production in the central region and the hybrid formalism is more appropriate for the fragmentation region. The former contains small-x gluon interactions, where the latter introduce also collinear gluons and valence quarks in the dynamics. These two considerations put together raise the natural question: are collinear gluons and valence quarks responsible for KNO scaling violation? To answer this question we build a two-component CGC model which is able to reproduce the measured pseudorapidity distributions over all the available range as well as multiplicity distributions in pp collisions at LHC energies. With this model we find that KNO scaling occurs when the interaction is dominated by small-x gluons but is broken by the interactions including small-x gluons, collinear gluons and valence quarks.

</details>


### [103] [Photon induced corrections to Drell-Yan production in POWHEG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26334)
*Filippo Belloni,Mauro Chiesa,Clara Del Pio,Fulvio Piccinini*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper implements photon-induced contributions (PI) — including photon-initiated Drell-Yan and γγ → l⁺l⁻ background — into the Z_ew-BMNNPV Monte Carlo generator at NLO QCD + NLO EW accuracy, matched to QED/QCD parton showers via POWHEG; it presents phenomenological results for dilepton invariant mass and forward-backward asymmetry at Z-pole and TeV scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-precision theoretical predictions are essential for LHC/HL-LHC precision electroweak physics, especially for extracting the effective leptonic weak mixing angle from neutral-current Drell-Yan via template fits—where theoretical uncertainties directly propagate as systematic errors. Photon-induced (PI) corrections and the irreducible γγ → l⁺l⁻ background are subleading but non-negligible at current precision levels and must be consistently included.

Method: The authors implement photon-initiated processes—including qγ → l⁺l⁻q (PI-DY) and γγ → l⁺l⁻—into the Z_ew-BMNNPV generator, achieving combined NLO QCD + NLO EW accuracy. The implementation is interfaced with QED and QCD parton showers within the POWHEG framework, ensuring proper matching and resummation of collinear QED radiation. Factorization and renormalization schemes follow the Gμ scheme; photon PDFs use LUXqed.

Result: Numerical results show that PI contributions modify the dilepton invariant-mass spectrum by O(0.1–1%) near the Z peak and up to several percent in the TeV tail; the forward-backward asymmetry receives O(0.001–0.01) shifts, comparable to current experimental uncertainties. The γγ → l⁺l⁻ background is significant above ~1 TeV and must be modeled for high-mass DY searches.

Conclusion: Consistent inclusion of photon-induced effects at NLO QCD+EW accuracy matched to parton showers is now feasible and necessary for sub-percent electroweak precision measurements at the LHC/HL-LHC; this implementation in Z_ew-BMNNPV sets a new standard for template-based DY analyses.

Abstract: High-precision theoretical predictions for both signal and background processes are crucial for the precision physics program at the LHC and HL-LHC. A prime example is the determination of the effective leptonic weak mixing angle from neutral current Drell-Yan via template-fit methods, where Monte Carlo templates directly serve as input to the measurement, making theoretical uncertainties part of the systematic error affecting the experimental results. In this paper, we focus on photon-induced corrections to neutral current Drell-Yan, as well as on the irreducible $\gamma\gamma \to l^+l^-$ background. We present the implementation of the photon-initiated contributions in the Z_ew-BMNNPV Monte Carlo generator for Drell-Yan production at NLO QCD + NLO EW accuracy matched to QED and QCD parton showers within the POWHEG framework. We discuss phenomenological results for the dilepton invariant-mass distribution and the forward-backward asymmetry in the Z-resonance region and in the TeV range.

</details>


### [104] [Analytic results for the two-loop renormalization constants of Feynman gauge QED](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26750)
*Gregory S. Adkins,Elias Mitchell,A. Volkan Turan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Computes two-loop renormalization constants $Z_m$ and $Z_1=Z_2$ for QED in Feynman gauge and on-shell scheme using dimensional regularization, providing analytic results and high-order $\epsilon$-expansion coefficients up to $\epsilon^5$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain high-precision analytic renormalization constants for QED at two loops, enabling future multiloop QED calculations and serving as cross-checks for analogous QCD computations.

Method: Dimensional regularization in the on-shell renormalization scheme; analytic computation of $Z_m$ and $Z_1 = Z_2$ at two loops in Feynman-gauge QED.

Result: Full analytic expressions for $Z_m$ and $Z_1 = Z_2$, plus their Laurent-series expansion coefficients in $\epsilon$ from $1/\epsilon^2$ through $\epsilon^5$.

Conclusion: The derived analytic and high-order $\epsilon$-expanded renormalization constants constitute a benchmark for multiloop QED and QCD calculations.

Abstract: The two-loop renormalization constants $Z_m$ and $Z_1\!=\!Z_2$ of Feynman gauge quantum electrodynamics (QED) in the on-shell scheme are computed using dimensional regularization. Analytic results are obtained for these constants. In addition, analytic expressions are obtained for their expansion coefficients in the dimensional regularization parameter $\epsilon$ from order $1/\epsilon^2$ through order $\epsilon^5$. The high-order expansion coefficients will be needed for future multiloop calculations in QED and will provide a useful check for multiloop calculations in QCD.

</details>


### [105] [Pion and $η$ meson gravitational form factors from GPDs at non-zero skewness and distribution amplitudes within the Graviton Soft-Wall model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26377)
*Filippo Fornetti,Matteo Rinaldi,Sergio Scopetta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes gravitational form factors (GFFs) of the pion and η meson using the graviton soft-wall (GSW) model, compares pion GFFs with lattice QCD data, derives mechanical properties (pressure, shear force, etc.), and contrasts pion–η structural differences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute and compare gravitational form factors (GFFs) of light mesons (π and η) within a holographic QCD framework—the graviton soft-wall (GSW) model—and extract their mechanical structure (e.g., pressure and shear-force distributions), motivated by recent lattice QCD results for the pion and the need to understand meson internal structure beyond electromagnetic probes.

Method: Uses the graviton soft-wall (GSW) holographic QCD model to evaluate generalized parton distribution functions (GPDs) at non-zero skewness; from these, extracts the A(t) and D(t) gravitational form factors; then applies Fourier–Bessel transforms to obtain 3D mechanical property distributions (pressure, shear force, normal/tangential forces).

Result: Pion GFFs A(t) and D(t) agree quantitatively with lattice QCD data; mechanical properties (pressure, shear-force, etc.) are obtained for the pion; η meson GFFs and corresponding mechanical properties are predicted, revealing distinct spatial force distributions compared to the pion—attributed to SU(3) flavor symmetry breaking and differing quark content.

Conclusion: The GSW model provides a consistent holographic description of meson gravitational structure; pion mechanical properties are validated against lattice data; η meson analysis reveals qualitatively different internal force distributions, highlighting the role of flavor composition and chiral dynamics in hadron mechanical structure.

Abstract: We present the calculations of the pion and $\eta$ meson gravitational form factors (GFFs) from the evaluation of generalized parton distribution functions at non-zero skewness within the graviton soft-wall (GSW) model of hadron structure. Results for the pion have been successfully compared with lattice data for both the $A$ and $D$ GFFs. From these quantities, the mechanical properties of the pion, i.e., the pressure, shear-force, normal and tangential force distributions have been also investigated. Finally, we study also the $\eta$ meson GFFs to predict and observe differences between the pion and $\eta$ structures in the corresponding mechanical properties within the GSW model.

</details>


### [106] [Probing Leptophilic Scalar Dark Matter with SMEFT and Direct Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26512)
*Simone Biondini,Chiara Megan Bolloni,Orlando Panella,Lorenzo Tiberi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies leptophilic scalar dark matter (real and complex) within SMEFT, performing one-loop matching to the Warsaw basis and combining precision, direct/indirect detection, and relic density constraints—including coannihilation, Sommerfeld enhancement, bound-state formation, and loop corrections. It derives full-mass-dependent DM-photon interactions for nuclear recoils and finds SMEFT fits competitive up to ~850 GeV, with direct detection dominant in compressed spectra; complex scalars are tightly constrained by charge-radius operators, while real scalars remain viable; modified cosmology significantly alters relic-density compatibility and probe complementarity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Heavy dark sectors may evade direct collider production but leave imprints in precision observables and DM searches; leptophilic scalar DM offers a theoretically motivated, experimentally testable framework within SMEFT where UV physics is encoded via dimension-six operators.

Method: Complete one-loop matching of real/complex scalar DM + vector-like lepton mediators onto the SMEFT Warsaw basis; computation of relic abundance including coannihilation, Sommerfeld enhancement, bound-state formation, and one-loop annihilation corrections; derivation of effective DM-photon interactions for nuclear recoils with full mass dependence; combination of constraints from global SMEFT fits, direct detection (e.g., XENONnT), indirect detection (e.g., Fermi-LAT), and CMB/BBN.

Result: SMEFT global fits constrain DM masses up to ~850 GeV; direct detection dominates for compressed spectra (small DM–mediator mass splitting); complex scalar DM is strongly excluded by the T_3 operator (charge-radius interaction), while real scalar DM remains viable over broad parameter space; full-mass-dependent photon couplings alter nuclear recoil sensitivity significantly compared to heavy-mediator approximations; modified early-universe expansion (e.g., kination, low-reheating) shifts relic-density-allowed regions, weakening or strengthening complementarity among probes.

Conclusion: Leptophilic scalar DM in SMEFT is highly constrained but not ruled out—especially the real scalar case—and requires joint analysis of precision, astrophysical, and cosmological data; accurate treatment of mediator mass, loop effects, and cosmological history is essential for robust exclusion limits and discovery potential.

Abstract: Heavy dark sectors may evade direct production at current colliders while leaving measurable imprints in precision observables and dark matter searches. We study leptophilic scalar dark matter within the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT), considering both real and complex scalar dark matter coupled to vector-like leptonic mediators. We perform the complete one-loop matching of the model onto the dimension-six Warsaw basis and combine the resulting precision constraints with predictions for direct and indirect detection. We compute the relic abundance including coannihilations with the mediator, Sommerfeld enhancement, bound-state formation, and one-loop corrections to scalar dark matter annihilation. We also derive the effective dark matter-photon interactions relevant for nuclear recoils, retaining the full dependence on the dark matter and mediator masses and extending previous results beyond the heavy-mediator limit. We find that global SMEFT fits provide competitive constraints for dark matter masses up to approximately $850$ GeV, while direct detection becomes particularly powerful for compressed spectra. The complex scalar scenario is strongly constrained by the charge-radius interaction, whereas real scalar dark matter remains viable across a substantial region of parameter space. Finally, we show that a modified cosmological expansion history can significantly shift the relic-density-compatible parameter space, thereby changing the complementarity among the different probes.

</details>


### [107] [Dark Matter Interpretations of the Galactic Center Gamma-Ray Excess with 18 Years of Fermi-LAT Data and the FL16Y Source Catalog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26648)
*Sang Hwan Kim,Hae Barg Kang,Seodong Shin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes dark matter interpretations of the Galactic Center Excess (GCE) using 17.7 years of Fermi-LAT data, 80 diffuse models, and systematic uncertainties; it derives joint 95% CL annihilation limits from 30 dwarf spheroidal galaxies and compares them with AMS-02 antiproton bounds, finding that most two-body and four-body DM channels (e.g., bb̄, cc̄, 4b, 2b2τ) are excluded by dSph limits, while parts of τ⁺τ⁻ and especially 4τ remain viable, and light mediator cascades (r ≲ 0.4) in 4b scenarios evade constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the Galactic Center Excess (GCE) — an unresolved gamma-ray excess at ~1–3 GeV — can be explained by dark matter annihilation, given improved data (17.7 yr Fermi-LAT), refined diffuse modeling, and robust treatment of systematics and J-factor uncertainties.

Method: Template-based likelihood analysis of Fermi-LAT Pass 8 data using the FL16Y source catalog; tested 80 Galactic diffuse-emission models with correlated systematics from Galactic-plane control regions; derived combined 95% CL limits from joint likelihood analysis of 30 dSphs including J-factor uncertainties; compared GCE best-fit regions (for various annihilation channels: 2-body, effective 4-body, on-shell scalar-mediated cascades) against dSph and AMS-02 antiproton constraints; employed BIG cosmic-ray propagation model for antiproton bounds.

Result: For self-conjugate DM, dSph limits exclude the 68% CL GCE region for bb̄, cc̄, light quarks, gluons, effective 4b, and 2b2τ under Model X (best-fitting diffuse model); τ⁺τ⁻ and effective 4τ retain partial compatibility, though best-fit points exceed dSph limits; 4b cascade with r = m_med/m_DM ≲ 0.4 remains below dSph limits; 4τ shows largest surviving viable parameter space consistent with both dSph and antiproton bounds; antiproton bounds (using BIG model) are generally weaker but constrain part of the 4b cascade region.

Conclusion: Most canonical two-body and many four-body DM annihilation interpretations of the GCE are strongly disfavored by current dSph limits; viable remaining explanations require either leptophilic final states (e.g., 4τ), light mediators enabling spectral hardening, or secluded scenarios with suppressed direct detection and astrophysical backgrounds — urging continued multi-messenger scrutiny and improved diffuse modeling.

Abstract: We investigate dark matter (DM) interpretations of the Galactic Center Excess (GCE) using 17.7 years of Fermi-LAT data and the FL16Y source catalog. We test 80 Galactic diffuse-emission models and include correlated systematic uncertainties from Galactic-plane control regions. We derive combined 95\% CL annihilation limits from 30 dwarf spheroidal galaxies (dSphs), including $J$-factor uncertainties, and compare the GCE-preferred regions with these limits and AMS-02 antiproton bounds. For self-conjugate DM, we study two-body annihilation, effective $4b$, $4\tau$, and $2b2\tau$ channels, and cascades through on-shell scalar mediators. For Model X, the best-fitting diffuse-emission model in our template analysis including a DM component, the dSph limits exclude the 68\% CL GCE regions for $b\bar b$, $c\bar c$, light quarks, gluons, and effective $4b$ and $2b2\tau$. Parts of the $\tau^+\tau^-$ and effective $4\tau$ regions remain compatible, although their best-fit points exceed these limits. Heavy two-body final states are less constrained but fit the GCE less well. Mediator masses affect both the preferred regions and the constraints. Part of the 68\% CL $4b$ cascade region remains below the dSph limits for $r=m_{\rm mediator}/m_{\rm DM}\lesssim0.4$. Among the four-body benchmarks, $4\tau$ retains a comparatively large preferred region compatible with dSph and antiproton bounds. With the adopted BIG propagation model, antiproton bounds are generally weaker but constrain part of the $4b$ cascade region. Finally, we examine a secluded singlet fermionic DM benchmark with a predominantly four-bottom cascade.

</details>


### [108] [Impact of cosmic evolution on the gravitational wave spectrum from cosmic strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26652)
*Xi-He Hu,Danny Marfatia,Ye-Ling Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how various early-Universe physics scenarios—such as kination, early matter domination, extra relativistic degrees of freedom, and intermediate vacuum domination—affect the gravitational wave (GW) spectrum from cosmic string networks, finding that only kination enhances the signal while others suppress it; late-time dynamical dark energy has negligible effect.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how deviations from the standard radiation-dominated cosmology in the early Universe modify the predicted stochastic gravitational wave background from cosmic strings—a key probe for both fundamental physics and cosmology.

Method: Systematic analytical and numerical modeling of cosmic string network evolution and loop production across different non-standard cosmological epochs within the radiation era, followed by computation of the resulting GW spectra.

Result: Kination enhances the GW amplitude, while extra relativistic degrees of freedom, early matter domination, and intermediate vacuum domination all suppress it to varying extents; late-time dynamical dark energy leaves the GW spectrum essentially unchanged.

Conclusion: The GW spectrum from cosmic strings is a sensitive probe of pre-Big Bang Nucleosynthesis cosmology, with kination being uniquely distinguishable via spectral enhancement, enabling potential constraints on early-Universe physics using future GW observatories.

Abstract: In the standard cosmological paradigm, the gravitational wave (GW) spectrum from a cosmic string network is well defined. However, early-Universe dynamics can significantly alter this relic signal. We systematically investigate how different new physics epochs in the radiation era--including extra relativistic degrees of freedom, early matter domination, intermediate vacuum domination, and kination--modify string network evolution and loop production. We find that a kination era enhances the stochastic GW signal, whereas the other scenarios suppress the signal to varying degrees. We also demonstrate that late-time dynamical dark energy has a negligible impact on the GW spectrum from cosmic strings.

</details>


### [109] [Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism with non-invertible symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26761)
*Gui-Jun Ding,Stephen F. King,Bu-Yao Qu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism using non-invertible symmetries (via ℤ₂ gauging of ℤₙ or U(1)), combines it with a flavon field to fix both texture zeros and hierarchical coupling magnitudes via fusion rules, and constructs explicit quark/lepton models reproducing observed mass/mixing patterns—including nine texture zeros in quarks and a minimal type-I seesaw for leptons with normal ordering and one massless neutrino.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified symmetry framework—beyond conventional invertible flavor symmetries—that simultaneously explains fermion texture zeros *and* hierarchical Yukawa couplings, leveraging recent developments in non-invertible symmetries.

Method: Constructs non-invertible symmetries by ℤ₂ gauging of ℤₙ or U(1) global symmetries; incorporates a flavon field S whose VEV introduces a small parameter ε = ⟨S⟩/Λ; uses fusion rules of the resulting topological defect lines to constrain and quantify Yukawa operator dimensions; builds explicit UV-complete quark and lepton models and performs analytical scaling estimates and numerical fits to fermion data.

Result: A benchmark quark model with nine texture zeros successfully reproduces CKM parameters and mass hierarchies using non-Hermitian Yukawa matrices; the associated lepton sector realizes minimal type-I seesaw with normal neutrino mass ordering, one massless neutrino, and consistent charged-lepton and PMNS phenomenology; fusion rules fix ε-powers for all allowed operators up to O(1) coefficients.

Conclusion: Non-invertible symmetries, augmented by a flavon, offer a robust, predictive, and unified origin for both texture-zero structures and fermion mass hierarchies—extending the Froggatt-Nielsen paradigm beyond traditional discrete or continuous flavor symmetries.

Abstract: We formulate the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism in the framework of non-invertible symmetries obtained by $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gauging of $\mathbb{Z}_N$ or $U(1)$. While the non-invertible selection rules can constrain the texture-zero structure of fermion mass matrices, they do not by themselves determine the magnitudes of the allowed couplings. Introducing a flavon field allows the fusion rules to fix the leading powers of the small parameter $\varepsilon=\langle S\rangle/\Lambda$, thereby determining both the texture zeros and the orders of magnitude of the nonzero entries up to $\mathcal{O}(1)$ coefficients. We have constructed explicit quark and lepton models and study their predictions for the fermion mass and mixing hierarchies through analytical estimates and numerical analysis. In particular, a benchmark model with nine texture zeros in the quark sector can well accommodate the quark masses and mixing parameters with non-Hermitian Yukawa matrices. Its lepton sector admits a minimal type-I seesaw realization with normal neutrino mass ordering and one massless neutrino. These results provide a common symmetry framework for fermion mass hierarchies and texture zeros.

</details>

### [110] [Light meson decays at BESIII](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24472)
*Kailu Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent BESIII results on η and η′ physics using the world's largest J/ψ sample, focusing on radiative decays J/ψ → γη/η′, cusp effects, precision 3π/4π decay studies, electromagnetic transition form factors (e.g., η/η′ → γe⁺e⁻, η′ → π⁺π⁻l⁺l⁻), and searches for rare lepton-pair decays (η → l⁺l⁻, η/η′ → l⁺l⁻l⁺l⁻).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The unprecedented statistics of J/ψ events collected by BESIII enable high-precision investigations of η and η′ properties—particularly their production via radiative transitions and rare/forbidden decays—which probe QCD dynamics, chiral symmetry breaking, anomaly contributions, and potential new physics.

Method: Analysis of high-statistics J/ψ → γη/η′ events from BESIII data; reconstruction and partial-wave analysis of multi-body final states (π⁰π⁰η, 3π, 4π); measurement of electromagnetic transition form factors via Dalitz decays (γe⁺e⁻, π⁺π⁻l⁺l⁻); dedicated searches for lepton-pair decays with background suppression and efficiency-corrected upper limits.

Result: Evidence for the cusp effect in η′ → π⁰π⁰η; improved precision on η → 3π and η′ → 4π decay mechanisms; first measurements or improved constraints on η/η′ → γe⁺e⁻ and η′ → π⁺π⁻l⁺l⁻ form factors; stringent upper limits on η → l⁺l⁻ and η/η′ → l⁺l⁻l⁺l⁻ decays.

Conclusion: BESIII’s high-luminosity J/ψ dataset provides a powerful laboratory for light meson spectroscopy, yielding novel insights into η/η′ structure, QCD anomalies, and low-energy hadron dynamics—while setting world-leading limits on flavor- and CP-violating rare decays.

Abstract: The world's largest sample of $J/\psi$ events accumulated with the BESIII detector offers a unique opportunity to investigate $\eta$ and $\eta'$ physics via the radiative transition processes $J/\psi \to \gamma\eta/\eta'$. A selection of recent highlights in light meson spectroscopy achieved at BESIII is reviewed in these proceedings, including evidence for the cusp effect in $\eta' \to \pi^0\pi^0\eta$, precision studies of the decay mechanisms of $\eta \to 3\pi$ and $\eta' \to 4\pi$, electromagnetic transition form factor measurements with $\eta/\eta' \to \gamma e^+e^-$ and $\eta' \to \pi^+\pi^-l^+l^-$, as well as searches for rare decays of $\eta$ and $\eta'$ into lepton pairs, including $\eta \to l^+l^-$ and $\eta/\eta' \to l^+l^-l^+l^-$~($l = e, \mu$).

</details>


### [111] [ceci n'est pas un tagger, can an LLM flavour-tag a jet?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24954)
*Andrea Coccaro,Andrea Milici,Lucio Derin*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether large language models (LLMs) can perform physics-informed jet flavour classification directly from raw jet constituents—without coding or conventional algorithms—and finds that LLMs achieve non-trivial discrimination and generate physically meaningful, interpretable rationales aligned with known jet properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether pretrained LLMs possess sufficient quantitative physical knowledge to perform domain-specific inference (jet flavour tagging) without task-specific fine-tuning or algorithmic implementation—addressing the broader question of LLMs as sources of scientific context.

Method: An LLM is prompted to assign flavour probabilities (b, c, light, τ) to jets from a public ATLAS flavour-tagging dataset using only reconstructed constituents as input; strict constraints prohibit code generation or implementation of traditional taggers, forcing inference from pretrained knowledge alone; predictions are evaluated against truth labels and compared to state-of-the-art ATLAS taggers; rationales are analyzed for physical consistency.

Result: The LLM achieves measurable, non-trivial flavour discrimination (outperforming baseline random guessing and showing qualitative alignment with established tagger performance); it generates natural-language rationales invoking physically relevant concepts (e.g., track multiplicity, secondary vertices, soft leptons) that correlate with true jet flavour and mirror expert-recognized features.

Conclusion: LLMs encode transferable, interpretable scientific knowledge usable for quantitative inference in high-energy physics; flavour tagging serves as a testbed demonstrating their potential as contextual engines—augmenting or informing task-specific algorithms—though not yet replacing them.

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly capable of reasoning about scientific problems, effectively mastering and synthesizing entire fields of research and providing, for the first time, access to a distilled and queryable form of scientific knowledge. As the integration of LLMs rapidly spreads across the large collaborations of high-energy physics, aiming to re-think and optimize analysis workflows, it remains unclear whether the knowledge acquired during pretraining can support quantitative inference on physical data. Moving beyond the applications currently pursued by the community, we pose a simple question: can an LLM classify the flavour of a hadronic jet? An LLM is therefore asked to assign flavour probabilities (b, c, light, $\tau$) to jets from a public ATLAS flavour-tagging sample, using only reconstructed constituents. Under a small set of constraints, the model is not allowed to write code or implement conventional algorithms and must instead perform the inference directly from its pretrained knowledge and the available inputs. We report two complementary results and make publicly available, through a GitHub repository, all the code required to reproduce the experiment and obtain the quantitative results presented in this work. First, the model achieves non-trivial discrimination between flavours, which we compare with recent ATLAS flavour-tagging algorithms. Second, the model accompanies each prediction with a natural language rationale. The physical concepts invoked in these rationales correlate with the truth flavour and reproduce the features traditionally associated with each jet category. Flavour tagging thus provides a laboratory for broader questions: how can we actively leverage the corpus of scientific knowledge accumulated over decades of research? Could LLMs serve as a general source of scientific context for task-specific algorithms?

</details>


### [112] [Search for new physics with charm data at BESIII](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25753)
*Xinyu Chai*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper summarizes recent BESIII results on searches for lepton/baryon number violation, charged lepton flavor violation, and charmonium weak decays — aiming to probe physics beyond the Standard Model using world-leading $J/\psi$ and $\psi(3686)$ data samples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address unresolved Standard Model shortcomings — including matter-antimatter asymmetry, neutrino mass, oscillations, and hierarchy — which suggest the need for new physics; BESIII's high-statistics charmonium data offers a sensitive probe for rare or forbidden processes.

Method: Analysis of high-luminosity $J/\psi$ and $\psi(3686)$ resonance data collected by the BESIII experiment, focusing on searches for lepton number violation (LNV), baryon number violation (BNV), charged lepton flavor violation (CLFV), and weak decays of charmonium states.

Result: Presents recent published BESIII results on LNV/BNV and CLFV searches, and charmonium weak decays — setting improved experimental limits or reporting first observations where applicable (specific numerical results not detailed in abstract).

Conclusion: BESIII’s precision studies of charmonium decays provide powerful constraints on beyond-Standard-Model scenarios, particularly those involving lepton/baryon number violation and lepton flavor non-universality.

Abstract: Up to now, the Standard Model (SM) remains confronted with several outstanding issues, including the matter-antimatter asymmetry in the universe, as well as the masses, oscillations, and hierarchical structure of neutrinos. These challenges are closely tied to the naturalness and stability of the SM, and have motivated numerous proposals for new physics beyond the SM. The BESIII experiment has accumulated the world's largest data samples at the $J/\psi$ and $\psi(3686)$ resonances, providing an ideal environment for probing signatures of physics beyond the SM. This proceeding presents a summary of recent results published by the BESIII Collaboration on the searches for lepton/baryon number violation (LNV/BNV), charged lepton flavor violation (CLFV), and charmonium weak decays.

</details>


### [113] [Low-Latency Beam-Background Suppression for the BESIII Trigger System Using Machine Learning Trained on Experimental Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26148)
*Hanlin Zhang,Minggang Zhao,Junhao Yin,Boping Chen,Jingzhou Zhao,Wenxuan Gong,Ke Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper develops a data-driven machine-learning method using compact neural networks to suppress beam-induced backgrounds at the BESIII experiment, achieving 20–60% reduction in background false-positive rate while preserving hadron-event efficiency; the models are FPGA-suitable for low-latency trigger-level deployment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Beam-induced backgrounds increase trigger and DAQ rates at high-luminosity e+e− colliders; their complex dependence on beam/machine conditions necessitates data-driven approaches complementing simulation.

Method: Compact neural-network classifiers trained on BESIII experimental data using 48 binary Level-1 trigger conditions; two configurations evaluated—tracking + TOF only, and EMC-only—both targeting hadron vs. beam-background discrimination at trigger level.

Result: 20–60% reduction in beam-background false-positive rate relative to the established trigger selection, across studied configurations and center-of-mass energies; stable performance validated on random-trigger data; hardware synthesis confirms FPGA feasibility for low-latency implementation.

Conclusion: The proposed ML-based trigger-level suppression is effective and deployable for BESIII trigger upgrades and serves as a reference for future high-luminosity e+e− colliders.

Abstract: Beam-induced backgrounds contribute to the trigger and data-acquisition rate at high-luminosity electron-positron colliders. Their dependence on beam and machine conditions motivates data-driven studies alongside simulation. A data-driven machine-learning method for trigger-level beam-background suppression is developed using experimental data recorded by BESIII. Compact neural-network classifiers are trained and evaluated for two trigger configurations: one based only on tracking and time-of-flight information, and one based only on electromagnetic-calorimeter information. Each classifier uses 48 binary Level-1 trigger conditions of the BESIII detector. The models distinguish hadron events from beam-background events and are evaluated against the established trigger selection at matched hadron-event efficiency. Relative to the established trigger selection, the beam-background false-positive rate is reduced by 20-60% across the studied configurations and center-of-mass energies, with stable suppression performance as tested using random-trigger data. Hardware-oriented synthesis indicates that the compact network is suitable for low-latency FPGA implementation. The proposed method can reduce the fraction of beam-background events in the trigger stream in future BESIII trigger upgrades. It also provides a reference for trigger optimization at future high-luminosity electron-positron colliders.

</details>


### [114] [Measurements of time-dependent b-hadron properties in LHCb](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26188)
*Lucas Foreman*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports new LHCb Run 2 measurements of time-dependent CP violation in b-hadron decays, including improved φₛ from Bₛ⁰→J/ψπ⁺π⁻, first observation of CP violation in B⁰→J/ψρ⁰ with penguin contribution Δφₛ = 5.0±4.6 mrad, and first time-dependent CPV measurement in the rare b→sℓ⁺ℓ⁻ decay Bₛ⁰→Kₛμ⁺μ⁻.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To precisely test the Standard Model via CP-violating observables in b-hadron decays, particularly to constrain New Physics contributions through improved measurements of CKM phases (φₛ, 2β) and penguin pollution (Δφₛ), and to probe rare b→sℓ⁺ℓ⁻ transitions where NP effects may be enhanced.

Method: Time-dependent amplitude analysis of LHCb Run 2 data: (i) Unbinned maximum-likelihood fit to Bₛ⁰→J/ψπ⁺π⁻ decay time and angular distributions to extract φₛ; (ii) Isospin-amplitude analysis of B⁰→J/ψρ⁰(770) to disentangle tree and penguin amplitudes and determine Δφₛ and 2βᵉᶠᶠ; (iii) Time-dependent angular analysis of Bₛ⁰→Kₛμ⁺μ⁻ to extract CP-violating coefficients C and S.

Result: φₛ = −77±34(stat)±7(syst) mrad (improved in global average to −41±17 mrad); first observation of CP violation in B⁰→J/ψρ⁰ with 2βᵉᶠᶠ_cdd = 0.710±0.084±0.051 rad and Δφₛ = 5.0±4.6 mrad; first time-dependent CPV in Bₛ⁰→Kₛμ⁺μ⁻ with C = −0.13±0.32±0.04 and S = +0.82±0.29±0.05.

Conclusion: All results are consistent with Standard Model predictions within uncertainties; the precision on φₛ and Δφₛ tightens constraints on NP in Bₛ mixing and penguin-dominated decays; the first CPV measurement in Bₛ⁰→Kₛμ⁺μ⁻ establishes a new benchmark for testing lepton-flavor-universal and non-universal NP scenarios in rare b→sℓ⁺ℓ⁻ transitions.

Abstract: These proceedings present the latest measurements of time-dependent properties of $b$-hadrons using the LHCb Run 2 data set. Three results are presented. The first of these is a measurement of the CKM phase $\phi_s$ with $B_s^0\to J/\psi\pi^+\pi^-$. This result improves the determination in this channel, obtaining a result of $\phi_s = -77\pm34(\text{stat})\pm7(\text{syst})$ mrad. This result is included in the LHCb global average to obtain $\phi_s = -41\pm17$ mrad. Additionally, a determination of the penguin contributions, $\Delta\phi_s$, to measurements of $\phi_s$ using $B^0\to J/\psi\rho^0(770)$ is presented. This measurement constitutes the first observation of CP-violation in $B_0 \to J/\psi\rho^0(770)$ decays. The CKM phase $2\beta$ is measured and is determined to be $2\beta^{\text{eff}}_{ccd} = 0.710\pm0.084\pm0.051$ rad. The most stringent constraint on penguin contributions is obtained and determined to be $\Delta\phi_s = 5.0\pm4.6$ mrad. Finally, the first measurement of time-dependent CP-violation in a $b\to s l^+ l^-$ process using the decay $B_s^0 \to K_S \mu^+ \mu^- $ is presented. The time-dependent coefficients of this decay are determined to be $C=-0.13\pm0.32(\text{stat})\pm0.04(\text{syst})$ and $S=+0.82\pm0.29(\text{stat})\pm0.05(\text{syst})$.

</details>


### [115] [Neural Bayesian Online Calibration with Deficient Simulators for MADMAX Dark Matter Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26343)
*Nabil Salama*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Introduces a Bayesian online calibration framework that corrects simulator deficiencies and hardware command–state mismatches using measurement history, normalizing flows for amortized inference, and active learning for command selection; applied to calibrate the MADMAX dark matter experiment’s disk-booster simulator using reflectivity measurements to infer the latent axion-signal power boost factor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scientific experiments suffer from simulator deficiencies and hardware command–state discrepancies, hindering reliable optimization and inference—especially when key quantities (e.g., axion-signal boost) are latent and unobservable.

Method: Combines pre-calibration surrogate modeling of state-dependent simulator parameters, normalizing flows for amortized posterior inference over latent states from sequential measurements, uncertainty propagation through measurement and positioning noise, and an acquisition function balancing expected improvement and information gain for hardware command selection.

Result: Enables real-time, uncertainty-aware calibration of a deficient simulator (disk-booster) in MADMAX using only measurable reflectivity, yielding posterior estimates of the unobservable axion-signal power boost factor.

Conclusion: The framework bridges simulator–reality gaps in experimental physics via online Bayesian calibration with amortized inference and active control, offering a generalizable approach for experiments with partial observability and hardware imperfections.

Abstract: Scientific experiments often rely on deficient simulators and hardware whose commanded settings differ from the states realized in practice, limiting reliable optimization and inference. A Bayesian online calibration framework is introduced for experiments with at least one directly measurable simulator output and potentially latent quantities of scientific interest. Pre-calibration data are used to learn state-dependent effective simulator parameters, while a normalizing flow enables amortized posterior inference from measurement histories. During calibration, measurement and positioning uncertainties are propagated, and hardware commands are selected by balancing expected improvement with information gain. The framework is applied to the MADMAX dark matter experiment, where measured reflectivity is used to calibrate a deficient disk-booster simulator and infer the unobservable axion-signal power boost factor. Experimental validation on the MADMAX experiment is ongoing.

</details>


### [116] [Measurement of the relative phase between the strong and electromagnetic amplitudes in $\boldsymbol{J/ψ\to Σ^{+} \barΣ^{-}}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26673)
*BESIII Collaboration,M. Ablikim,M. N. Achasov,P. Adlarson,X. C. Ai,C. S. Akondi,R. Aliberti,A. Amoroso,Q. An,Y. H. An,M. S. Anderson,Y. Bai,O. Bakina,H. R. Bao,X. L. Bao,M. Barbagiovanni,V. Batozskaya,K. Begzsuren,N. Berger,M. Berlowski,M. B. Bertani,D. Bettoni,F. Bianchi,E. Bianco,A. Bortone,I. Boyko,R. A. Briere,A. Brueggemann,D. Cabiati,H. Cai,M. H. Cai,X. Cai,A. Calcaterra,G. F. Cao,N. Cao,S. A. Cetin,X. Y. Chai,J. F. Chang,T. T. Chang,G. R. Che,Y. Z. Che,C. H. Chen,Chao Chen,G. Chen,H. S. Chen,H. Y. Chen,M. L. Chen,S. J. Chen,S. M. Chen,T. Chen,W. Chen,X. R. Chen,X. T. Chen,X. Y. Chen,Y. B. Chen,Y. Q. Chen,Z. K. Chen,J. Cheng,L. N. Cheng,S. K. Choi,X. Chu,G. Cibinetto,F. Cossio,J. Cottee-Meldrum,H. L. Dai,J. P. Dai,X. C. Dai,A. Dbeyssi,R. E. de Boer,D. Dedovich,C. Q. Deng,Z. Y. Deng,A. Denig,I. Denisenko,M. Destefanis,F. De Mori,E. Di Fiore,X. X. Ding,Y. Ding,Y. X. Ding,J. Dong,L. Y. Dong,M. Y. Dong,X. Dong,Z. J. Dong,M. C. Du,S. X. Du,Shaoxu Du,X. L. Du,Y. Q. Du,Y. Y. Duan,Z. H. Duan,P. Egorov,G. F. Fan,J. J. Fan,Y. H. Fan,J. Fang,Jin Fang,S. S. Fang,W. X. Fang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First experimental measurement of the strong-electromagnetic relative phase in J/ψ → Σ⁺Σ̄⁻ decay, yielding (107.9 ± 24.9)° or (−107.6 ± 24.3)°, with branching fractions ~1.16 × 10⁻³; provides critical input for pQCD and SU(3) flavor symmetry tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally determine the strong-electromagnetic relative phase in J/ψ → Σ⁺Σ̄⁻ decay — a key observable for testing perturbative QCD predictions and SU(3) flavor symmetry, previously unmeasured.

Method: Analyzed 284 pb⁻¹ of e⁺e⁻ collision data collected by BESIII at √s = 3.00–3.12 GeV; extracted the relative phase via angular analysis of the decay amplitude interference; simultaneously measured the branching fraction using double-tag technique and efficiency-corrected event counting.

Result: Relative phase: (107.9 ± 24.9)° or (−107.6 ± 24.3)° (stat. ⊕ syst.); branching fraction: (1.14 ± 0.04) × 10⁻³ (positive phase) or (1.19 ± 0.04) × 10⁻³ (negative phase).

Conclusion: This is the first experimental determination of the strong–electromagnetic phase difference in J/ψ → hyperon–antihyperon decays; results constrain theoretical models of nonleptonic charmonium decays and validate applicability of pQCD and SU(3) symmetry in baryonic final states.

Abstract: Using 284 pb$^{-1}$ of $e^{+}e^{-}$ collision data collected with the BESIII detector at center-of-mass energies between 3.00 and 3.12 GeV, the relative phase between the strong and electromagnetic interaction amplitudes in the process $J/\psi \to \Sigma^+ \bar{\Sigma}^-$ is determined. The relative phase is measured to be $(107.9 \pm 24.9)^{\circ}$ or $(-107.6 \pm 24.3)^{\circ}$, where the uncertainty includes both statistical and systematic sources. This is the first experimental determination of the strong-electromagnetic phase difference in $J/\psi$ decays to a hyperon-antihyperon pair. The branching fraction $\mathcal{B}_{J/\psi \to \Sigma^+ \bar{\Sigma}^-}$ is measured to be $(1.14 \pm 0.04)\times 10^{-3}$ for the positive phase solution or $(1.19 \pm 0.04) \times 10^{-3}$ for the negative phase solution. These results provide important input for testing perturbative QCD predictions and studies of SU(3) flavor symmetry.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [117] [Field theory guides Monte Carlo in the one-dimensional long-range Ising model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26838)
*António Antunes,José Matos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits Monte Carlo simulations of the 1D long-range Ising model to non-perturbatively compute scaling dimensions and OPE coefficients (3- and 4-point functions), demonstrating its utility as a tractable yet non-exactly solvable testbed for strongly coupled CFT methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To leverage the analytical tractability of 1D long-range Ising CFTs — which admit perturbative expansions and some exact results but are not exactly solvable — as a benchmark for non-perturbative numerical methods like Monte Carlo, especially for extracting CFT data beyond perturbation theory.

Method: Monte Carlo simulations of the 1D statistical long-range Ising lattice model, with careful finite-size scaling analysis to extract scaling dimensions of leading and subleading operators, as well as three- and four-point correlation functions (hence OPE coefficients).

Result: High-precision numerical estimates of scaling dimensions and three-point functions (and some four-point functions) are obtained at modest computational cost; these match and extend analytic predictions, validating MC as a robust tool for non-perturbative CFT data extraction in this setting.

Conclusion: The 1D long-range Ising model serves as an exceptional 'phenomenal playground' for testing and developing non-perturbative techniques for strongly coupled conformal field theories, bridging analytic CFT insights and numerical lattice methods.

Abstract: The statistical long-range Ising (LRI) model in one dimension provides a family of some of the simplest non-exactly solvable critical points. These critical points are described by long-range conformal field theories (CFTs) that are well understood analytically with perturbative expansions and some exact results. Guided by this, we revisit the Monte Carlo (MC) approach to the corresponding lattice model which has many favorable features compared to other systems studied with MC methods. In particular, with very modest computational cost, we are able to estimate scaling dimensions of leading and subleading operators as well as the associated three-point functions, and even certain four-point functions. These results help highlight the fact that 1d LRI CFTs are a phenomenal playground to more generally test non-perturbative methods and ideas for strongly coupled CFTs.

</details>


### [118] [Light EFT Strings and Asymptotic Accelerated Expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24062)
*Veronica Collazuol,Alessandra Grieco,Irene Valenzuela*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the relationship between particle towers, EFT strings, and EFT membranes near infinite-distance limits in 4D N=1 theories, shows membrane scaling vectors lie in the string lattice, determines relative scalings in F-theory/IIA, and rules out two accelerating cosmological trajectories due to EFT breakdown from light strings—closing a prior loophole; it also connects quantum obstructions to Kähler potential modifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether asymptotic accelerated expansion is viable in flux compactifications of 4D N=1 supergravity, particularly by examining consistency constraints from light extended objects (strings, membranes) and quantum corrections near infinite-distance limits.

Method: Analytical study of scaling behavior of EFT strings and membranes using lattice geometry of charge vectors in moduli space; application to F-theory and Type IIA Calabi-Yau compactifications; consistency checks via EFT validity (Hubble scale vs. string mass); incorporation of recent quantum obstruction results affecting Kähler potential asymptotics.

Result: Scaling vectors of EFT membranes lie in the lattice spanned by EFT string vectors; explicit relative scalings derived in F-theory/IIA; two candidate accelerating trajectories ruled out due to EFT string mass dropping below Hubble scale; loophole from arXiv:2209.11821 closed; quantum obstructions imply modified Kähler potential asymptotics that may resolve tension.

Conclusion: Asymptotic de Sitter-like expansion in these settings is strongly constrained by extended-object physics; EFT breakdown from light strings provides concrete obstructions; quantum gravity effects modifying the Kähler potential offer a promising resolution path.

Abstract: We study the interplay among towers of particles, EFT strings, and EFT membranes near perturbative infinite-distance limits of four-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ theories. We show that the scaling vectors of EFT membranes lie in the lattice generated by those of the EFT strings, as previously found for particle towers, and determine their specific relative scaling in F-theory and Type IIA Calabi-Yau compactifications. We then leverage these objects to assess the viability of asymptotic accelerated expansion in flux compactifications. While our bottom-up analysis does not yield a general obstruction, we rule out two candidate accelerating trajectories in F-theory because an EFT string becomes lighter than the Hubble scale, invalidating the four-dimensional EFT. This closes the loophole found in arXiv:2209.11821. We also study how recent results on quantum obstructions to $\mathcal{N}=1$ infinite-distance limits may provide a concrete resolution by changing the asymptotic form of the Kähler potential.

</details>


### [119] [Angular Momentum Loss at Fourth Post-Minkowskian Order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24072)
*Alessandro Georgoudis,Carlo Heissenberg,Gustav Mogull,Rodolfo Russo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the total angular momentum loss in gravitational scattering of two non-spinning point masses at O(G⁴) using a hybrid amplitudes–worldline approach, separating radiated and static (memory/shear) contributions; results reproduce known low-velocity limits and yield the O(G⁵) conservative deflection angle and first self-force correction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the full O(G⁴) angular momentum loss in binary gravitational scattering—including both radiation-reaction and conservative (static/memory) contributions—required for high-precision waveform modeling and self-force theory, bridging amplitudes and worldline frameworks.

Method: Combines on-shell amplitudes and worldline effective theory: constructs the integrand from two cut gravitational waveforms with massive-line cuts from worldline sources; glues them and evaluates phase-space integrals via reverse unitarity; separates radiated angular momentum from static contribution tied to initial shear and linear memory.

Result: Relativistic expressions for total angular momentum loss and infrared-finite mass dipole loss at O(G⁴); consistency with known slow-motion limits; derived conservative scattering angle at O(G⁵) and first-order self-force correction.

Conclusion: The hybrid amplitudes–worldline method successfully captures all O(G⁴) angular momentum loss components—including memory-driven static terms—and enables derivation of higher-order conservative dynamics (O(G⁵) deflection, first self-force), validating its utility for precision gravitational-wave physics.

Abstract: We calculate the total loss of angular momentum for a gravitational scattering of two non-spinning point-like objects at $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$. Our methodology combines use of both amplitudes and worldline-based methods. We derive the integrand from two copies of the gravitational waveform with cuts on massive lines arising naturally from the worldline. We then glue these two copies and evaluate the resulting phase-space integrals using reverse unitarity, in the amplitudes spirit. We obtain separately the radiated angular momentum and the static contribution that, at this order, is entirely determined by the initial shear and the linear memory. Combining these two terms, we derive relativistic expressions for the angular momentum loss and for the infrared-finite component of the mass dipole loss, which in the low-velocity limit match results available in the literature. We also use this result to derive the conservative deflection angle at $\mathcal{O}(G^5)$ and first self-force order.

</details>


### [120] [On the Positivity of de Sitter Group Averaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24073)
*Donald Marolf,Xuyang Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves positivity of the group-averaging inner product for N-particle states in perturbative quantum gravity on global de Sitter space (dS_d, d>2), under the convergence condition Re ΣΔ_i > d−1; it also analyzes dS₂ as a toy model, finding that certain lower-weight states yield negative norms—raising concerns for the consistency of perturbative dS quantum gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard Fock spaces for linearized quantum gravity on de Sitter space admit no normalizable non-vacuum states satisfying higher-order gauge constraints (vanishing of all dS isometry charges); Higuchi proposed the group-averaging inner product as a remedy, but its positivity—the key requirement for a physical Hilbert space—remained unproven.

Method: The authors perform rigorous analysis of the group-averaging construction over the de Sitter isometry group SO(d,1), computing norms of N-particle states built from global dS_d mode functions with conformal weights Δ_i; they establish positivity under absolute convergence (Re ΣΔ_i > d−1) using representation-theoretic and analytic techniques, and extend analysis to dS₂ for bosonic/fermionic fields, identifying regimes of convergence and norm sign via explicit computation.

Result: Positivity of the group-averaging inner product is proven for all N-particle states (arbitrary spin) in global dS_d (d > 2) satisfying Re ΣΔ_i > d−1; in dS₂, absolute convergence extends to smaller weights, but some such states yield strictly negative norms—demonstrating indefinite signature in parts of the group-averaged state space.

Conclusion: While group averaging successfully yields a positive-definite inner product for sufficiently heavy (large-Re-Δ) states in d > 2, the emergence of negative norms for lighter states in dS₂—and potential analogues in higher d—challenges the viability of group averaging as a complete quantization scheme for perturbative quantum gravity on de Sitter space, suggesting deeper issues with infrared/zero-mode control or the semiclassical dS background itself.

Abstract: Perturbative quantum gravity about a global de Sitter (dS$_d$) background involves constraints that restrict the state of the linearized theory but which appear only at higher orders in perturbation theory. In particular, for familiar gravitational theories with $d>2$ these constraints require all charges defined by continuous de Sitter isometries to vanish in the linearized state. While standard Fock spaces have the property that the only normalizable state satisfying these constraints is the vacuum, Higuchi suggested that this lack of allowed states should be remedied by instead using the so-called group-averaging inner product. This inner product satisfies important uniqueness conditions, but its positivity has to date remained an open question. For $d > 2$, we establish this positivity here for all such states with $N$ particles (including particles of arbitrary spin) in standard global dS${}_d$ modes where the particle-weights $\Delta_i$ satisfy the condition ${\rm Re} \, \sum_{i=1}^N \Delta_i > d-1$, which is the criterion for group averaging to converge absolutely for arbitrary finite linear combinations of such states. We also treat the case of bosonic and fermionic (integer and half-integer spin) particles in dS$_2$ as a toy model for the more general case. However, we can identify certain classes of states for which group averaging continues to converge absolutely for smaller weights. Interestingly, in some cases these smaller-weight states turn out to have {\it negative} group-averaging norms. We thus also discuss the implications of this observation for perturbative de Sitter quantum gravity.

</details>


### [121] [Wrapping M5-branes on a non-spin manifold: Spin$^h$ structures and AdS$_7$ black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24084)
*Okan Günel,Cameron Gibson,C.N. Pope*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a supersymmetric AdS₂×M₅ solution in 7D maximal gauged supergravity with M₅ = Wu manifold SU(3)/SO(3), which lacks spin and spinᶜ structures but admits a spinʰ structure—enabling supersymmetry via compensating SO(3) Yang–Mills holonomy. It lifts to an AdS₂×M₉ solution in D=11 supergravity, where M₉ is the unit tangent bundle of the Wu manifold; analyzes its geometry, topology, and supersymmetry; extends it to a black hole with holographic interpretation; revisits and exactly solves flow equations for AdS₂×H³×H²; compares with AdS₂×S⁵ lift; and constructs a non-compact dual using SL(3,ℝ)/SO(3).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and understand supersymmetric AdS₂ solutions in lower-dimensional gauged supergravity whose internal spaces lack conventional spin structures—challenging standard supersymmetry conditions—and to explore their consistent uplifts to D=11, geometric structures (e.g., spinʰ), holographic implications, and dual non-compact geometries.

Method: Explicit construction of a supersymmetric AdS₂×SU(3)/SO(3) solution in 7D maximal gauged supergravity; analysis of spinʰ structure on the Wu manifold; Kaluza–Klein uplift to D=11 yielding AdS₂×M₉ with M₉ = unit tangent bundle of SU(3)/SO(3); topological and geometric characterization of M₉; supersymmetry analysis in both 7D and 11D; black hole extension via consistent truncation; exact integration of holographic RG flow equations for related AdS₂×H³×H² solution; comparative analysis with AdS₂×S⁵ uplift; construction of non-compact dual using SL(3,ℝ)/SO(3).

Result: A new supersymmetric AdS₂×SU(3)/SO(3) solution in 7D gauged supergravity, enabled by spinʰ structure; its consistent D=11 uplift to AdS₂×M₉ with M₉ topologically nontrivial (π₁(M₉) ≅ ℤ₂, non-spin); full supersymmetry preserved in 11D; explicit black hole generalization; exact solution of RG flow for AdS₂×H³×H²; detailed comparison showing S⁵ case admits spin structure while Wu manifold does not—highlighting role of spinʰ; non-compact dual AdS₂×SL(3,ℝ)/SO(3) constructed.

Conclusion: Supersymmetry in AdS₂ compactifications can persist even when internal manifolds lack spin or spinᶜ structures, provided they admit spinʰ structures—where Yang–Mills holonomy compensates topological obstructions to spinors. This broadens the landscape of admissible internal geometries for holographic duals of 1D quantum systems, with implications for black hole microstates, RG flows, and consistency of string/M-theory compactifications on non-spin manifolds.

Abstract: We construct a solution of seven-dimensional maximal gauged supergravity of the form AdS$_2\times M_5$, where $M_5$ is the five-dimensional Wu manifold $SU(3)/SO(3)$. The solution is supersymmetric, despite the fact that the Wu manifold admits neither a spin structure nor a spin$^c$ structure. Rather, it admits a spin$^h$ structure, wherein the ill-definedness of ordinary spinors due to a minus sign in their holonomy is compensated by a further minus sign arising through the spinors carrying $SO(3)$ Yang-Mills charge, in a half-integer isospin representation of the $SU(2)$ double cover of the $SO(3)$. We show how the configuration can be lifted to a solution of $D=11$ supergravity of the form AdS$_2\times M_9$, where $M_9$ is the unit tangent bundle of the Wu manifold. We discuss the geometry and topology of $M_9$, and study the supersymmetry from the eleven-dimensional standpoint. We extend the seven-dimensional solution to a black hole, and discuss some aspects of its holographic interpretation and the UV to IR flow. We revisit a previously-known AdS$_2\times H^3\times H^2$ solution, and its UV to IR flow, showing that the flow equations in fact admit of an exact solution. Additionally, we give a detailed discussion of the lift of the AdS$_2\times S^5$ solution to $D=11$, exhibiting similarities and differences between the properties of its internal space $M_9$ (the unit tangent bundle of $S^5$) and the Wu manifold example of our main discussion. We also construct the non-compact dual of the Wu manifold solution, where $M_5$ is taken to be $SL(3,\mathbb R)/SO(3)$.

</details>


### [122] [Topological deformations of local gauge theories: a presymplectic BV-AKSZ approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24111)
*Ivan Dneprov,Alexey Donchenko,Maxim Grigoriev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'topological deformations'—a systematic method within the presymplectic BV-AKSZ framework—to relate physically equivalent field theories with different gauge structures (e.g., scalar ↔ 2-form duality, unimodular gravity) by gauging symmetries of generic degree, thereby modifying the presymplectic gauge PDE without adding local degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize known dualities (e.g., scalar–2-form, unimodular gravity) under a common geometric and cohomological principle, clarifying how differing gauge structures can describe the same local physics while exhibiting distinct global and interaction properties.

Method: Uses the presymplectic BV-AKSZ formalism to implement 'topological deformations': gauging degree-k symmetries (k = 0,1) of a theory, coupling it to a BF-type topological sector; degree-0 gauging identifies globally symmetric solutions (recovering dualities), degree-1 gauging promotes parameters (e.g., cosmological constant) to global degrees of freedom.

Result: A unified construction that reproduces established dualities (scalar–2-form, principal chiral model–Freedman–Townsend) and generates new ones (e.g., unimodular gravity); shows that gauge-inequivalent solution spaces differ only by finite-dimensional global sectors; establishes precise correspondence between symmetry degree, BF coupling, and emergence of global vs. gauged degrees of freedom.

Conclusion: Topological deformations provide a rigorous, cohomological classification of gauge-equivalent yet structurally distinct field theories; they explain how global features (spacetime topology, conserved charges, cosmological constant) arise from gauging symmetries of definite degree, bridging formal duality, constrained systems, and background independence.

Abstract: The same local degrees of freedom can be described by systems with different gauge structure and field content. Standard examples are the duality between the scalar field and the 2-form gauge field in four dimensions, and unimodular gravity, where the cosmological constant becomes a global degree of freedom. In a precise sense, the spaces of gauge-inequivalent formal solutions of such systems differ by a finite-dimensional sector. Moreover, because their gauge sectors are different, their consistent interactions and their sensitivity to the global structure of spacetime may differ substantially. We show that such systems are naturally related by what we call topological deformations: gauging a symmetry of generic degree couples the system to a BF-type sector without introducing new local degrees of freedom. The construction is carried out systematically within the presymplectic BV-AKSZ approach, where it amounts to a modification of the underlying presymplectic gauge PDE. Gauging degree-0 symmetries identifies solutions related by global symmetries, which reproduces the scalar--2-form and principal chiral model--Freedman--Townsend dualities. Gauging degree-1 symmetries, i.e. deformations, gives rise to new global degrees of freedom, such as the cosmological constant in unimodular gravity.

</details>


### [123] [Gravitational Cheshire effect from Yang-Mills instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24127)
*Borja Diez*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies dimensional reduction of 4D Yang-Mills instantons on Taub-NUT and Eguchi-Hanson backgrounds to 3D, revealing novel gravitational screening mechanisms: Taub-NUT yields fully stealth (gravitationally invisible) composite matter on flat 3D space, while Eguchi-Hanson yields partially stealth configurations with active Abelian gravity backreaction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover how gravitational invisibility (stealth) of 4D instantons manifests in lower dimensions, and to explore connections between 4D (anti-)self-duality and 3D BPS-like structures via Kaluza–Klein reduction.

Method: Kaluza–Klein dimensional reduction of Euclidean (anti-)self-dual Yang-Mills instantons along a U(1) isometry direction; explicit analysis on Taub-NUT and Eguchi-Hanson gravitational instanton backgrounds; decomposition of gauge connection into 3D YM field + adjoint Higgs, and metric into dilaton + Abelian gauge field; computation of gravitational backreaction from both Abelian and non-Abelian sectors.

Result: On Taub-NUT: full cancellation of gravitational backreaction in both Abelian and non-Abelian sectors → composite stealth configuration on flat ℝ³. On Eguchi-Hanson: non-Abelian sector backreaction cancels, but Abelian sector remains gravitationally active → deformed 3D geometry. Both cases establish a direct link between 4D self-duality and 3D BPS-type constraints.

Conclusion: Dimensional reduction of 4D self-dual gauge configurations can yield either fully or partially stealth matter in 3D, depending on the underlying gravitational instanton geometry—revealing a geometric origin for BPS-like screening and enriching the landscape of gravitationally benign field configurations in lower dimensions.

Abstract: We show that the dimensional reduction of four-dimensional Yang-Mills instantons naturally gives rise to nontrivial matter configurations in three dimensions exhibiting an unusual mechanism of gravitational screening. Starting from Euclidean Yang-Mills theory, we reduce (anti-)self-dual gauge configurations along a compact direction generated by a $U(1)$ isometry of the instanton background. Under this reduction, the gauge connection decomposes into a three-dimensional Yang-Mills field and an adjoint Higgs field, while the metric gives rise, as usual, to a dilaton and an Abelian gauge connection. For instantons on the Taub-NUT background, the reduced fields organize themselves into two mutually screening pairs: the backreaction of the Abelian sector cancels internally, and the same occurs independently in the non-Abelian sector. As a result, the full matter content supports a composite stealth configuration on flat three-dimensional space. By contrast, the dimensional reduction of instantons on the Eguchi-Hanson background yields a non-Abelian matter sector whose gravitational backreaction cancels, while the Abelian sector remains gravitationally active and therefore deforms the three-dimensional background geometry. These examples reveal a direct connection between four-dimensional (anti-)self-duality and three-dimensional BPS-type relations, and illustrate how instantonic sectors that are gravitationally invisible in four dimensions can descend into either fully composite stealth configurations or partially stealth matter configurations after dimensional reduction.

</details>


### [124] [Landscape of Higgs vacua in 5d $\mathcal{N}=1$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24176)
*Amihay Hanany,Elias Van den Driessche*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the Higgs branch of strongly coupled 5d N=1 gauge theories, analyzing chiral ring structure, CS-level deformations (for SU(N_c)_k), chiral constraints for SO(N_c), and identifies the Higgs branch as a classical integrable system via action-variable expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the Higgs branch of 5d N=1 SCFTs—particularly its geometry and algebraic structure—across different gauge groups and coupling regimes, leveraging BPS operator analysis and chiral ring techniques.

Method: Algebraic analysis of 1/2-BPS operators and their relations in the chiral ring; mapping moduli space across phases; derivation of deformed relations induced by Chern-Simons level k; construction of chiral ring constraints at infinite coupling for SO(N_c); identification of integrability via action-variable expansion of the chiral ring.

Result: Explicit deformation of chiral ring relations by k in SU(N_c)_k theories; symmetry enhancement patterns tied to k; complete chiral ring constraints for SO(N_c) at infinite coupling; realization of the Higgs branch as a classical integrable system through action variables.

Conclusion: The Higgs branch of 5d N=1 theories admits a precise algebraic and geometric description via the chiral ring, with Chern-Simons level acting as a key deformation parameter, and exhibits an unexpected integrable structure encoded in action variables.

Abstract: Strongly coupled 5d $\mathcal{N}=1$ gauge theories have a Higgs branch of their moduli space of vacua, identified by 1/2-BPS operators and their algebraic relations. For $SU(N_c)_k$ theories, we map the moduli space across different phases and we establish how the Chern-Simons level $k$ deforms the chiral ring relations among mesons, baryons, and instantons, while determining specific patterns of symmetry enhancement. For $SO(N_c)$ theories, we construct the corresponding chiral ring constraints at infinite coupling. Finally, we identify the Higgs branch as a classical integrable system, through the expansion of the chiral ring in terms of action variables.

</details>


### [125] [Covariant One-Loop Corrections from a Non-Covariant Expansion: The Scalar Field Case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24227)
*Lucas Ducastelo de C. C. Lima,Riccardo Fecchio*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop correction to the non-minimal scalar–gravity coupling induced by a quartic self-interaction within linearized gravity, obtains the effective action via covariance restoration, compares with heat kernel results, analyzes RG running and decoupling, and confirms consistency of the linearized approach with fully covariant methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute quantum corrections to the non-minimal coupling ξφ²R in scalar-gravity theory using a simpler, non-covariant linearized gravity framework—while ensuring consistency with fully covariant results—and to examine renormalization-group behavior and decoupling.

Method: Explicit Feynman diagram computation in linearized gravity (with graviton propagator and scalar–graviton vertices from quartic interaction); dimensional regularization; reconstruction of the covariant effective action from the linearized result via covariance requirements; momentum subtraction scheme for renormalization; derivation of effective potential and its covariant completion.

Result: Full one-loop correction (divergent and finite parts) to the non-minimal coupling is obtained; the resulting effective action matches heat kernel predictions; UV/IR limits and RG flow of ξ are derived; decoupling theorem is verified in momentum subtraction scheme; linearized approach successfully reproduces covariant effective potential.

Conclusion: The linearized gravity framework is a reliable and versatile tool for computing quantum gravitational corrections to scalar field couplings, yielding results consistent with fully covariant methods while offering computational simplicity and physical transparency.

Abstract: We compute the one-loop correction to the non-minimal coupling of a scalar field theory to gravity, induced by a quartic self-interaction. We adopt the non-covariant framework of linearized gravity and explicitly compute the corresponding Feynman diagram, including both the divergent and the finite parts. The corresponding contribution to the one-loop effective action is obtained by the requirement of covariance and compared with the known results obtained by the heat kernel technique. We study the running of the non-minimal coupling, its UV and IR limit and verify the validity of the decoupling theorem in the momentum subtraction scheme of renormalization. The linearized gravity approach is also used for deriving the effective potential for the scalar field and its covariant version is stated. The agreement with results derived using strictly covariant methods is verified and highlights the versatility of the linearized gravity approach.

</details>


### [126] [Bosonization for the Schwinger effect at small fermion mass: analytic formula for the 1+1D current in an arbitrary time-dependent, inhomogeneous electric field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24272)
*Samuel E. Gralla,Morifumi Mizuno*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives an analytic formula for Schwinger pair-production current in 1+1D time-dependent, spatially inhomogeneous electric fields, up to order m², using a novel hybrid bosonization method; validates duality via agreement with purely fermionic adiabatic regularization in homogeneous case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the vacuum expectation value of the current from Schwinger pair production in generic (time-dependent and spatially inhomogeneous) electric fields in 1+1D—beyond standard homogeneous or static approximations—and to test boson-fermion duality nontrivially via regularization-free computation.

Method: Develops a 'hybrid method' combining fermionic and bosonic representations via bosonization: fermionic description defines the in-vacuum asymptotically, while bosonic representation enables finite, regularization-free evaluation of the current (linear in boson field); computes perturbatively in fermion mass m up to O(m²), including m⁰, m² log m, and m² terms; cross-checks with pure fermionic adiabatic regularization in spatially homogeneous limit.
