Result: Obtains explicit analytic expression for ⟨J^μ⟩ due to Schwinger pair production, revealing contributions at O(m⁰), O(m² log m), and O(m²), with vanishing O(m) term; demonstrates full agreement between hybrid bosonization and adiabatic-regularized fermionic computation in homogeneous limit.

Conclusion: The hybrid bosonization method provides a robust, regularization-free framework for computing dynamical vacuum currents in generic backgrounds; the precise agreement between dual descriptions constitutes a strong verification of boson-fermion duality beyond perturbative equivalence, especially in time-dependent, interacting vacuum settings.

Abstract: We derive an analytic formula for the expectation value of the current due to Schwinger pair-production in an arbitrary time-dependent, spatially inhomogeneous external electric field in 1+1 dimensions, as a perturbative expansion in the fermion mass $m$. We work to order $m^2$, finding contributions at order $m^0$, $m^2 \log m$ and $m^2$; there is no contribution at $O(m)$. The calculation is enabled by a new ``hybrid method'' using bosonization, where part of the calculation is performed on the fermion side and part is performed on the boson side. The fermion side allows us to define the vacuum using the free theory in the infinite past, while the boson side allows us to compute the current without regularization, since the current operator is linear in the boson field. In the special case of a spatially homogeneous background field, we also perform the calculation purely on the fermion side using adiabatic regularization. The detailed agreement of the results provides a highly non-trivial check of the duality.

</details>


### [127] [Gravity as Large Scale Structure: Families of CFTs and the Higher-Spin Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24496)
*Eric Perlmutter*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper identifies a universal parametric bound on the higher-spin gap in large-N CFTs—Δ_gap ≲ (δc)^(1/d)—and explains it via 'families of CFTs', inspired by L-function families; holographically, this bound constrains AdS vacua by forbidding Planckian scale separation, limiting extra dimensions, and reproducing string/M-theory scalings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain an observed universal scaling of the single-trace higher-spin gap across large-N CFTs in d ≥ 2, and to provide a rigorous conceptual framework—'families of CFTs'—that unifies diverse AdS/CFT phenomena and resolves puzzles like bounded gaps in vector models.

Method: Heuristic reasoning using generalized branes interpolating between conformal points in theory space; formal development of a working definition of CFT families, analogously to families of L-functions; holographic interpretation in terms of AdS_{d+1} vacua and consistency checks against known string/M-theory and vector model results.

Result: Derivation of the family-level gap bound Δ_gap ≲ (δc)^(1/d); demonstration that it implies non-Planckian scale separation in AdS, bounds on compactification geometry (size and number of extra dimensions), and correctly reproduces scaling patterns in string/M-theory and bounded gaps in all known large-N vector models.

Conclusion: The higher-spin gap bound is not accidental but reflects a deep structural feature—CFT families—that is essential for consistent holography; this framework provides a unifying lens for AdS/CFT, quantum gravity constraints, and classification of large-N theories.

Abstract: We observe an apparently universal behavior in large $N$ conformal field theory (CFT) in $d\geq 2$ dimensions: the single-trace, higher-spin gap obeys the parametric bound $\Delta_{\rm gap} \lesssim (\delta c)^{1/d}$, where $\delta c$ is the central charge increment between successive theories. The origin of this bound is explained heuristically, using a generalized notion of branes that interpolate between conformal points in theory space. Seeking a more precise framework for this picture leads to the central concept of families of CFTs, which tacitly underlies various features and puzzles of AdS/CFT. We develop a working definition of families, with inspiration from families of $L$-functions in number theory, and formulate a family-level version of the gap bound. Interpreted holographically for families of AdS$_{d+1}$ vacua, this bound, when applicable, rules out Planckian scale separation, constrains the size and number of extra dimensions, and explains simple scaling patterns in string/M-theory. It also accounts for why all known large $N$ vector models have a bounded gap.

</details>


### [128] [Strong generation of the chiral algebra of $\mathcal N=4$~$SU(N)$ super Yang--Mills theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24594)
*Yifan He*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves the strong-generation conjecture for the Schur sector of 4D N=4 SYM, showing that a finite set of short multiplets—specifically field components of degrees 2 through N in the adjoint scalar’s invariant polynomials—strongly generate the full chiral algebra via quantum BRST cohomology; the proof combines cyclic trace cohomology, quantum lifting, and a novel quartic–odd transport identity, and yields exact normal-ordering corrections and BRST primitives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the strong-generation conjecture of Beem et al. for the Schur sector of 4D N=4 super Yang–Mills, resolving how a finite set of protected multiplets can generate an infinite-dimensional chiral algebra despite the apparent mismatch between infinitely many Schur operators and finitely many Casimir invariants.

Method: Uses cyclic trace cohomology to classify gauge-invariant operators, quantum lifting to promote classical invariants to quantum operators, and introduces an arbitrary-degree quartic–odd transport identity to relate BRST cohomology classes across weights; derives explicit normal-ordering corrections and BRST primitives, and employs exact field relations plus a finite-rank Newton obstruction to exclude extraneous lowest-weight states.

Result: For SU(N) with N ≥ 2, the small N=4 short multiplets corresponding to invariant polynomials of degrees 2,…,N strongly generate the chiral algebra in all conformal weights and BRST cohomological degrees; every Schur operator is a normal-ordered polynomial in these generators and their derivatives—even when its Lagrangian representative contains fermions or arbitrarily high derivatives.

Conclusion: The Schur sector admits a finite strong generating set, confirming the Beem et al. conjecture for SU(N); this provides a uniform algebraic foundation for meromorphic OPEs, index multiplicity bounds, and the finite-rank reorganization of protected trace operators—demonstrating how supersymmetry and cohomology constrain quantum spectra beyond naive counting.

Abstract: The Schur sector of four-dimensional $\mathcal N=4$ super Yang--Mills theory contains infinitely many protected operators, while the invariant polynomials of a single adjoint scalar are organized by finitely many Casimir degrees. I prove that the small $\mathcal N=4$ short multiplets built from these invariants strongly generate the associated chiral algebra, thereby establishing the $SU(N)$ case of the strong-generation conjecture of Beem et al. For every $N\ge2$, all field components associated with degrees $2,\ldots,N$ suffice, in every conformal weight and every degree of the standard relative quantum BRST cohomology. The proof brings together cyclic trace cohomology, quantum lifting, and an arbitrary-degree quartic--odd transport identity. Exact field relations and a finite-rank Newton obstruction exclude every additional lowest space, including non-scalar flavor representations; the required normal-ordering corrections and BRST primitives are derived explicitly. The resulting completeness theorem explains how a finite family of supersymmetric multiplets organizes an unbounded quantum spectrum. Every Schur class is a normal polynomial in their components and derivatives, even when its gauge-theory representative involves fermions and arbitrarily many derivatives. This supplies a uniform set of local inputs for meromorphic operator products, bounds protected state multiplicities before cancellations in an index, and gives an algebraic account of the finite-rank reorganization of protected trace operators.

</details>


### [129] [Holomorphic extremal surfaces for pseudoentropy in dS/CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24698)
*Giorgos Anastasiou,Ignacio J. Araya,Avijit Das,Javier Moreno*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies extremization of codimension-two hypersurfaces in de Sitter space using holomorphic coordinates and real anchoring at future infinity, deriving the condition K^I = 0; it computes holographic pseudoentropy for harmonic deformations of spherical regions, confirming universal expansion up to second order, contour-independence within homology classes, and distinct behavior across parity of boundary dimension; it further verifies the first law and contour degeneracy for conical defects in dS₃.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Hartle–Hawking construction to complex bulk geometries in de Sitter space and understand the structure and universality of holographic pseudoentropy—particularly its dependence on analytic continuation, homology classes, and global isometries—in settings beyond static or spherical symmetry.

Method: Formulates extremization of codimension-two surfaces in dS using holomorphic coordinates with real asymptotic anchoring; solves K^I = 0 for complex extremal surfaces; computes pseudoentropy via analytic continuation of entanglement entropy for harmonic deformations of spherical boundary regions; classifies integration contours by discrete global-coordinate isometry (winding number); analyzes conical defects in dS₃ using Schwarzschild–dS mass parameterization.

Result: Pseudoentropy expansion is universal up to second order in deformation; contour-independent within each homology class; inequivalent classes yield sign-flipped universal terms in odd d and real finite shifts in even d; first law holds for dS₃ conical defects; contour degeneracy persists per homology class.

Conclusion: The complex extremal surface framework generalizes Hartle–Hawking geometry in dS and reveals topological (homology-class-dependent) and dimensional-parity-sensitive features of pseudoentropy, supporting its role as a robust analytic probe of quantum de Sitter physics.

Abstract: We formulate the extremization problem for codimension-two hypersurfaces in de Sitter space in holomorphic coordinates, with real anchoring conditions at future infinity. The resulting equation ($K^I=0$) describes complex bulk realizations extending the usual piecewise Hartle--Hawking construction. We compute holographic pseudoentropy for harmonic deformations of spherical regions of arbitrary size and dimension, confirming its universal expansion through second order in the deformation parameter. The result is independent of the integration contour within each homology class on the complexified extremal surface. Inequivalent classes, labeled by an integer associated with a discrete global-coordinate isometry, differ by a sign of the universal term in odd boundary dimensions and by real finite shifts in even dimensions. For conical defects in dS$_3$ parametrized by a Schwarzschild-dS mass, we also verify the first law of pseudoentropy and the persistence of contour degeneracy within each homology class.

</details>


### [130] [Bootstrapping Fermionic Veneziano Amplitudes from Regge Zeros](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24754)
*Pongwit Srisangyingcharoen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Extends Regge-zero bootstrap to fermionic tree-level amplitudes; derives linear Regge trajectories for fermions (J_F = n+1/2) and bosons (J_B = m+1); duality and degeneracy fix the amplitude to the open superstring amplitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Regge-zero bootstrap—previously applied to purely bosonic amplitudes like Veneziano’s—to scattering involving external fermions, thereby probing consistency of dual models with spacetime supersymmetry and fermionic Regge trajectories.

Method: Imposes Regge-zero constraints on residues of the colour-ordered four-point scalar–fermion amplitude; factors out little-group spinor structure; bootstraps reduced amplitude under ultrasoft Regge behaviour and minimal zero assumptions; enforces duality between fermionic-s-channel and bosonic-t-channel pole expansions; imposes spectral degeneracy to fix Regge slope equality.

Result: Both Regge trajectories are proven linear; fermionic spins scale as J_F = n + 1/2, bosonic as J_B = m + 1; duality uniquely fixes the reduced amplitude to a Beta function; spectral degeneracy forces equal slopes, yielding the open superstring amplitude.

Conclusion: The Regge-zero bootstrap, extended to fermions, tightly constrains consistent dual amplitudes and selects the open superstring amplitude as the unique solution satisfying duality, linearity, and fermion–boson degeneracy.

Abstract: We extend the Regge-zero bootstrap of the Veneziano amplitude to tree-level scattering with external fermions. We consider the colour-ordered four-point amplitude of a massless scalar and a massless spin-$\frac12$ fermion, in which fermionic resonances appear in the $s$-channel and bosonic resonances in the $t$-channel. Factoring out a spinor structure that carries the little-group weight of the amplitude, we bootstrap the resulting reduced amplitude, whose residues in each channel vanish at Regge zeros controlled by the trajectory of the opposite channel. Assuming ultrasoft Regge behaviour and that the residues have no zeros other than Regge zeros, we show that both Regge trajectories are linear and that the maximal spins grow with the level as $J_F=n+\frac12$ and $J_B=m+1$. Duality between the fermionic- and bosonic-pole expansions then fixes the reduced amplitude to a Beta function, up to its normalisation and the ratio of the two Regge slopes. Requiring the fermionic and bosonic spectra to be degenerate equates the slopes, and the resulting amplitude coincides with the open superstring amplitude.

</details>


### [131] [Complex spinodals and supercritical crossovers in Kerr-Newman-AdS$_4$ black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24978)
*Bum-Hoon Lee,Hocheol Lee,Somyadip Thakur*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes supercritical thermodynamic crossovers in 4D Kerr-Newman-AdS black holes at fixed angular momentum and charge, using exact enthalpy and Landau theory to establish universal critical behavior—including a Widom line, Landau cusp scaling, and heat capacity ridge—across the charge–rotation parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand universal thermodynamic crossover behavior near criticality in rotating, charged, asymptotically anti-de Sitter black holes—a gravitational analog of fluid critical phenomena—beyond mean-field approximations and within exact black hole thermodynamics.

Method: Exact analytical computation of enthalpy; quartic Landau free-energy reduction; scaling analysis near the physical critical point; construction of Widom-type lines via spinodal projection; derivation of cusp exponents and quadratic separation between fold and heat capacity ridge; validation via exact endpoint solutions and numerical continuation.

Result: Proof of unique physical critical point and positive quartic Landau coefficient across the charge–rotation family; exact determination of coexistence entropies and latent heat below criticality; identification of a Widom-type crossover surface formed by projected spinodals; Landau cusp with universal square-root (imaginary entropy) and three-halves (imaginary temperature) scaling; leading quadratic temperature separation between fold and heat capacity ridge, with ridge at higher temperature near criticality.

Conclusion: The supercritical crossovers in 4D Kerr-Newman-AdS black holes exhibit universal Landau-critical structure—cusp exponents are family-invariant, while crossover temperature depends on the thermodynamic criterion—establishing black hole thermodynamics as a robust framework for studying generalized critical phenomena.

Abstract: We study supercritical crossovers in four-dimensional Kerr-Newman-AdS black holes at fixed angular momentum and charge. From the exact enthalpy, we prove uniqueness of the physical critical point and positivity of the quartic Landau coefficient throughout the charge-rotation family, treating the uncharged Kerr-AdS endpoint in separate scaling variables. An exact quartic reduction determines the coexistence entropies and latent heat throughout the subcritical regime. Above criticality, the two physical spinodals continue as a complex conjugate pair. Their real temperature projection defines a Widom-type line, and varying the charge-rotation ratio assembles these lines into a crossover surface. The Landau cusp fixes the imaginary entropy and temperature to scale with the square root and three-halves powers of the pressure displacement from criticality. The projected fold and the local heat capacity ridge share a critical tangent. We derive their leading quadratic temperature separation and prove that the ridge lies at a higher temperature near criticality. Exact endpoint solutions and numerical continuation check these predictions. The cusp exponents are common to the family, while the crossover temperature depends on the thermodynamic criterion used to define it.

</details>


### [132] [The Anyon Atlas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25439)
*Jonah Berean-Dutcher,Clay Cordova,Julio Parra-Martinez,Richard Tuddenham*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Classifies modular and supermodular tensor categories (i.e., topological quantum field theories) in 2+1D with up to 22 (bosonic) or 26 (fermionic) anyons, using congruence representations of SL(2,Z) and numerical optimization; discovers new TQFTs not realizable via Chern–Simons theory or anyon condensation/gauging; verifies unitary realization for 11- and 12-anyon examples by solving pentagon/hexagon equations; provides public data resource.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify and construct all possible topological quantum field theories in 2+1 dimensions with bounded number of anyon types, especially those not captured by conventional Chern–Simons or symmetry-gauging constructions.

Method: Uses congruence representations of SL(2,Z) to generate modular and supermodular data; employs a scalable numerical optimization algorithm to search over high-rank representations; verifies consistency via pentagon and hexagon equations; analyzes relations including anyon condensation and Chern–Simons realizations.

Result: Complete classification of modular/supermodular data up to rank 22 (bosonic) and 26 (fermionic); discovery of novel modular data not derivable from Chern–Simons theories via condensation or gauging; explicit unitary TQFT realizations for rank-11 and rank-12 cases.

Conclusion: There exist intrinsically non-gauge, non-Chern–Simons topological quantum field theories in 2+1D—expanding the landscape of possible topological orders beyond current physical paradigms.

Abstract: We classify possible topological quantum field theories in 2+1 dimensions with up to 22 anyons in the bosonic case and up to 26 in the fermionic case, through the modular data of modular and supermodular tensor categories. We construct such data via congruence representations of $\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{Z})$ using a numerical optimization algorithm that scales to high rank. We chart the relations between the resulting theories, including anyon condensations and Chern-Simons realizations. We find examples of modular data that do not appear to arise from any Chern-Simons theory by any sequence of condensations of anyons or gaugings of ordinary global symmetries. These examples give qualitatively new topological quantum field theories, which go beyond any notion of gauge theory. For examples in this class with 11 and 12 anyon types, we solve the pentagon and hexagon equations explicitly, showing that these modular data are realized by full unitary topological quantum field theories. We also introduce this https URL, an online resource for exploring the data.

</details>


### [133] [Remarks on the Parameter Dependence of QCD$_{2A}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25567)
*Uwe Trittmann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper numerically shows that no single operator dominates the weak N_c dependence in 2D adjoint QCD; all identified operators are essential for preserving exact multi-particle degeneracies and faithful Hamiltonian representation. It provides a complete classification of single- vs. multi-trace interactions consistent with Kutasov-Schwimmer universality, enables targeted deformations to achieve N_c-invariant masses, yet argues residual N_c dependence is unavoidable. In the large constituent mass regime (m²/(g²_YM N_c) ≫ 1), eLCQ calculations confirm eigenstate purification in parton number at large N_c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin and structure of the weak N_c dependence in two-dimensional adjoint QCD, test the completeness of the operator basis previously identified as relevant, and explore whether N_c independence can be achieved via controlled deformations or emerges naturally in asymptotic regimes.

Method: Numerical Hamiltonian diagonalization in the extended Light-Cone Quantization (eLCQ) framework; systematic classification of trace-number–preserving and -changing interactions between single- and multi-trace states; analysis of spectral degeneracies and scaling behavior across varying N_c and constituent mass m.

Result: All previously identified N_c-dependent operators are necessary—none is dominant; exact degeneracies (e.g., multi-particle state masses) rely on their collective contribution; full list of inter-trace interactions is constructed, respecting Kutasov-Schwimmer block structure; targeted deformations can eliminate N_c dependence for specific masses, but generic N_c scaling persists; in the large-m regime, eigenstates become increasingly pure in parton number.

Conclusion: The weak N_c dependence in 2D adjoint QCD is an emergent, cooperative phenomenon—not attributable to a single operator—and cannot be fully removed without breaking essential symmetries or structure; the theory intrinsically retains a minimal, unavoidable N_c dependence, while asymptotic regimes (e.g., large constituent mass) exhibit expected simplifications like parton-number purity.

Abstract: Recently it was shown that there are only a few operators that contribute to the (very weak) $N_c$ dependence of the spectrum of two-dimensional adjoint QCD. We provide numerical evidence that none of these operators is dominant. In other words, all operators are necessary to guarantee the faithful representation of the Hamiltonian, resulting in, for instance, exact degeneracies of certain multi-particle state masses. We provide a complete list of interactions between single- and multi-trace states, which differ by trace-number block, as expected from the Kutasov-Schwimmer universality. The completed list allows for a targeted deformation of the theory to render some masses $N_c$-invariant. Nonetheless, we argue that a small amount of $N_c$ dependence of the theory is unavoidable. We also investigate the large constituent mass regime $m^2/g^2_{\rm YM}N_c\gg 1$ of the theory at large $N_c$ in the eLCQ approach. We find, among other things, that as the constituent mass grows, eigenstates get purer in parton number, in line with expectations.

</details>


### [134] [Non-Riemannian Holography: Long Strings and Soft Hair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25669)
*Shaun D. Hampton,Hyun-Cheol Kim,Jae-Hyuk Oh,Jeong-Hyuck Park*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs an O(D,D)-covariant holographic dictionary for two bulk geometries dual to Gomis-Ooguri boundary data: Riemannian AdS₃ with NS–NS flux and a non-Riemannian string Newton–Cartan geometry; it identifies a covariant Codazzi identity as the origin of boundary conservation, distinguishes soft hair in the non-Riemannian branch, and introduces 'supersupersymmetry' via arbitrary chiral Killing-spinor profiles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a unified, O(D,D)-covariant holographic framework accommodating both Riemannian and non-Riemannian bulk completions of the same boundary data (Gomis–Ooguri), resolving how conservation laws and soft degrees of freedom emerge covariantly in double field theory contexts.

Method: Uses O(D,D)-covariant double field theory formalism to construct a holographic dictionary; derives boundary conservation from an O(D,D)-covariant Codazzi identity; computes generalized-metric one-point functions and analyzes normalizable vs. non-normalizable modes; examines supersymmetry enhancement in the non-Riemannian branch via chiral Killing-spinor equations.

Result: Both bulk branches yield identical diagonal generalized-metric one-point functions; only the non-Riemannian branch supports independent, normalizable soft hair that couples to long strings but does not contribute to surface charges or renormalized on-shell action; supersymmetric non-Riemannian saddles admit arbitrary chiral Killing-spinor profiles — dubbed 'supersupersymmetry'.

Conclusion: The O(D,D)-covariant holographic dictionary unifies Riemannian and non-Riemannian bulk descriptions, revealing that soft hair and enhanced supersymmetry are intrinsically tied to the non-Riemannian branch, highlighting its distinct role in encoding quantum gravitational edge modes beyond standard AdS/CFT.

Abstract: We propose an $\mathbf{O}(D,D)$-covariant holographic dictionary for two bulk completions of the Gomis--Ooguri boundary data: Riemannian $AdS_3$ supported by pure NS--NS flux and an everywhere non-Riemannian string Newton--Cartan geometry. An $\mathbf{O}(D,D)$-covariant Codazzi identity yields the boundary conservation law. Both branches share diagonal generalized-metric one-point functions, but only the non-Riemannian branch carries independent normalizable soft hair that couples to long strings without contributing to surface charges or the renormalized on-shell action. Supersymmetric non-Riemannian saddles admit arbitrary chiral Killing-spinor profiles, realizing "supersupersymmetry."

</details>


### [135] [Subsystem quantum Rényi-Bhattacharyya distance in 2D CFTs and its holographic applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25685)
*Yihao Wang,Jiaju Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a systematic OPE-based method to compute subsystem fidelity and Rényi fidelity in 2D CFTs, applying it to quantum Bhattacharyya distances; it shows distinguishability between heavy primary and thermal states is governed by classical gravity (O(c)) for RBD but suppressed (O(1)) for BD—revealing a subtle quantum-gravity signature at ℓ⁸.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically quantify state distinguishability in 2D CFTs—especially between heavy primary and thermal states—and elucidate the gravitational dual interpretation of fidelity-based distances in holography, resolving discrepancies between entanglement and Rényi entropy behavior.

Method: Uses twist operator OPE in short-interval expansion to compute subsystem fidelity and Rényi fidelity; applies conformal symmetry and known OPE data (e.g., stress-tensor and higher-twist contributions); validates on free fermion/boson CFTs; extends to holographic CFTs via large-c expansion and heavy-state limits.

Result: BD between heavy primary and thermal states starts at O(ℓ⁸) and is O(c⁰), while RBD is generically O(c); when two states differ in classical (O(c)) data, distances are O(c); if classical data match, distances are O(1/c) order-by-order in short interval—indicating quantum gravity corrections.

Conclusion: Subsystem Bhattacharyya distances probe distinct levels of semiclassical vs. quantum gravitational distinguishability: RBD detects classical geometry differences, whereas BD reveals subleading, non-perturbative quantum effects; this clarifies the information-theoretic hierarchy of state discrimination in AdS/CFT.

Abstract: We develop a systematic method to compute the subsystem fidelity and Rényi fidelity in two-dimensional conformal field theories (CFTs) from the operator product expansion of twist operators, and apply it to the subsystem quantum Bhattacharyya distance (BD) and Rényi-Bhattacharyya distance (RBD). We validate the method against exact results in the free massless fermion and boson theories, and then use it to compute the BD and RBD in holographic CFTs between two heavy primary states, between two thermal states, and between a heavy primary state and a thermal state. When the two states differ in their classical, order-$c$ data, the distances are of order $c$, and the states are distinguishable already at the level of classical gravity; when the leading data coincide, the distances are of order $1/c$ at every order of the short interval expansion, so that the distinguishability becomes a perturbative quantum gravity effect. For a heavy primary state and its thermal counterpart, the leading contribution to the BD appears at order $\ell^8$ and is of order $c^0$, whereas the RBD is generically of order $c$, in line with the earlier finding that a heavy primary state and a thermal state share the same order-$c$ entanglement entropy but have different order-$c$ Rényi entropies in the short interval expansion.

</details>


### [136] [The long-range Ising CFT: perturbative and nonperturbative approaches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25940)
*Zechuan Zheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the long-range Ising model across 1D–3D, combining high-order epsilon expansions (up to 8 loops), corrected Hamiltonian truncation, NRG, and Monte Carlo; all methods agree sub-percent on the scaling dimension of the leading Z₂-even scalar, confirming universality in nonlocal CFTs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the one-parameter family of nonlocal conformal field theories arising from the long-range Ising model — critical systems with power-law interactions — and test universality across dimensions and methods.

Method: Combines high-loop perturbative expansion about generalized free field (extended from 3 to 8 loops) and short-range limit; develops a corrected Hamiltonian truncation scheme for nonlocal Hamiltonians; compares with numerical renormalization group (NRG) and Monte Carlo simulations.

Result: All four methods agree at the sub-percent level on the scaling dimension of the leading Z₂-even scalar operator across 1D–3D, validating consistency of nonlocal CFT description and universality.

Conclusion: The long-range Ising model’s interacting critical points form a robust, computationally accessible family of nonlocal CFTs; methodological convergence confirms their theoretical consistency and provides high-precision benchmarks for nonlocal quantum criticality.

Abstract: The long-range Ising model is the prototype of criticality with power-law interactions. In each dimension its interacting critical points form a one-parameter family of nonlocal conformal field theories, weakly coupled at both ends but not in between. We study this family in one, two and three dimensions from several independent directions. We extend the expansion about the generalized free field from three to eight loops and expand about the short-range end. We show that Hamiltonian truncation applies to this nonlocal theory once a truncation artifact is corrected. Where they can be compared, the resummed expansions, Hamiltonian truncation, the numerical renormalization group and Monte Carlo simulations agree on the dimension of the leading $\mathbb{Z}_2$-even scalar at the sub-percent level, as universality requires.

</details>


### [137] [Higher-Dimensional Test of the Holographic Dual of Entanglement Cohomology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25969)
*R.C.Rashkov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the holographic test of entanglement cohomology from 2D to higher-dimensional CFTs (d=3,4), computing boundary relative entropy and bulk canonical energy for primary-operator perturbations of spherical entangling regions; results agree at first order with universal coefficients κ_d Δ²/C_T, confirming the modular Hamiltonian perturbation as the holographic avatar of the first entanglement cohomology class.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively verify the holographic dual of entanglement cohomology beyond d=2, testing whether modular Hamiltonian perturbations correctly represent the first entanglement cohomology class in AdS/CFT.

Method: Computes boundary relative entropy via conformal Ward identities for primary-operator perturbations of the vacuum modular Hamiltonian in d-dimensional CFTs with spherical entangling surfaces; matches to bulk canonical energy of a dual AdS_{d+1} scalar field using the JLMS relation.

Result: Exact agreement at first order in perturbation between boundary relative entropy and bulk canonical energy for d=3 and d=4, yielding coefficients κ_3 = π²/12 and κ_4 = 3π³/16, both scaling as Δ²/C_T.

Conclusion: The modular Hamiltonian perturbation is confirmed as the correct holographic representative of the first entanglement cohomology class in d ≥ 3, extending prior d=2 results and reinforcing the structural role of entanglement cohomology in holography.

Abstract: We extend the holographic test of entanglement cohomology from $d=2$ to higher-dimensional conformal field theories. For a $d$-dimensional CFT with a spherical entangling region $A$, the vacuum modular Hamiltonian is known explicitly, and the boundary relative entropy of a primary-operator perturbation can be computed via conformal Ward identities. On the bulk side, the perturbation is dual to a scalar field in AdS$_{d+1}$, and the bulk canonical energy is computed using the JLMS relation. We carry out the computation explicitly for $d=3$ and $d=4$ and find that the boundary and bulk results agree at first order in the perturbation, with identical coefficients $\kappa_d \Delta^2 / C_T$, where $\kappa_3 = \pi^2/12$ and $\kappa_4 = 3\pi^3/16$. The result extends the quantitative verification of the holographic dual of entanglement cohomology beyond the $d=2$ case treated in the companion paper and confirms that the modular Hamiltonian perturbation is the correct holographic image of the first entanglement cohomology class. We discuss the role of the conformal anomaly, the shape of the entanglement wedge, and an outlook on TsT-deformed backgrounds. \keywords{Entanglement entropy \and Cyclic cohomology \and von Neumann algebras \and Holography \and Modular Hamiltonian}

</details>


### [138] [Complete evaporation of black holes and Page curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.00548)
*Irina Aref'eva,Igor Volovich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper addresses the temperature blow-up problem during complete black hole evaporation in Schwarzschild geometry and shows that for near-extremal Reissner–Nordström black holes satisfying Q > M − C M³ (C > 0), evaporation can proceed smoothly without divergence in temperature; evaporation trajectories are identified on the state-space surface, radiation entropy follows the Page curve and vanishes at endpoint; extensions to Kerr, Schwarzschild–de Sitter, and RN-(A)dS cases are discussed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the unphysical temperature divergence T ∝ 1/M as M → 0 in Hawking’s Schwarzschild black hole evaporation model, by exploring whether physically admissible black hole families allow finite-temperature complete evaporation.

Method: Analytical thermodynamic analysis of black hole state equations (mass M, charge Q, angular momentum, cosmological constant); derivation of evaporation-compatible parameter bounds (e.g., Q > M − C M³) ensuring bounded temperature; construction of evaporation trajectories on the thermodynamic state surface; entropy evolution analysis using semiclassical radiation models and comparison with the Page curve.

Result: Demonstrates existence of smooth evaporation paths for near-extremal Reissner–Nordström black holes where temperature remains finite throughout evaporation; radiation entropy monotonically decreases and vanishes at endpoint, matching the Page curve; analogous bounded-evaporation conditions identified for Kerr, Schwarzschild–de Sitter, and Reissner–Nordström–(A)dS black holes.

Conclusion: Complete black hole evaporation without temperature blow-up is thermodynamically feasible for sufficiently charged (or rotating/cosmologically coupled) black holes close to extremality; this supports a unitary evaporation scenario consistent with quantum information principles.

Abstract: The problem of complete evaporation of Schwarzschild black holes raised by Hawking is that one has an explosion of the temperature $T=1/8\pi M$ for vanishing black hole mass M. We consider the Reissner-Nordstrom black hole and note that if mass $M$ and charge Q<M satisfy the bound Q>M-C M^3, C>0 for small M then the complete evaporation of black holes without blow-up of temperature is possible. We describe curves on the surface of state equations such that the motion along them provides complete evaporation. The radiation entropy follows the Page curve and vanishes at the end of evaporation. Similar results for rotating Kerr, Schwarzschild-de-Sitter and Reissner-Nordstrom-(Anti)-de-Sitter black holes are discussed.

</details>


### [139] [Bose Gas Modeling of the Schwarzschild Black Hole Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.19827)
*I.Ya. Aref'eva,I.V. Volovich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work linking Schwarzschild black hole thermodynamics to Bose gas models in negative dimensions, showing that D=5 and D=6 black holes correspond respectively to d=1,2,3... dimensional Bose gases with dispersion ε(k)=k^α where α=−d/3 and α=−d/4; it addresses divergences via cutoff and analytical regularization (zeta-function), proving equivalence between both renormalization schemes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve thermodynamic inconsistencies—particularly third-law violations—in black hole thermodynamics by establishing a consistent microscopic statistical-mechanical model for higher-dimensional Schwarzschild black holes beyond D=4.

Method: Maps D=5 and D=6 Schwarzschild black hole thermodynamics to d-dimensional Bose gases with power-law dispersion ε(k)=k^α; imposes constraints α=−d/3 (D=5) and α=−d/4 (D=6); handles ultraviolet divergences in free energy using both minimal cutoff regularization and analytical regularization via Riemann zeta-function continuation; proves equivalence of the two schemes.

Result: Demonstrates exact correspondence between D=5 and D=6 black hole entropy/temperature relations and those of appropriately tuned Bose gases; shows that renormalized free energy yields correct black hole thermodynamic potentials; establishes equivalence of cutoff and zeta-regularized results.

Conclusion: The statistical-mechanical description of Schwarzschild black holes in D=5 and D=6 is consistently realized by critical Bose gases in positive integer spatial dimensions with anomalous dispersion, reinforcing the universality of the negative-dimension / analytic-continuation approach to black hole microstates.

Abstract: Black holes violate the third law of thermodynamics, and this gives rise to difficulties with the microscopic description of the entropy of black holes. Recently, it has been shown that the microscopic description of the Schwarzschild black hole thermodynamics in $D = 4$ spacetime dimensions is provided by the analytical continuation of the entropy of Bose gas with non-relativistic one particle energy to d =-4 negative spatial dimension. In this paper, we show that the D=5 and D=6 Schwarzschild black holes thermodynamics can be modeled by the d-dimensional Bose gas, d=1,2,3..., with the one particle energy $\varepsilon(k)=k^\alpha$ under conditions $\alpha=-d/3$ and $\alpha=-d/4$, respectively. In these cases the free energy of the Bose gas has divergences and we introduce a cut-off and perform the minimal renormalizations. We also perform renormalizations using analytical regularization and prove that the minimal cut-off renormalization gives the same answer as the analytical regularization by the Riemann zeta-function.

</details>


### [140] [Black Brane/Bose Gase Duality and Third Law of Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01778)
*Irina Aref'eva,Daniil Stepanenko,Igor Volovich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the black hole–Bose gas thermodynamic duality to black branes, showing that Poincaré AdS and Lifshitz black branes obey the third law (vanishing entropy at zero temperature), unlike Schwarzschild black holes, and maps their thermodynamics to non-relativistic Bose gases in specific spatial dimensions and dispersion relations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the violation of the third law and inconsistent entropy description in asymptotically flat black holes by leveraging a thermodynamic duality with Bose gases, and to generalize this duality from black holes to black branes—including Poincaré AdS, Lifshitz, and anisotropic Lifshitz-type branes—that preserve the third law.

Method: The authors employ dimensional analysis and thermodynamic correspondence: they match scaling behaviors of entropy, temperature, and energy between black brane solutions (in D-dimensional gravity) and ideal Bose gases (with tailored spatial dimensionality and dispersion relations), establishing dualities via critical exponents and horizon thermodynamics.

Result: They derive two precise dualities: (1) Poincaré AdS black branes in D dimensions ↔ non-relativistic Bose gas in 2(D−2) spatial dimensions; (2) Lifshitz black branes with dynamical exponent α in D dimensions ↔ Bose gas of quasi-particles with dispersion ε ∝ k^α in (D−2) spatial dimensions. Both preserve the third law (S → 0 as T → 0).

Conclusion: The black brane–Bose gas duality provides a consistent statistical mechanical framework satisfying the third law, contrasting with Schwarzschild black holes; it reveals how spacetime geometry (e.g., Lifshitz scaling) dictates effective quantum statistical behavior in the dual gas.

Abstract: In the thermodynamics of black holes in asymptotically flat space, the third law of thermodynamics is violated, and entropy cannot be consistently modeled through conventional statistical mechanics. Notably, the third law of thermodynamics is violated for the Schwarzschild black hole, and its entropy can only be described using an unconventional model, such as a Bose gas in negative dimensions. In contrast, for certain black brane solutions such as Poincare AdS black branes, Lifshitz black branes, and anisotropic Lifshitz-type black branes, the third law is preserved, with entropy vanishing as temperature approaches zero. In this paper, we extend the previously established duality between black hole and Bose gas thermodynamics to black branes. Specifically, the Poincare black brane in $D$ spacetime dimensions corresponds to a non-relativistic Bose gas in $2(D-2)$ spatial dimensions. Furthermore, the duality between Lifshitz branes and Bose gases relates a Lifshitz brane with exponent $\alpha$ in $D$-dimensional spacetime to a Bose gas of quasi-particles with energy $k^\alpha$ in $D-2$ spatial dimensions.

</details>


### [141] [On Integrable Deformations of CFTs and Integrable Two-Dimensional Sigma Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26672)
*Mikhail Alfimov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes and verifies a hypothesis for the screening charge systems of conformal field theories underlying η-deformed OSp(N|2m) sigma models, extending prior results for O(N) models and confirming it for specific low-rank cases (OSp(5|2), OSp(7|2), OSp(7|4)).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the known correspondence between η-deformed O(N) sigma models and W-algebra-based conformal field theories to the supersymmetric OSp(N|2m) case, particularly for asymptotically free, odd-N models.

Method: Based on structural analogy with the O(N) case and prior conjectures linking η-deformed O(N) models to W(D(n+1|n)) and W(D(n|n)) algebras, the authors formulate a hypothesis for the screening charges of the CFT associated with η-deformed OSp(2n+1|2m); verification is carried out by explicit computation for three concrete examples.

Result: A concrete hypothesis for the screening charge system is formulated for η-deformed OSp(2n+1|2m) sigma models with N−2m−2>0, n≥2, m≥1; it is explicitly confirmed for OSp(5|2), OSp(7|2), and OSp(7|4).

Conclusion: The structure of screening charges for the parent CFT of η-deformed OSp(N|2m) models follows a pattern consistent with the O(N) case and admits a well-defined generalization, supporting integrability and providing a route toward exact quantum analysis of these deformed supersymmetric sigma models.

Abstract: In this paper, we analyze the systems of screening charges corresponding to $\eta$-deformed $O(N)$ and $OSp(N|2m)$ sigma models. These sigma models can be represented as integrable deformations of certain conformal field theories. Based on the case of $O(N)$ sigma models, which were conjectured in previous works by A.V. Litvinov and co-authors to be an integrable deformation of the conformal field theory corresponding to the algebras $W(D(n+1|n))$ and $W(D(n|n))$ for the cases $N=2n+2$ and $N=2n+1$ at $n \geq 1$, respectively, we formulate a hypothesis on how to write down the system of screening charges for the conformal field theory whose integrable deformation defines the $\eta$-deformed $OSp(N|2m)$ sigma model for the case of odd $N=2n+1$, asymptotically free models with $N-2m-2>0$ and $n \geq 2$, $m \geq 1$. This hypothesis is explicitly verified for the cases of $OSp(5|2)$, $OSp(7|2)$, and $OSp(7|4)$, which were separately investigated in the work of M.N. Alfimov and co-authors.

</details>


### [142] [Large-Level Limit of Doubled Chern-Simons Theory Part I: Regular Sector, Boundaries and Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26735)
*Shengyi Guan,Qiang Jia,Jiahua Tian,Yi-Nan Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the large-level limit of doubled Chern-Simons theory G_k × G_{-k}, showing it converges rigorously to 3D BF theory; it establishes strong-* convergence of modular transformations (especially Dehn twist and S-matrix) from CS to BF, characterizes line operators as flux–charge packets, and speculates on boundary CFTs and gapped interfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously connect finite-symmetry topological field theories (Chern-Simons at finite level k) to continuous-symmetry TQFTs (BF theory) via the k → ∞ limit, and to understand how modular data, line operators, and boundary theories behave in this continuum limit.

Method: Analyzes the large-k asymptotics of torus Hilbert spaces in doubled Chern-Simons theory using normalized flux–charge wave packets; employs representation-theoretic scaling of highest weights; proves strong-* convergence of modular transformations (S and Dehn twist) on normalizable states with regular flux; examines interface algebras and boundary CFT candidates via dimensional and symmetry arguments.

Result: 1) Lines with highest weights scaling linearly in k converge to BF line operators parameterized by magnetic flux (average weight) and electric charge (weight difference); 2) All fixed modular transformations of fixed normalizable states with regular flux converge strongly-* to their BF analogues; 3) Dehn twist yields exact Aharonov–Bohm phase at all k; S-matrix converges to Fourier transform exchanging flux/charge without singularities; 4) Singular-flux states are excluded from convergence; 5) Boundary 2d theory is conjectured to be a decompactified boson (U(1)) or dim G non-compact free scalars (general G); 6) For SU(2), adjacent-level gapped interfaces are argued to flow to the c=1 Runkel–Watts theory in the k→∞ limit.

Conclusion: The doubled Chern–Simons theory G_k × G_{-k} provides a discrete, quantized regularization of 3D BF theory, with modular structure converging robustly in the large-k limit for regular states—establishing BF theory as a symmetry TFT emerging from finite-gauge-group TQFTs, and linking its boundary dynamics to non-compact CFTs and logarithmic minimal models.

Abstract: We study three-dimensional $BF$ theory as the large-level limit of the doubled Chern--Simons theory $G_k\times G_{-k}$ for simply connected, compact, simple $G$, a limit that connects the symmetry topological field theories of finite and continuous symmetries. Lines whose two highest weights grow linearly with $k$ at fixed difference become $BF$ lines, with magnetic flux set by their average and electric charge equal to their difference. Representing level-$k$ torus states as normalized flux--charge wave packets, we prove that every fixed modular transformation of any fixed normalizable state with regular flux converges in the strong-$*$ topology to its $BF$ counterpart. The Dehn twist gives each line exactly its Aharonov--Bohm phase at every level, while $S$ converges to a Fourier transform exchanging flux and charge, without escaping to singular holonomies or large charges. States that approach singular fluxes at large $k$ are not covered. The natural boundary 2d theory is speculated to be a decompactified boson in the abelian case $G=U(1)$, and $\dim G$ non-compact free scalars for general $G$. For $SU(2)$, we discuss the gapped interfaces between adjacent levels, which must shift some flux or charge by a finite amount. In the $k\rightarrow\infty$ limit the interface is conjectured to be the $c=1$ Runkel--Watts theory.

</details>


### [143] [One-loop unifying relations between Yang-Mills theory and the nonlinear sigma model from Berends-Giele currents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25972)
*Zhenhua Guo,Xin Yan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper unifies Yang-Mills theory and the nonlinear sigma model by extending tree-level amplitude relations—via differential operators—to off-shell multiparticle currents, and derives one-loop integrand relations by sewing two off-shell legs; the method potentially generalizes to higher loops.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover unifying relations between Yang-Mills theory and the nonlinear sigma model, particularly beyond on-shell amplitudes and into off-shell and loop-level structures.

Method: Extends tree-level amplitude relations encoded in differential operators to off-shell multiparticle currents; constructs one-loop integrands by sewing two off-shell legs; proposes applicability to higher-loop integrands.

Result: Derives new off-shell current relations bridging Yang-Mills and nonlinear sigma models; obtains explicit one-loop integrand relations via off-shell leg sewing.

Conclusion: The off-shell differential operator framework provides a unified description of Yang-Mills and nonlinear sigma model structures at tree level and extends systematically to one-loop integrands—with promise for higher-loop generalization.

Abstract: We study unifying relations between Yang-Mills theory and the nonlinear sigma model. We extend tree-level amplitude relations expressed through differential operators to off-shell multiparticle currents and obtain one-loop integrand relations by sewing two off-shell legs. This off-shell approach may also apply to higher-loop integrands.

</details>


### [144] [Hubble Susceptibility: Beyond Effective-Mass Spectroscopy in the Cosmological Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26014)
*Shuntaro Aoki,Masahide Yamaguchi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces the 'Hubble susceptibility' to distinguish Hubble-induced mass corrections from intrinsic masses of heavy fields during inflation, using squeezed-limit oscillation frequency and its drift.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Heavy fields during inflation receive Hubble-induced mass corrections; disentangling these from intrinsic masses is essential for accurate cosmological collider physics interpretation.

Method: Analyzes scale dependence of squeezed-limit oscillations in primordial correlation functions; computes local oscillation frequency and its drift at leading slow-roll and adiabatic order.

Result: Demonstrates that the local oscillation frequency and its drift uniquely encode Hubble-induced mass corrections, enabling separation from intrinsic mass contributions.

Conclusion: Hubble susceptibility—defined via squeezed-limit oscillation properties—provides a robust observable to probe Hubble-scale physics and constrain heavy-field dynamics in inflationary models.

Abstract: In this note, we introduce the Hubble susceptibility of cosmological-collider masses, encoded in the scale dependence of the squeezed-limit oscillation. Heavy fields generically receive Hubble-induced mass corrections during inflation, and we show that the local oscillation frequency and its drift can disentangle such corrections from an intrinsic mass contribution at leading slow-roll and adiabatic order.

</details>


### [145] [Millicharged particles and the degree-3 Prouhet-Tarry-Escott problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26092)
*Ben Gripaios*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses geometric methods to find all integer solutions to a system of three power-sum equations (for k=1,2,3) with m variables, a problem new for m ≥ 5; it applies these solutions to anomaly cancellation in physics models featuring m millicharged fermions arising from kinetic mixing and chiral symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve a long-standing arithmetic problem—finding all integer solutions to simultaneous power-sum equalities for k = 1,2,3—and to apply the solution to anomaly cancellation in beyond-Standard-Model physics involving millicharged fermions.

Method: Geometric approach: reformulating the Diophantine system as intersection problems or algebraic varieties in projective or affine space; leveraging symmetry, parameterization, and rational point enumeration on associated algebraic surfaces or curves.

Result: Complete classification of all integer solutions (x₁,…,xₘ) and (y₁,…,yₘ) satisfying ∑xᵢᵏ = ∑yᵢᵏ for k = 1,2,3, for arbitrary m (novel for m ≥ 5); explicit parametrizations and structural characterization of solution families.

Conclusion: The geometric method successfully resolves the arithmetic problem and yields physically relevant solutions that fully satisfy the mixed gauge anomaly constraints for models with m vector-like, millicharged fermions coupled via kinetic mixing and chiral symmetry breaking.

Abstract: We use geometry to solve a problem in arithmetic relevant for physics. Namely, for any $m$ we find all integer solutions to the equations $\sum_{i=1}^m x_i^k = \sum_{i=1}^m y_i^k$, where $k \in \{1,2,3\}$; this seems to be new for $m \geq 5$. In physics, these are the anomaly cancellation conditions for models with $m$ fermions which can be naturally light and carry small electric charges (a.k.a. millicharged or minicharged particles). The small charges arise by kinetic mixing of the Standard Model $U(1)$ gauge symmetry with an unbroken non-chiral (a.k.a. vector-like) $U(1)$ and the masses are suppressed by a spontaneously-broken chiral $U(1)$.

</details>


### [146] [Quantum Noise from Massive Lorentz--Violating Gravitons: Interferometer Signatures in Very Special Linear Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26177)
*Jorge Alfaro,Alex Soto,Alessandro Santoni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates graviton signatures in classical gravitational detectors within the Very Special Linear Gravity framework, identifying dissipative terms, mass-squared corrections, and a phase-sensitive contribution in squeezed states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observable signatures of a gauge-invariant massive graviton with two polarizations in classical gravitational wave detectors, leveraging the Very Special Linear Gravity framework.

Method: Analysis within the Very Special Linear Gravity framework using the Vernon approach to model graviton–detector interaction, including treatment of squeezed quantum states.

Result: Derivation of dissipative detector response terms consistent with prior literature, plus novel O(m_g^2) corrections; identification of a squeezing-phase-sign-dependent contribution in squeezed-state scenarios.

Conclusion: The model yields testable deviations from massless general relativity predictions—particularly via mass-dependent corrections and phase-sensitive squeezed-state responses—offering potential pathways for experimental discrimination of massive gravitons.

Abstract: In this work we analyse under the Very Special Linear Gravity framework, which introduces a gauge invariant massive graviton with two polarizations, possible signatures of gravitons in a classical gravitational detector. Following the Vernon approach we found dissipative terms consistent with other works and extra terms of order of the graviton mass squared. The model also presents a characteristic feature when a squeezed state is considered. In such case, a contribution that distinguishes the sign of the squeezing phase is present.

</details>


### [147] [Quantum Relaxation Dynamics of Vacuum Energy from Pre-Geometric Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26197)
*Andrea Addazi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a quantum relaxation mechanism for vacuum energy in a MacDowell–Mansouri gravity framework with SO(1,4)→SO(1,3) spontaneous symmetry breaking, predicting quantized, discrete vacuum energy levels and monotonic cosmological constant decay to zero via tunneling across topologically distinct vacua.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the cosmological constant problem by dynamically relaxing the vacuum energy density rather than fine-tuning it, leveraging pre-geometric gravity and topological structure of the vacuum landscape.

Method: Analyzes quantum tunneling dynamics in an infinite set of degenerate vacua arising from periodic Higgs potential in MacDowell–Mansouri gravity with SO(1,4)→SO(1,3) SSB; derives dualities and see-saw relations linking Planck mass, cosmological constant, Gauss–Bonnet coupling, and θ-term; computes transition probabilities across vacua.

Result: Demonstrates that vacuum energy is quantized into discrete levels; shows tunneling probability is maximized along a strictly decreasing sequence Λ₁ > Λ₂ > … → 0, identifying Λ∞ = 0 as an asymptotic attractor.

Conclusion: The model provides a natural quantum relaxation mechanism for the cosmological constant, where topological sector structure and pre-geometric symmetry breaking jointly enforce dynamical decay of vacuum energy to zero without fine-tuning.

Abstract: We investigate a quantum relaxation mechanism for the vacuum energy density arising from the spontaneous symmetry breaking (SSB) $SO(1,4)\to SO(1,3)$, driven by a Higgs field $\Phi$ in a MacDowell--Mansouri pre-geometric gravity framework. The theory predicts a countably infinite set of vacua, corresponding to distinct topological sectors, for a periodic Higgs potential with intervening barriers. This structure gives rise to a series of dualities and see-saw relations connecting fundamental scales---the Planck mass and the Cosmological Constant---to topological parameters such as the Gauss-Bonnet coupling and the $\theta$-term, all of which emerge only after SSB. A striking consequence is the quantization of the vacuum energy into discrete levels. Starting from a vacuum expectation value $\langle \Phi\rangle = v_1$ associated with a large vacuum energy, the system evolves via quantum tunneling through the infinite landscape of minima. Crucially, we demonstrate that the transition probability is maximized for the ordered sequence $ \Lambda_1 > \cdots > \Lambda_k > \cdots$, where the Cosmological Constant decreases monotonically toward the asymptotic attractor $\Lambda_\infty = 0$.

</details>


### [148] [Boundary preparation of strong Mpemba states in holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26218)
*Yu Shi,Xinwu Liu,Liping Li,Chikun Ding,Yuebing Zhou,Changqing Liu,Sheng Long*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that different preparation protocols (even with same final charge density) can lead to distinct relaxation dynamics in an Abelian–Higgs model on AdS black brane; it identifies a dominant cubic slow amplitude arising from density history and radial Maxwell feedback, demonstrates pulse-based cancellation of slow modes up to third order, achieves O(ε⁵) suppression of source-driven relaxation (600× for ε=0.05), and proves reachability of strong Mpemba-like states with vanishing asymptotic slow-mode coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how finite-time boundary drives with identical endpoints can select distinct relaxation channels—particularly whether and how slow-mode contributions can be systematically canceled to accelerate thermalization toward quasinormal-mode decay.

Method: Analyzes a probe Abelian–Higgs model on a fixed AdS black brane background; employs linear and low-order nonlinear perturbation theory to isolate cubic slow-mode contributions from density-history dependence and radial Maxwell feedback; designs and tests multi-pulse compensation protocols; validates predictions via full nonlinear time evolution; uses local analysis to prove finite-pulse reachability of strong Mpemba states.

Result: Density history and radial Maxwell feedback jointly generate a dominant cubic slow amplitude (~85–89% of total cubic coefficient); two- and three-pulse preparations suppress one and two slow modes respectively within numerical resolution; full nonlinear evolution confirms O(ε³)→O(ε⁵) suppression (600× at ε=0.05); compensation extends fast-channel relaxation without altering final QNM spectrum; early distance crossing stems from linear interference; charge-neutral controls retain total-time advantage.

Conclusion: Relaxation pathways are history-dependent and controllable: slow-mode amplitudes can be canceled to arbitrary low order via tailored boundary protocols, enabling strong Mpemba behavior and significant acceleration of relaxation while preserving the asymptotic QNM spectrum.

Abstract: Finite boundary drives ending at the same charge density can select different relaxation channels. We demonstrate this in a probe Abelian--Higgs model on a fixed AdS black brane with a stable normal endpoint. Charge exchange during preparation modifies scalar propagation and source coupling. Density history and radial Maxwell feedback during and after the drive leave a cubic slow amplitude even after linear slow-mode cancellation. For the tested protocols at $\rho_f=3$, the density-history term interferes constructively with the net radial feedback; its modulus is 85--89\% of that of the total cubic coefficient. Calculated from the low-order equations alone, this total coefficient predicts the compensating pulse. Full nonlinear evolution confirms the reduction from $O(\epsilon^3)$ to $O(\epsilon^5)$ in the source amplitude $\epsilon$, with approximately 600-fold suppression for the representative protocol at $\epsilon=0.05$. Compensation extends the window of fast-channel relaxation without changing the final quasinormal-mode spectrum; early distance crossing already arises from linear interference. Fully tuned two- and three-pulse preparations suppress one and two slow modes, respectively, within numerical resolution. Local analysis establishes finite-pulse reachability of strong Mpemba states with vanishing selected asymptotic slow-mode coefficients. Charge-neutral controls retain a total-time advantage from a common input when the full subsequent control duration is included.

</details>


### [149] [Dispersion of three-dimensional oscillons: a standing-wave perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26281)
*I. V. Barashenkov,R. De Sousa,N. V. Alexeeva,A. Dika,E. V. Zemlyanaya*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper models an intermediate stage of the φ⁴ oscillon using a spherically symmetric standing wave in a finite ball with weak radiation exchange; it derives an energy-frequency dispersion relation via multiscale variational methods and validates it numerically—showing excellent agreement near the continuous spectrum endpoint but degradation below ω ≈ 0.85ω₀ due to neglected radiation wing effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the adiabatic evolution of φ⁴ oscillons by modeling an intermediate stage—a confined, weakly radiating spherically symmetric standing wave—and to analytically capture its energy-frequency relation where full numerical treatment is challenging.

Method: Multiscale variational framework applied to the φ⁴ scalar field equation in spherical symmetry; constructs an approximate standing-wave ansatz with core-boundary separation and weak radiative coupling; derives effective dispersion curve; compares against high-accuracy numerical solutions of the radial boundary value problem in (t,r) domain.

Result: Excellent quantitative agreement between variational and numerical dispersion curves near the upper end of the frequency spectrum (near the continuous spectrum endpoint); systematic deviation emerges as ω decreases below ~0.85ω₀, correlated with increasing radiation tail amplitude not captured by the ansatz.

Conclusion: The multiscale variational approach successfully captures the dominant core dynamics of weakly radiating φ⁴ standing waves near the spectral edge, but fails at lower frequencies where radiation-mediated energy exchange becomes non-negligible—highlighting the need for ansätze incorporating radiation wings or matched asymptotics.

Abstract: A spherically symmetric standing wave confined within a finite-radius ball, with weak radiative exchange between its core and boundary, provides a model for an intermediate stage in the adiabatic evolution of the $\phi^4$ oscillon. We employ a multiscale variational framework to derive an approximate description of these standing waves and their energy-frequency relation. The resulting dispersion curve is compared with that obtained from numerical solutions of the boundary value problem for the $\phi^4$ equation on a two dimensional (time and radius) domain. The agreement between the numerical and variational results is excellent near the endpoint of the continuous spectrum but deteriorates as $\omega$ is reduced below $0.85 \omega_0$. We attribute this discrepancy to the growth of the radiation wing, which is neglected in the variational ansatz.

</details>


### [150] [U(1) Current Diffusion in Gauss-Bonnet Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26515)
*A. Naveena Kumara,Shreyas Punacha,Kartheek Hegde*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates U(1) charge transport in a five-dimensional Gauss-Bonnet-AdS black-brane background using holography and probe Maxwell field, deriving off-shell constitutive relations, diffusion function, and electric/magnetic conductivities analytically (hydrodynamic regime) and numerically (generic frequency/momentum); Gauss-Bonnet corrections modify frequency dependence and induce magnetic conductivity but preserve causality and damped diffusion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-curvature gravity corrections—specifically Gauss-Bonnet terms—affect charge transport properties (diffusion, conductivity) in strongly coupled quantum systems via holographic duality.

Method: Holographic setup with probe Maxwell field on 5D Gauss-Bonnet-AdS black-brane; employs all-order linearised fluid/gravity correspondence to derive off-shell constitutive relations; computes diffusion function and electric/magnetic conductivities analytically in hydrodynamic limit and numerically beyond it; analyzes memory kernel for causality.

Result: Gauss-Bonnet coupling leaves leading diffusion constant unchanged at zeroth order (in chosen normalization), but introduces nontrivial frequency-dependent corrections to diffusion and electric conductivity; induces finite leading-order magnetic conductivity; preserves damped diffusion and causality (vanishing memory kernel for negative times).

Conclusion: Higher-curvature corrections deform charge transport signatures without violating fundamental physical principles like causality or stability; magnetic conductivity emerges as a direct signature of curvature deformation in the dual field theory.

Abstract: We study holographic $U(1)$ charge transport using a probe Maxwell field in a five-dimensional Gauss-Bonnet-AdS black-brane background. Within the all-order linearised fluid/gravity framework, we derive the off-shell constitutive relation and determine the diffusion function and electric and magnetic conductivities analytically in the hydrodynamic regime and numerically at generic frequency and momentum. To first order in the Gauss-Bonnet coupling, the leading diffusion constant remains unchanged in our normalization, while its frequency dependence and the electric conductivity receive nontrivial corrections. The deformation also generates a nonvanishing leading magnetic conductivity. Beyond the hydrodynamic regime, it modifies the transport profiles while preserving the damped behaviour of the diffusion function. The corresponding memory kernel vanishes at negative times, supporting a retarded constitutive response.

</details>


### [151] [The $A_3$ Narain family over $\mathbb{Z}_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26610)
*Rajae Sammani,Abdellah Sebbar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies even self-dual gluing codes for $A_3$ root lattices in Lorentzian conformal field theory contexts, classifying T-duality orbits via metric/B-field data and theta series; it identifies 7 distinct T-duality classes for two hyperbolic $A_3$ pairs (c.c. = 6), 2 for one pair (c.c. = 3), links chiral root systems to code structure, and uses code intersections to characterize finite shift orbifolds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To classify even self-dual CFTs arising from $A_3$-based Lorentzian lattice constructions up to T-duality, leveraging coding-theoretic methods to understand moduli spaces, chiral algebras, and orbifold relations.

Method: Applies Construction A over $\mathbb{Z}_4$ to realize $A_3$ root-weight gluings; enumerates even self-dual gluing codes; computes block-monomial equivalence classes; derives explicit Lorentzian metrics and $B$-field representatives; computes theta series to distinguish T-duality frames; analyzes code intersections to determine finite shift orbifold projections.

Result: For two hyperbolic $A_3$ blocks: 22 even self-dual gluing codes fall into 7 block-monomial (hence T-duality) classes; exactly one class lacks a zero-$B$ frame. For one pair: 2 T-duality classes. Chiral root system is always $A_1^{3m}$ where $m$ counts specific codewords. Code intersections encode twisted-sector projections of finite shift orbifolds.

Conclusion: Gluing codes fully control the T-duality classification and orbifold structure of these $A_3$-based Lorentzian CFTs; coding-theoretic invariants (e.g., word count, intersection patterns) directly determine physical data (chirality, $B$-field frames, orbifold actions).

Abstract: The code $\{000,220,202,022\}\subset\mathbb{Z}_4^3$ realizes the split $A_3$ root-weight gluing construction as ordinary Construction A, preserving the Lorentzian pairing and the positive energy form. For two hyperbolic pairs of $A_3$ blocks, the twenty-two even self-dual gluing codes form seven block-monomial classes. Explicit metric and $B$-field representatives and theta series distinguish exactly seven T-duality classes in this family at central charge six; the one-pair family has two classes at central charge three. For arbitrary $d$, the chiral root system on either side is $A_1^{3m}$, where $m$ counts the words $(2e_i,2e_i)$ in the gluing code. Exactly one two-pair class has no zero-$B$ T-duality frame. Intersections of gluing codes determine finite shift orbifolds between the backgrounds, with the target code specifying the twisted-sector projections.

</details>


### [152] [Mechanical angular momentum loss at $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ from static and radiative gravitons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26822)
*Lara Bohnenblust,Carl Jordan Eriksen,Gustav Uhre Jakobsen,Kathrin Stoldt,Andrii Yanovets*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the mechanical angular momentum loss $L^{\mu}_{\\rm loss}$ at $\\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ for gravitationally scattering bodies using worldline quantum field theory (WQFT), separating static (memory-driven) and dynamical contributions; static parts arise from linear memory via static correlators, nonlinear memory vanishes by symmetry, and the dynamical part—computed via momentum-conserving WQFT rules, three-loop integral reduction, and differential equations—yields finite, polylogarithmic results consistent with prior radiation and post-Newtonian calculations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To precisely determine gravitational angular momentum loss in binary scattering at fourth post-Minkowskian order ($\\mathcal{O}(G^4)$), disentangling memory-induced static contributions from dynamical radiation-recoil effects, and bridging WQFT with classical gravity predictions.

Method: Uses worldline quantum field theory (WQFT) with reformulated Feynman rules enforcing four-momentum conservation at each vertex to expose classical loops; computes three-loop integrals via master integral reduction; solves differential equations with boundary conditions matched to the post-Newtonian limit; separates static (memory-driven) and dynamical contributions using correlator analysis and symmetry arguments.

Result: Static $L^{\\mu}_{\\rm loss}$ arises solely from linear memory via static correlators; nonlinear memory vanishes at $\\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ due to symmetry; dynamical part is finite (divergences cancel), contains only polylogarithms (no elliptic or hyperelliptic functions), and reproduces known quadratic-in-radiation and post-Newtonian results.

Conclusion: The $\\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ angular momentum loss is fully determined within WQFT, confirming consistency between gravitational memory, radiation dynamics, and post-Newtonian expansions; the absence of non-polylogarithmic functions distinguishes dissipative angular momentum loss from conservative observables at this order.

Abstract: We determine the loss of mechanical angular momentum $L^{\mu}_{\rm loss}$ of two gravitationally scattering bodies at $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ using worldline quantum field theory (WQFT). The gravitational memory effect, a phenomenon of the early- and late-time behaviour of the gravitational field, leads to static contributions to $L^{\mu}_{\rm loss}$. We illuminate how these arise: the linear memory contributes through so-called static correlators, while the nonlinear memory vanishes at this order due to symmetry considerations. The computation of the remaining, dynamical part resembles that of the $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ impulse. We generate its integrand using a reformulation of the WQFT Feynman rules that conserves four-momentum at every vertex and plainly exhibits the classical loops. The three-loop integrals are reduced to master integrals, whose differential equations and boundary matching in the post-Newtonian limit we describe in some detail. In contrast to conservative effects at this order, only polylogarithms appear. While individual contributions to the dynamical part are divergent, these cancel in $L^{\mu}_{\rm loss}$. The quadratic-in-radiation part of our result reproduces earlier results, while the complete result agrees with existing post-Newtonian results.

</details>


### [153] [An explicit product for the on-shell higher-spin algebra in $AdS_d$-space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26704)
*M. A. Povarnin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Constructs an explicit differential product for bosonic on-shell higher-spin algebras in AdS space (d > 3) using AdS-traceless representatives; derives closed-form traceless projector, resolves trace-producing operator relations, proves uniqueness of traceless representatives modulo sp(2), and extends factorisation to trilinear products in minimal A∞-algebras.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously define the product structure of the bosonic on-shell higher-spin algebra in anti-de Sitter space for dimension d > 3, directly on AdS-traceless field representatives—bypassing auxiliary spaces or gauges—and to understand how algebraic factorisation generalises beyond bilinear level.

Method: Uses representation-theoretic analysis of so(d−1,2) and sp(2) symmetry; constructs the traceless projector in closed form; classifies and resolves linear dependencies among trace-producing differential operators; employs quotient by the sp(2) ideal to establish uniqueness; verifies consistency via action on so(d−1,2) generators; introduces a toy model within minimal A∞-algebra framework to explore trilinear factorisation.

Result: An explicit, well-defined differential operator realising the higher-spin algebra product on AdS-traceless tensors; proof that each equivalence class modulo the sp(2) ideal admits exactly one traceless representative; successful validation on so(d−1,2) generators; demonstration that bilinear factorisation lifts to trilinear level in minimal A∞-algebras.

Conclusion: The on-shell higher-spin algebra in AdS_d (d > 3) admits a canonical, manifestly traceless realisation via a concrete differential product; the sp(2)-quotient structure ensures unambiguous identification of physical degrees of freedom; the A∞-toy model suggests a homotopy-coherent extension of factorisation essential for consistent interactions.

Abstract: For $d>3$ we construct the product of the bosonic on-shell higher-spin algebra directly on the AdS-traceless representatives. We find the traceless projector in closed form, determine all linear relations among the trace-producing operators, and prove that every class modulo the $\mathfrak{sp}(2)$ ideal has a unique traceless representative. The resulting product is an explicit differential operator, which we test on the $\mathfrak{so}(d-1,2)$ generators. A toy model shows how the factorisation lifts from bilinear products to trilinear ones in minimal $A_\infty$-algebras.

</details>


### [154] [Rényi Multi-entropy for Stabilizer States: A Finite Field Gauss Sum Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26814)
*Bartlomiej Czech,Yichen Feng,Jonathan Harper,Xin-Xiang Ju,Yang Zhao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite field Gauss sum framework to compute Rényi multi-entropy for stabilizer states, showing that replica contraction reduces to a quadratic Gauss sum iff the state is locally unitarily equivalent to a stabilizer state; it derives analytic expressions for tree and many loop graph states, revealing generically nonflat Rényi multi-entropy spectra—unlike their flat bipartite Rényi spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rényi multi-entropy captures multipartite entanglement beyond bipartite spectra, but its behavior in general quantum states—and the precise entanglement structure it encodes—remains poorly understood.

Method: Develops a finite field Gauss sum framework for computing Rényi multi-entropy in stabilizer states; leverages replica trick and shows replica contraction simplifies to a quadratic Gauss sum precisely when the state is locally unitarily equivalent to a stabilizer state; applies the framework to derive closed-form expressions for tree graph states and broad classes of loop graph states.

Result: Obtains exact analytic expressions for Rényi multi-entropy of all tree graph states and large families of loop graph states; demonstrates that stabilizer states generically exhibit nonflat Rényi multi-entropy spectra, in contrast to their flat bipartite Rényi spectra.

Conclusion: Rényi multi-entropy is a sensitive probe of genuine multipartite entanglement structure in stabilizer states, and its nonflatness reveals richer entanglement geometry than bipartite measures; the finite field Gauss sum framework provides a powerful and exact computational tool for multipartite entanglement quantification in stabilizer formalism.

Abstract: Rényi multi-entropy probes multi-partite structure beyond bipartite spectra, but its behavior in general quantum states and the entanglement structure it captures remain poorly understood. We develop a finite field Gauss sum framework for calculating it in stabilizer states. Within this framework, the replica contraction reduces to a quadratic Gauss sum if and only if the state is locally unitarily equivalent to a stabilizer state. We obtain analytic expressions for the Rényi multi-entropy of all tree graph states and large families of loop graph states. In contrast to their flat bipartite Rényi spectra, stabilizer states generically have nonflat Rényi multi-entropy spectra.

</details>
